## Phase 44 — Phase 43's punch list worked through step by step: Workday `/Search`-board rediscovery, Legitimacy two-strike rule + recheck cron, Adzuna retry-once, RemoteOK shipped, Careerjet researched and rejected

**Done on the SAME branch as Phase 42/43** (`feature/supabase-migration`), same reasoning as before — this work is backend-agnostic and doesn't need its own revert boundary.

**SerpApi re-checked live, still exhausted**: all 3 keys confirmed `0/250` again this session (`serpapi.com/account.json`, not just the dashboard) — the Phase 43 temporary Adzuna+JobsPipe+JSearch exhaustion-fallback reorder in `lib/jobScraper.ts` stays in place, not reverted, per its own "revert once quota resets" instruction.

### 1 — Workday `/Search`-board 404, fixed with self-healing rediscovery
`crawlKnownWorkdayCompanies` (`lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts`) now detects a generic `board === "search"` (case-insensitive) and re-resolves the real board via `discoverAtsForRegistry(company_domain)` — the exact same careers-page-HTML-scraping technique already used for brand-new companies, just applied retroactively using the `company_domain` already stored on the row from the original seed. A successful rediscovery is persisted back onto `ats_registry.config`, so the fix is paid for once per company, not on every crawl pass. Failures are capped at 5 attempts via the existing `failed_attempts` column (a genuinely dead/moved domain shouldn't retry forever) — and every exit path, including the give-up path, now unconditionally bumps `last_crawled_at` via a new `touchCrawled()` helper, since the function is ordered oldest-crawled-first and a row that never got touched would otherwise monopolize every future batch. No live re-crawl was triggered this session (that's the cron's own job on its normal schedule) — the fix itself was `tsc`/`eslint`-verified only; watch the next few cron runs' logs to confirm real `/Search` rows actually resolve.

### 2 — Legitimacy hard-hide now requires 2 consecutive D/F grades, plus an ongoing recheck cron
New migration `20260903120000_add-legitimacy-two-strike-recheck.sql` (applied live to Supabase via the same scratchpad `pg` client Phase 41/42 used) adds `jobs.legitimacy_fail_count`/`legitimacy_checked_at`. State machine: `fail_count=0` normal; `fail_count=1, is_hidden=false` "on probation" (one D/F grade recorded, still shown); `fail_count=2, is_hidden=true` confirmed, hard-hidden. `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s per-job evaluation persist step now only ever writes `fail_count` 0 or 1 on a job's first-ever evaluation — it never hides on a single grade any more. A new `legitimacyRecheckAsync` cron (every 30 min, batch of 25) re-grades probation jobs via a new, deliberately profile-independent `evaluateLegitimacyOnly` (`lib/evaluator.ts` — legitimacy is a fact about the posting, not about any one candidate's fit, so this needs no Profile/constraints and can batch jobs from many users together): a second D/F confirms and hides; anything else clears the flag back to 0. The migration also backfilled every job already hidden under the OLD single-strike rule to `fail_count=1`, so they get a real, ongoing second look too, not just the one-time 2026-09-01 manual reset. **Known, accepted imprecision, same posture as that 2026-09-01 migration's own heuristic**: the recheck sets `is_hidden` unconditionally from its own verdict for any job it selects, which could in principle overwrite a user's own manual hide on a job that also happened to be on probation — there's still no dedicated hidden-reason column to disambiguate that. Not yet live-observed (the cron hasn't had a real production job cycle through both grades yet) — worth spot-checking after it's run a few times.

### 3 — Adzuna retries once instead of swallowing on first failure
A transient Adzuna error (network blip, a passing 5xx — Adzuna has no shared-quota-exhaustion concept the way SerpApi does) used to drop straight to an empty merge result for that entire search. `searchAdzunaWithRetry` (`lib/jobScraper.ts`) now retries once after a 1.5s pause before giving up, same "retry once, then fall back" shape `lib/evaluator.ts`'s own JSON-parse retry already uses.

### 4 — RemoteOK shipped as a new free source; Careerjet researched and rejected
**RemoteOK** (`remoteOkProvider`, `lib/jobScraper.ts`) — confirmed live, real, keyless (`remoteok.com/api`, HTTP 200, genuine postings). Wired into both the primary SerpApi merge (`fetchAndMergeFreeSources`, renamed from `fetchAndMergeAdzuna`) and the SerpApi-exhaustion fallback, in both cases deliberately excluded from `filterByCity` — every RemoteOK result is inherently remote by construction, so city-filtering it would wrongly drop genuine remote postings. **A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not assumed correct from the API docs**: RemoteOK's own feed has a genuine data-quality issue — some non-tech postings (e.g. "Kitchen Technician" at Four Seasons) carry nonsensical auto-generated tags including "engineer"/"dev". The first implementation matched search terms against `position + tags` combined, and a live test searching "Engineer" against the real feed surfaced that exact "Kitchen Technician" as a false match. Fixed to match against `job.position` only (tags dropped entirely from relevance matching) — re-verified live afterward: "Engineer" now correctly returns only genuine matches ("AI Engineer Data APIs" at Benzinga, "Principal Engineer").

**Careerjet researched, deliberately NOT shipped** — a real live-verification failure, not an oversight, following this project's own established precedent (Arbeitnow/SmartRecruiters/Workable). The legacy `public.api.careerjet.net/search` endpoint (documented elsewhere as "keyless") returns a genuine, reproducible `401 "The legacy Job Search API is only accessible for authenticated legacy users"` when called with this app's own real domain as the `Referer` header — it only returned real results in testing when the `Referer` was set to `https://example.com`, which appears to sit on some grandfathered legacy allowlist. Shipping code that only works by presenting a fake referrer identity would be dishonest and could break the moment that allowlist is tightened further. Careerjet's own current partner docs (fetched live via WebFetch) confirm the real, current API requires a registered Publisher account and an API key used as an HTTP Basic Auth username — genuine account creation this app can't do on the user's behalf, the exact same real blocker PayPal Payouts already has. If revisited, needs a real Careerjet Publisher API key from the user first, then the v4 API's actual request shape researched properly (not guessed).

**Verification**: `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout. The exhaustion-fallback merge and RemoteOK's corrected title-matching were both confirmed against real live data via a one-off `tsx`-run script (Doppler-injected real env vars, not mocked) calling the actual `searchJobs()` function end to end — not just the standalone provider fetches.

### 5 — A real, significant relevance bug found the SAME session, in direct response to user pushback on "the number staying the same"
The first report of this session's testing only exercised `searchJobs()` (the raw aggregator layer) and reported 103 raw results for "Financial Advisor"/Toronto — technically accurate but incomplete, since it skipped the two layers `scraper.actions.ts` actually runs afterward (reactive direct-ATS enrichment, the proactive-crawl cache read). Re-testing the FULL pipeline (via a one-off `tsx` script replicating `scrapeAndEvaluateJobs`'s exact `searchJobs → enrichWithDirectAtsJobs → queryProactiveCrawlCache → dedupe` sequence) surfaced a real, previously-undiscovered bug:

**`fetchRegisteredAtsJobs` (`lib/atsProviders.ts`) only filters by title for Workday (`searchText`) and iCIMS (its own word-overlap check) — it silently drops the `searchTitle` argument for the other 4 platforms (Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby/SmartRecruiters) and returns the company's ENTIRE board.** `scraper.actions.ts`'s own existing comment on `enrichWithDirectAtsJobs` already asserted "pulls a company's board FILTERED BY TITLE ONLY" — that assumption was simply wrong for 4 of the 6 supported platforms, and had been for a while (not something this session introduced). Confirmed live: enriching the real "Financial Advisor"/Toronto search with iCapital (a real Greenhouse-hosted company in that same result set) pulled 212 of its openings — ZERO finance-relevant ("Actuarial Software Engineer," "Agentic AI Engineer," etc.); Blue Moon Metals (a mining company, appearing twice due to a duplicate registry row) dumped 33 irrelevant mining roles, twice. Of 298 total "enriched" jobs, only 3 were genuinely relevant.

**Real, measured consequence, not just noise**: `scrapeAndEvaluateJobs`'s `MAX_EVALUATED_JOBS=80` trim ranks candidates by apply-link TRUST TIER only (direct ATS links rank above aggregator links) — there is no relevance check at that trimming stage. That means a single enriched company's irrelevant full-board dump could crowd Adzuna's actually-relevant "Financial Advisor" postings entirely out of the 80-slot cap before evaluation ever saw them. This is a real, plausible root cause for "all this ATS-registry work and the visible number isn't moving" — the underlying reach was real and growing, but a large fraction of what it added was noise actively competing with, and potentially displacing, the genuinely relevant results.

**Fixed** in `lib/atsRegistry.ts`'s `fetchJobsForCompany` — the one caller that's genuinely a bulk relevance filter (not a specific-posting lookup). Deliberately NOT fixed inside the shared `fetchRegisteredAtsJobs` itself: `lib/reresolveApplyLink.ts`'s per-job apply-link rescue path also calls into this same machinery (via `fetchDiscoveredAtsJobs`) and needs the FULL unfiltered board — it does its own stricter, order-sensitive exact-substring `titlesMatch` afterward, and a looser word-overlap pre-filter at the shared layer could wrongly drop a legitimate match when the searched job's title is longer/more specific than the real live posting's title (e.g. searching "Senior Financial Advisor" against a board's real "Financial Advisor" posting). Results from the 4 slug-based platforms now go through the same word-overlap title filter RemoteOK/iCIMS already use; Workday/iCIMS results pass through unfiltered since they're already filtered upstream.

**Also fixed the same round**: JSearch was observed failing with a real HTTP 504 timeout during this exact live testing, contributing 0 results during a SerpApi-exhaustion window — the moment this fallback tier matters most. Now retries once, same treatment as the Adzuna fix above.

**Live-verified before/after on the same real query**: candidate pool for "Financial Advisor"/Toronto went from 376 (mostly noise) to 128 (Adzuna 102, JobsPipe 1, JSearch 8, Workday 17 — all genuinely relevant this time) after the fix. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

### 6 — The actual root cause of "the number never moves", plus 4 new ATS platforms (2026-09-03, later same session)
Full detail in `context/progress-tracker.md`'s Phase 44 entries. Headlines:
- **`lib/jobPreFilter.ts` was hiding 100% of direct-ATS jobs on arrival** — its short-description check fired on every ATS board listing, because those endpoints return empty descriptions by design (Greenhouse is fetched `?content=false`). Measured live: 39/39 greenhouse, 3/3 workday, 2/2 ashby. That silently nullified the entire direct-ATS strategy before a single job reached evaluation. **Visible jobs 13 → 129** after the fix and data repair.
- **The Inngest worker wasn't running**, which is what "scanning takes too long" actually was — searches never finished. It's a local dev process; it stops with the machine.
- **Adzuna given its own `aggregator_indirect` tier** — visible, ranked last, permanently eligible for upgrade — rather than promoted to Tier-1, because its URL is a redirect to a destination we provably cannot see (ECONNRESET server-side; Jina Reader gets a challenge page). Justified by measurement: 34 of 44 companies behind those jobs are on no free ATS, and JSearch re-linking found a genuine employer link for 0 of 8.
- **Four platforms added**: iCIMS (1,617 — the adapter had existed unused since the original ATS work, contributing nothing), Workable (6,499), BambooHR (2,457), Dayforce (692). Registry now **24,059 companies across 8 platforms, 98,549 active cached postings**.
- **Three more bugs surfaced by running things rather than reading them**: the gate discarding links our own rescue had just fixed (abbreviated ATS tenants like `fil` for Fidelity International); stale-marking silently failing on the largest boards (every posting id stuffed into one URL); and `ON CONFLICT ... cannot affect row a second time` when a board returns a duplicate posting id, which killed the whole batch for that company.
- **Standing lesson, learned by getting it wrong**: Dayforce was declared unreachable on the strength of guessed URL shapes, then shipped after the user pushed back — driving the real portal in a browser and reading its own network calls found the API in minutes. Never conclude a platform has no API from guessed URLs; drive its real client first.

**Next session, start here, in order:**
1. **Do a real, full-pipeline live test (not just `searchJobs()` alone) before ever reporting a "how many jobs" number again** — this session's own mistake, corrected the same day, but worth stating as a standing rule: `searchJobs()` alone omits reactive direct-ATS enrichment AND the proactive-crawl cache read, both of which materially change the real candidate count.
2. Spot-check a few more real queries (different title/city, ideally a tech-titled one where RemoteOK/Greenhouse actually have supply) with the new `fetchJobsForCompany` relevance filter in place — only one query ("Financial Advisor"/Toronto) was used to find and verify this fix; confirm it holds up elsewhere and doesn't over-filter a legitimate match.
3. Consider whether `ats_registry` has other duplicate-company rows like the "Blue Moon Metals" / "Blue Moon Metals, Inc." pair found live this session (same real company, two registry rows, both being polled) — a dedup pass may recover some of the `MAX_DIRECT_ATS_COMPANIES=8` enrichment budget currently being spent twice on the same employer.
4. Watch the Workday proactive-crawl cron's own logs for a real pass to confirm `/Search`-board rows actually resolve now (not verified live this session, only `tsc`/`eslint`-checked).
5. Watch `legitimacyRecheckAsync`'s first few real cron runs — confirm probation jobs actually get a second grade and the confirm/clear split behaves as designed.
6. Re-check SerpApi's quota; revert the temporary exhaustion-fallback reorder once it resets.
7. Remaining Part A items, still not done: require Careerjet only if/when the user gets a real Publisher API key; the bigger architecture pivot (decouple live search from provider calls, extend Workday-style proactive coverage to iCIMS using the already-downloaded dataset, regional expansion) — still separate, later phases.
8. Commit this session's work if not already done (see below), and ask before pushing to `origin` — still held local per standing preference.

## Phase 43 — Job-search Part A work: ATS directory seeded (9,646 companies), crawl throughput raised, removal tracking added, JobsPipe wired in and the exhaustion-fallback reordered, a real root-cause found for why Adzuna barely contributes genuine results, and a full Workday proactive-crawl mechanism built from scratch (not in the original Part A scope, added by direct user request)

**Done on the SAME branch as Phase 42** (`feature/supabase-migration`) — direct user decision to keep both migration and job-search work together rather than a third branch, since the job-search work is backend-agnostic and doesn't need its own revert boundary the way the InsForge/Supabase split does.

### A1 — LastRound AI ATS directory ingested
9,935-company Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby directory (huggingface... no — `datahub.io/lastroundai-hiring-data`, CC-BY-4.0, confirmed real via direct download in Phase 40) ingested into `ats_registry`: **9,638 new companies**, registry grew from 8 → 9,646. Live sample of 15 random newly-seeded companies: **14/15 had real, live boards, 467 open roles found** (including a real Epic Games board with 162 open roles) — confirms the dataset's real-world quality, not just its claimed row count.

### A2 — Crawl throughput raised
`CRAWL_BATCH_SIZE` in `lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts`: 15 → 150. Cron in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`: `*/30 * * * *` → `*/15 * * * *`. At the old rate, a full pass over the new 9,646-company registry would have taken ~13 days; at the new rate, under a day. These are plain unauthenticated public JSON endpoints (Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby's own APIs) — no shared quota to protect, unlike SerpApi, so there's no equivalent budget constraint to worry about raising this.

### A3 — Removal/expiry tracking added
New migration `20260903001101_add-discovered-postings-active-tracking.sql`: `is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true` on `discovered_postings`, plus updating `search_discovered_postings()` to filter on it. `crawlKnownAtsCompanies` now diffs each fetch's real external_ids against what's already marked active for that company and flags anything missing — but ONLY on a genuine fetch success (a new `fetchSucceeded` flag), never on a failed fetch, so a transient network error can never be mistaken for "this company removed everything." Live-verified with an isolated test (3 fake postings, one dropped from a simulated re-fetch, confirmed only that one flipped to `is_active=false`).

### JobsPipe wired in for real, and the exhaustion-fallback reordered (temporary, while SerpApi is down)
Built a real `jobsPipeProvider` in `lib/jobScraper.ts` — JobsPipe was only ever tested standalone before (Phase 40), never actually integrated. Direct user decision, given SerpApi is still fully exhausted (re-confirmed live: `0/250` on all 3 keys): while SerpApi is down, **Adzuna + JobsPipe + JSearch now run concurrently and merge together** (`fetchMergedExhaustionFallback()`), replacing the old sequential "try TheirStack, then JSearch, then Apify, first non-empty wins" chain — the old approach meant only ONE fallback's real supply ever reached the user even when several were independently available, the same class of bug already fixed once for Adzuna itself. TheirStack/Apify kept as a last-resort safety net only if the merged set is completely empty. **Explicitly commented as temporary** — revert this reorder once SerpApi's monthly quota resets, it's not a permanent architecture decision. Live-verified: re-running the exact "Financial Advisor"/Toronto query that started this thread went from **6 → 9 visible, correctly-scored jobs** (TD Bank, a second Scotiabank posting, KPMG, Raymond James all newly visible).

### Real root cause found: why Adzuna barely contributes genuine results despite being the biggest raw-volume source
Traced live, not assumed: of 9 canonical Adzuna-sourced jobs matching this query across all of today's testing, **8 were hidden (89%)** — all 8 share `external_apply_url` pointing at `adzuna.ca` itself (Adzuna's own redirect page, correctly on the `low_quality` blocklist in `lib/applyLinkTrust.ts`) and `desc_len: 500` exactly (Adzuna's API only ever returns a fixed-length preview). The free-tier rescue pipeline (`reresolveApplyLinkForJob`) structurally cannot save these: `tryStoredCandidates` needs alternate candidate links Adzuna never provides; `tryAtsGuess` only checks Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby/SmartRecruiters, and these are financial-sector companies (Intact Financial, CI Financial, Hydro One, Vanguard Group) confirmed NOT on those platforms; `tryEmployerAtsDiscovery` needs to extract the real employer domain from the current link, but the current link IS the Adzuna redirect, not the employer's domain — nothing real to extract. **This is the Workday gap manifesting as a different symptom** — these are exactly the kind of enterprise/financial companies real Workday coverage would let the rescue pipeline actually save.

### Full Workday proactive-crawl mechanism, built from scratch (direct user request, not in the original Part A scope)
Real, working "list mode" confirmed to exist for Workday despite it having no dedicated list-everything endpoint: its CXS search API (`fetchRegisteredAtsJobs`, already existed in `lib/atsProviders.ts` from earlier reactive-discovery work) accepts an **empty `searchText`** and returns the board's current postings same as a real browse — confirmed live against real tenants (`axiomspace` total=81, `samaritanhealth` total=264, `2020companies` total=1,384, all with empty query).
- **Real dataset found and verified via `agy` CLI research**: `huggingface.co/datasets/latmay/ats-career-page-urls`, CC-BY-4.0, 69,638 real ATS career page URLs across 40 platforms, confirmed by direct download (not just an AI research claim) — **5,410 are Workday** (exact match to the claimed count). Also covers iCIMS (1,622) and 38 other platforms, not yet used but available for the same treatment later.
- Parsed with the SAME `WORKDAY_TENANT_PATTERN` regex `lib/atsProviders.ts` already uses for reactive discovery (tenant/wdInstance/locale/board) — no new parsing logic invented. **3,113 companies ingested** into `ats_registry` (platform='workday') after company-key deduplication (2,285 rows collapsed to already-known companies).
- New `crawlKnownWorkdayCompanies()` in `lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts` (kept separate from `crawlKnownAtsCompanies` since Workday needs a real POST body and a different config shape, not a drop-in extension) — same diff/is_active tracking pattern as A3. New `proactiveWorkdayCrawlAsync` Inngest cron (`*/15 * * * *`), registered in `app/api/inngest/route.ts`. **Live-verified end to end**: real query against `ats_registry`, real fetch against 5 real tenants, real upsert into `discovered_postings` — 4/5 succeeded, 65 real postings upserted (including one company — `2020companies` — with 1,384 total open jobs).
- **Real, disclosed limitation, NOT resolved this session**: ~1,323 of the 5,410 source rows have their canonical URL ending in a generic `/Search` path (Workday's own default browser route) rather than a company-specific board name — confirmed live this fails with a 404 on the actual CXS API call (company-specific board names worked fine in the same test). These rows are still ingested (a 404 is caught and logged, not fatal), but won't yield postings until resolved — likely needs the same careers-page-HTML-scraping technique `discoverAtsFromDomain()` already uses to find the real board identifier. **Fix this next session.**

### Calibration on volume, stated directly and worth repeating
9 visible jobs for a single financial-sector query is still far from JobRight's claimed 100+, and today's work doesn't close that gap by itself — it's real, verified, incremental progress (roughly 50% more visible genuine results for the exact query tested), concentrated on unlocking sectors/companies the free ATS approach structurally couldn't reach before (Workday-hosted enterprise/financial employers). Full parity still requires: SerpApi's quota actually resetting (the single biggest historical volume driver, currently zero), the Workday `/Search`-board fix above, and eventually the bigger live-query-to-background-index architecture pivot from the original Phase 40 plan — none of which happened today.

**Next session, start here, in order:**
1. **Resolve the Workday `/Search`-board 404 issue** (~24% of the ingested Workday companies) — needs real board-identifier discovery via careers-page HTML scraping, not a data fix.
2. **Check if SerpApi's quota has reset** — if so, revert the temporary Adzuna+JobsPipe+JSearch concurrent-merge reorder in `lib/jobScraper.ts` back to the original sequential-fallback shape (explicitly commented in the code as temporary).
3. **Remaining Part A items, still not done**: the consistency bug fixes (retry Adzuna once on failure instead of swallowing, require 2 consecutive D/F Legitimacy grades before hard-hiding, periodic re-check backfill for AI-hidden jobs), Careerjet as a free concurrent source, RemoteOK as a free supplement.
4. **Commit this session's work if not already done**, and ask before pushing to `origin` — still held local per standing preference.
5. Only after the above: the bigger architecture pivot (decouple live search from provider calls, extend Workday-style proactive coverage to iCIMS using the same verified dataset, regional expansion) — still a separate, later phase, not started.

## Phase 42 — SDK rewrite finished, auth/storage/payments all rebuilt and live-verified against Supabase; real bugs found and fixed at every layer, not a clean mechanical port

**No InsForge account action taken this session** — still fully intact, still paused, per the Phase 41 standing rule. Everything below is app-code and Supabase-project work only.

**SDK rewrite — confirmed complete.** Started this session at 126 files still referencing the InsForge SDK surface; nearly all of those turned out to already be covered by the shim strategy from earlier work (`lib/insforge-server.ts`/`lib/admin/client.ts` kept their exact function names but now wrap a real Supabase client — `.database`/`.auth`/`.storage` namespacing preserved so ~100+ call sites needed zero changes). The **genuinely remaining** work was 6 files with real direct `@insforge/sdk` imports:
- `app/api/auth/request-reset/route.ts`, `app/api/auth/resend-verification/route.ts` — one-line fix, redirected to the existing `createInsforgeServerAnon()` shim.
- `app/api/auth/reset-password/route.ts` — needed real new shim logic: InsForge's 2-step "exchange code for token, then reset with that token" shape has no literal Supabase equivalent. Added `exchangeResetPasswordToken`/`resetPassword` to `lib/insforge-auth-shim.ts`, mapping to Supabase's real recovery flow (`verifyOtp({type: "recovery"})` establishes an in-memory session on the client, then `updateUser({password})` on that same client instance relies on it — no separate token needed for the second call, unlike InsForge's shape).
- `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`'s `getUserJobs()` — was constructing its own anon-key InsForge client inline; redirected to the already-imported `createAdminDbClient()`.
- `lib/insforge-client.ts` (the browser client) — the one file never touched by earlier work, still 100% on `@insforge/sdk/ssr`. Rewritten with the same shim pattern as the server-side files, wrapping `lib/supabase-client.ts`'s real Supabase browser client.
- `lib/admin/client.ts` — false positive, already fully Supabase-backed; only a comment mentioned the old import path.

`npx tsc --noEmit` is clean across the entire codebase after this — confirmed, not assumed. `@insforge/sdk` stays in `package.json` for now (harmless, unused) — removing it is deferred to the final cleanup once InsForge itself is decommissioned, not before, matching this migration's own "don't tidy up until everything is proven stable" discipline.

**A real, separate bug found and fixed: the `auth.users` signup trigger was silently missing.** `public.handle_new_user()` (auto-creates a `profiles` row on signup) ported correctly since it's a `public`-schema function — but the `CREATE TRIGGER on_auth_user_created AFTER INSERT ON auth.users ...` statement attaching it was catalogued under the `auth` schema in the original dump (trigger definitions are filed under the table they're attached to, not the function's own schema) and was excluded along with the rest of `auth` during Phase 41's schema cleanup. Confirmed live: zero rows in `profiles` for a real signed-up-and-verified test user, zero matching triggers in `pg_trigger`. Fixed by recreating the exact original trigger directly; backfilled `profiles` rows for any `auth.users` that predated the fix. **This is a real gap that could recur for any other trigger defined on a table inside an excluded schema** — worth a dedicated audit pass (compare `pg_trigger` on live InsForge against live Supabase) before considering the schema migration fully closed out, not assumed complete just because this one instance is fixed.

**Auth, live-verified end to end via the Browser pane, not just code-reviewed**: signup → email confirmation → sign-in → profile-setup UI all confirmed working with a real test account. One real, separate bug surfaced and fixed along the way: Supabase's default "Confirm signup" email template sends a clickable link (`{{ .ConfirmationURL }}`), not the 6-digit code this app's UI expects — required setting up custom SMTP (routed through the project's existing Resend account, `smtp.resend.com`, sender `onboarding@resend.dev` since no custom domain is verified in Resend yet — a pre-existing gap, not new) and editing the template to display `{{ .Token }}` as visible text (the first attempt wrongly put it in the link's `href`, rendering an unusable non-URL link with the code hidden). Also confirmed live: Resend's sandbox sender only reliably delivers to the Resend account owner's own exact email — any `+alias` variant silently fails with a generic "Error sending confirmation email," confirmed by direct signup-API test comparing an alias (fails) against the plain address (succeeds).

**Storage — a second real gap, same root cause as the missing trigger (schema-migration boundary excluded something load-bearing).** Zero storage buckets existed on the new Supabase project (buckets/storage config live under the `storage` schema, correctly excluded since Supabase manages its own — but that also means nothing recreated the app's actual buckets). Created `resumes` (public, PDF-only, 2MB limit) and `support-attachments` (private), matching Phase 31's own prior audit of the original config. A real authenticated-user upload still failed after that with an RLS violation — InsForge's own storage RLS policies were InsForge-internal and never portable. Added policies on `storage.objects` matching the exact `${user.id}/...` path convention already used in `actions/resumes.ts`/`actions/support.ts` (confirmed via direct grep, not assumed) — authenticated users can manage their own folder, `resumes` also allows public read. Live-verified: admin upload worked before the fix (bucket exists), authenticated-user upload failed with RLS violation, succeeded cleanly after adding the policies — then confirmed again through the real app UI.

**Payments — a full from-scratch rebuild, InsForge's managed Stripe integration has no Supabase equivalent.** Real blocker hit and resolved: this app never held its own direct Stripe API key before (InsForge's managed layer handled Stripe communication internally) — got a real test-mode secret key from the user directly. Confirmed real, live Stripe Price IDs already exist for all 3 paid tiers (`price_1U6v9O...` Command, `price_1U9TzR...` Ace, `price_1U7T5X...` Vanguard, all under one Stripe account). Built:
- `app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts` — new webhook handler, verifies signatures, ports the exact fulfillment logic from the original `public.fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()`/`fulfill_stripe_one_time_purchase()` SECURITY DEFINER trigger functions (recovered from the original pg_dump, not rewritten from memory) into `invoice.paid`/`invoice.payment_failed`/`customer.subscription.deleted`/`checkout.session.completed` handlers. User identity now travels via Stripe's own `client_reference_id`/`metadata.supabase_user_id` (set at Checkout creation) instead of InsForge's `insforge_subject_id` + a separate `payments.customer_mappings` lookup table (excluded, InsForge-internal) — Stripe propagates Checkout metadata onto the created Subscription and from there onto every invoice automatically, so no mapping table is needed.
- `actions/billing.ts`'s `createCheckoutSessionAction`/`createBillingPortalSessionAction` rewritten to call the Stripe SDK directly; portal session lookup uses `user_subscriptions.payment_customer_id` (a column that already existed on the table ported from the original schema — no new table needed for this piece).
- Seeded `subscription_plans` with the 4 real tier rows (recon/command/ace/vanguard) from the original dump — a small, non-user config table, worth carrying over regardless of the "start fully fresh" decision, same reasoning already applied to `ai_model_config`/`app_settings`/`ats_registry`.

**A real Stripe API-shape bug found and fixed via live testing, not assumed from docs**: the first webhook handler version assumed `invoice.lines.data[0].price.id` (an older Stripe API shape) — live testing against this account's actual API version (`2026-07-29.dahlia`) showed the real path is `invoice.lines.data[0].pricing.price_details.price`, confirmed by fetching a real invoice directly via the Stripe SDK. Bonus finding while debugging: the line item's own `metadata.supabase_user_id` is directly present (Stripe propagates Checkout/Subscription metadata onto invoice line items automatically) — simpler than the `parent.subscription_details.metadata` path originally assumed, though that path is kept as a fallback.

**Full payments flow live-verified end to end, real money-shaped test, not just code review**: created a fresh non-admin test user (the admin test account bypasses billing entirely and can't be used for this), went through the real `/pricing` → Stripe Checkout → test-card (4242...) → redirect-back flow for the Command tier, confirmed via direct DB query that `user_subscriptions` was correctly upserted (`tier: command`, `status: active`, correct period dates, correct `payment_customer_id`) only after fixing the API-shape bug above — the first attempt genuinely failed with "no price on its first line item," confirmed via live logs, not assumed working. Test artifacts cleaned up after (Stripe test subscription cancelled, test user deleted, cascades to profiles/user_subscriptions).

**Setup tooling used this session, standing pattern for anything similar going forward**: no `psql` on this machine — an isolated `pg`/`stripe`/`@supabase/supabase-js` Node environment in the session scratchpad (never added to the actual repo's `package.json`) was used for every direct-DB/direct-Stripe diagnostic and fix throughout. Real secrets pasted directly into chat by the user (Stripe secret key, webhook signing secret) were stored into Doppler immediately rather than left sitting only in the transcript — same reasoning as the Supabase credentials in Phase 41.

**OAuth sign-in — a real, separate gap InsForge had fully absorbed, now rebuilt provider by provider.** The login page's own copy said "OAuth secured by InsForge" — not just branding: InsForge held its own pre-registered OAuth apps with every provider as a managed platform feature, so this app never needed direct credentials for standard OAuth sign-in (confirmed by checking Doppler: only `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_*`/`GOOGLE_ONE_TAP_*` existed, both unrelated integrations, zero credentials for actual sign-in with any provider). Confirmed live by clicking "Continue with GitHub" pre-fix: a real `400 "provider is not enabled"` from Supabase, not a code bug. Direct user decision: **set up Google, GitHub, and Microsoft** (all confirmed free — verified live via WebSearch, not assumed); **skip LinkedIn and Apple** (Apple specifically costs $99/year for the Apple Developer Program, confirmed required even just for the "Sign in with Apple" entitlement on the free tier — a real, non-optional cost, not a technicality). Both dead buttons removed from `components/auth/LoginCard.tsx` (their now-unused icon imports cleaned up too) rather than left as broken UI pointing at unconfigured providers.
- **Google**: reused the existing `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` rather than registering a new OAuth client — the user added Supabase's callback URL as an additional authorized redirect URI on that same existing Google Cloud OAuth client. Confirmed live: real, correct redirect to `accounts.google.com`.
- **GitHub**: new OAuth App registered, `GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` stored in Doppler. Confirmed live: real, correct redirect to `github.com`'s sign-in, correctly branded "to continue to Sortie."
- **Microsoft**: new Azure App Registration — a real, direct user mistake caught before it caused problems: the app was initially registered as "Single tenant only," which would have blocked every real candidate outside the developer's own Azure directory from signing in at all; corrected to "any organizational directory and personal Microsoft accounts" before proceeding. `MICROSOFT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` stored; Azure Tenant URL field set to `https://login.microsoftonline.com/common` (confirmed the exact required format via Supabase's own docs — the field name implies a bare tenant id, but it actually wants the full URL). **A real code bug found and fixed via live testing**: `app/api/auth/oauth/[provider]/route.ts` passed this app's own provider name (`"microsoft"`) straight through to `signInWithOAuth()` — failed live with `400 "Provider microsoft could not be found"`, since Supabase's actual GoTrue provider identifier for this is `"azure"` (matching its own dashboard label), not `"microsoft"`. Fixed with a small `SUPABASE_PROVIDER_NAMES` mapping in that route rather than renaming the app's own button/URL naming everywhere else. Confirmed live after the fix: real, correct redirect to `login.microsoftonline.com`.

All three now confirmed working end-to-end up through the real provider's own sign-in page (completing an actual third-party login wasn't done — that needs a real account on each provider, not something to fake in testing).

**Committed to a dedicated branch, direct user decision**: all of this work lives on `feature/supabase-migration` (commit `b8b9493`), not `feature/signal-redesign` — that branch stays exactly where it was (`f57576b`, fully InsForge-based, completely untouched), specifically so reverting to InsForge later is a clean branch switch, not a re-migration of code. Not pushed to `origin` yet, held local by direct request. Real caveat discussed and logged: this only makes the *code* revert free — any real users/payments that accumulate on the Supabase branch once actually deployed still diverge from InsForge's own data the longer it runs, same tradeoff that made migrating away from InsForge cheap pre-launch in the first place.

**Trigger/FK audit completed this session, not deferred** — the `handle_new_user()` gap was found by circumstance (a real test user had no profile row), not a systematic check, so one was run before calling the schema migration closed: every trigger in the original dump was re-reviewed against what schema it's attached to, and every foreign key from a `public` table into any of the 10 excluded InsForge-internal schemas was checked. Result: `on_auth_user_created` (already fixed) was the only real gap — every other excluded trigger is InsForge's own internal housekeeping (auto-`updated_at` triggers on its own platform-management tables) or fully superseded by Supabase's native equivalent (its own OAuth config, its own Realtime, its own Storage), and no `public`-schema foreign key points into any excluded schema at all — every cross-schema FK found stays entirely within the excluded schemas themselves. Zero RLS policies existed on `auth`-schema tables in the original dump either, so nothing was lost there.

**Next session, start here, in order:**
1. **Real remaining gaps, all genuinely blocked on a decision or external action, not on more code**: no real Stripe webhook endpoint registered in Stripe's dashboard yet (only the local `stripe listen` CLI tunnel has been tested) — needs a real registered endpoint once there's a real deployed URL to point it at; Resend still has no verified custom domain (pre-existing gap, affects all outbound email including this new SMTP-for-Supabase-auth setup); Google's OAuth consent screen shows the raw Supabase project domain instead of an app name (needs a Supabase custom domain, $10/mo add-on, plus Google's lighter "brand verification" for non-sensitive scopes — logged as a pre-launch item, not urgent now); the actual cutover (pointing a real deployment at Supabase instead of InsForge) hasn't happened — this whole session was local-dev verification only; LinkedIn/Apple OAuth deliberately not built — don't re-add without being asked.
2. Then resume the job-search volume/authenticity work from Phase 40, per the already-agreed sequencing (after the migration, not before).

## Phase 41 — Supabase project created, schema extracted/cleaned/applied and live-verified; InsForge deliberately left running untouched throughout

**Standing rule, direct user instruction, applies for the rest of this migration**: InsForge is NOT to be deleted, disconnected, or decommissioned at any point until the complete Supabase-based system is built and verified working end-to-end. Both backends stay intact in parallel during the entire transition — InsForge remains the actual live backend for the deployed app the whole time. Do not treat any Supabase progress as a reason to touch InsForge.

**Real correction to Phase 40's own claim**: Phase 40 stated InsForge's pause was resolved based on the user's direct contact with InsForge confirming a per-cycle reset policy. A live check this session (`npx @insforge/cli projects get`, `billing status`) found the project **still `Status: paused`, `Current period end: -`** — the identical unresolved signal from Phase 39. Lesson for next time: a policy confirmation ("it resets each cycle") is not the same claim as "it has reset" — always re-check live status before asserting resolution, even when a human source confirmed the underlying policy.

**Supabase project set up**: `sortie-prod`, ref `fdwdmlpesmxsthgpbopk`, `https://fdwdmlpesmxsthgpbopk.supabase.co`. Credentials (`SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` — new `sb_publishable_...` format, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` — new `sb_secret_...` format, `SUPABASE_DB_URL`) stored in the Doppler `sortie` project (`dev` config) set up this same session — see `context/SETUP.md`'s Doppler section for the full retrieval process on any machine.

**A real security incident during this setup, worth remembering**: running `doppler secrets --project sortie` (without `--only-names`) printed several real secret VALUES into this session's transcript — `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ADZUNA_APP_ID`, `ADZUNA_APP_KEY`, `APIFY_API_TOKEN` (plus `ADMIN_EMAILS`, lower sensitivity). User was advised to rotate these at their source. **Always use `--only-names` when checking Doppler** — never the bare `secrets` list command. Separately, the user pasted the new Supabase project's real credentials (DB password, publishable/secret keys, service role key) directly into chat rather than running the `doppler secrets set` commands themselves — those went straight into Doppler immediately once shared (no reason to leave real secrets sitting only in a chat transcript once they're already exposed there), with a suggestion to regenerate the Secret/Service Role key as optional cheap insurance.

**Getting the schema out of InsForge, since the CLI itself was blocked**: `npx @insforge/cli db export --no-data --include-functions --include-sequences --include-views` (the intended, credential-free approach — no `INSFORGE_DB_URL` needed at all, it exists and would have been the right tool) failed with a real `503` because the project is paused. The user instead pulled a full `pg_dump` manually from InsForge's own dashboard (a real, ~15MB, 18,257-line dump including data) and handed it over as a local file — this is the artifact actually used, not the CLI export.

**Schema extraction and cleaning — a real, non-trivial filtering pass, not a blind apply**: a Node script (`extract-public-schema.mjs`, in the session scratchpad, not committed to the repo) parsed the dump's `-- Name: X; Type: Y; Schema: Z` block markers and kept only `public`-schema objects, dropping everything else. Real findings and fixes made along the way:
- **InsForge wraps its OWN infrastructure in 11 schemas** beyond `public`: `auth`, `compute`, `deployments`, `email`, `functions`, `memory`, `payments`, `realtime`, `schedules`, `storage`, `system` — all InsForge-platform-internal (several names match the CLI's own top-level subcommands exactly), none of it app data, all excluded.
- **InsForge's `auth` schema is its OWN re-implementation**, not literally Supabase's GoTrue — it defines its own `auth.uid()`, `auth.jwt()`, `auth.role()`, `auth.email()` functions and its own `auth.users`/`auth.config`/`auth.custom_oauth_configs`/`auth.email_otps`/`auth.oauth_configs`/`auth.user_providers` tables, deliberately named to match Supabase's convention. Supabase already has its own native versions of all of these — blindly applying InsForge's copies would have conflicted with or shadowed Supabase's real auth internals. Correction to a prior (Phase 39/40) assumption: "RLS uses `auth.uid()`, same as Supabase" is true for how `public`-schema policies *call* it, but InsForge's own backing implementation was never actually Supabase's — this only worked out safely because Supabase's real `auth.uid()` has the same signature/behavior.
- **All grants/ownership tied to InsForge's internal `project_admin` role were stripped** (~700+ per-object `GRANT ... TO project_admin` lines, plus 4 `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE project_admin ...` statements rewritten to `FOR ROLE postgres` — the role actually applying this schema on Supabase). `GRANT ... TO anon`/`TO authenticated` were left untouched — Supabase uses those exact role names natively, no translation needed.
- **One real functional bug caught and fixed, not just deleted**: `public.prevent_profiles_admin_field_change()` (a trigger gating `is_suspended`/`custom_usage_multiplier`/`feature_flags` changes to admin-only) checked `current_user <> 'project_admin'` — since that role will never exist as the executing user on Supabase, this would have silently misbehaved (likely always-blocking legitimate admin writes too). Fixed to check `current_user <> 'service_role'`, Supabase's actual equivalent elevated role.
- **`fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()` and `fulfill_stripe_one_time_purchase()` were deliberately excluded**, not ported — both reference the now-gone `payments.customer_mappings` table and are getting properly rebuilt from scratch against a new Supabase-native `subscriptions` table per this migration's own plan (Approach step 3); applying them as-is would just fail immediately.
- **pg_dump 18's `\restrict`/`\unrestrict` meta-commands** (a newer pg_dump security feature, psql-only, not real SQL) had to be stripped before any raw-SQL client could apply the file — a real, easy-to-miss gotcha for anyone using pg_dump 18+ output with a non-psql client.
- **`pgcrypto`, `vector`, `pg_cron`, `http` extensions were kept** (confirmed `http` is genuinely called by 10 real functions, not leftover cruft) — `pg_cron`'s own `cron.job`/`cron.job_run_details` data was excluded (no data, and this app's real scheduling is Inngest-based, not native pg_cron).

**Applying it**: no `psql` available on this machine — a `pg` npm client was installed in an isolated scratchpad directory (not added to the actual repo's `package.json`/lockfile, this was a one-off DBA operation) and used to apply the cleaned schema directly via `SUPABASE_DB_URL`. Two real errors surfaced and were fixed live before it succeeded (the `\restrict` meta-command, then the `project_admin` role references) — confirms the "every phase surfaces real bugs only visible via live execution" pattern held here too, even for a "just apply a SQL file" step.

**Live-verified after applying, not just trusted from a success message**: direct queries against the new Supabase DB confirm 51 tables, 96 RLS policies (exact match to the source), 146 total functions in `public` (12 real app functions + ~134 from the `vector` extension's own installed helpers, expected and correct, not a bug), and 0 rows in `profiles`/`jobs` (confirmed clean, no data, per direct user decision to start fully fresh — deemed safe specifically because no `auth.users` accounts are being carried over either, so any preserved profile row would have been orphaned/unusable regardless of whether the underlying accounts were real users or dev/test accounts).

**Next session, start here**: continue the migration plan's remaining steps — the ~127-file SDK rewrite (`@insforge/sdk` → `@supabase/supabase-js`, codemod-assisted per Phase 32's established bulk-transform pattern), the payments/Stripe rebuild (new `subscriptions` table + Supabase Edge Function webhook handler, reusing `fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()`'s original fulfillment logic as reference), Supabase Auth/storage bucket configuration, then cutover — all while InsForge stays running untouched per the standing rule above.

## Phase 40 — Backend-migration decision finalized (Supabase, for company-risk reasons), full migration plan scoped, and a separate deep-dive into the job-search volume/authenticity/consistency problem, both logged for execution next session

**No code was written this session.** This was pure research, live verification (WebSearch/WebFetch, a real API test call), and decision-making, in response to the InsForge pause that ended Phase 39. Two independent threads, covered in order below: (1) resolving whether InsForge is safe to keep, and the resulting migration plan; (2) a long-standing, still-unsolved product problem — low job-search volume/authenticity and run-to-run inconsistency — diagnosed to real, code-verified root causes with a concrete fix plan.

### Thread 1 — InsForge resolution and the Supabase migration decision

**The Phase 39 "lifetime cap" fear was wrong.** Direct user contact with InsForge confirmed the usage-limit pause resets per billing cycle, same as any normal metered SaaS. The scary signals from Phase 39 (`billing status` showing no `currentPeriodEnd`, `projects restore` failing with `"Organization is blocked"`) were InsForge's own tooling communicating this badly, not evidence of an unusual policy. InsForge's own in-dashboard "Ask AI" chatbot separately claimed the same monthly-reset policy when asked directly — consistent with the user's finding, but a chatbot answer was correctly treated as unconfirmed until the direct human answer came back. **Do not re-open this question** — it's settled.

**Despite that, the decision is to migrate to Supabase anyway — company-longevity risk, verified independently, not the usage-cap incident.** Two independent AI research passes (Gemini, Perplexity) were run and cross-verified against primary sources (not taken at face value — several specific claims from both were checked live and corrected):
- **InsForge, verified real**: founded 2025, Seattle WA; **6-person team** (InsForge's own ycombinator.com/companies/insforge page, not the "4-person" a research pass initially claimed); Y Combinator **Spring 2026 (S26)** batch (confirmed via the actual Show HN post, news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48181342 — "YC P26" from one search result was a garbled read of "Spring 26"); **$1.5M–$2.2M raised** (Crunchbase shows a Pre-Seed round; other aggregators cite a $1.5M seed led by MindWorks Ventures with ~$2.2M total across 1984 Ventures/Apertu Capital/Llama Ventures/Multimodal Ventures — exact figure genuinely varies by source, treat as a range not a point estimate); public beta blog post Nov 18 2025, wider Show HN launch ~3 months before this session (~May/June 2026).
- **Supabase, verified real, and materially bigger than either research pass initially conveyed**: raised a **$500M Series F in June 2026 at a $10.5B valuation** (confirmed via CNBC, TechCrunch, and PRNewswire independently — Gemini's own "$100M raised, ~$2B valuation" claim was stale, describing an earlier round), total raised **over $1B**, reported ARR ~$170M (up from ~$70M in 2025), with **Stripe and Salesforce Ventures** among the round's investors (a small positive signal for Supabase's own Stripe-integration story, even without a managed wrapper).
- One claim that did NOT hold up on direct verification: Perplexity quoted Simplify's homepage as saying they check "the career pages of 50,000+ companies every hour" — fetching simplify.jobs directly did not find that text. Treat that specific figure as unconfirmed (possibly on a different subpage), not established fact, even though the general shape of what Simplify does (ATS-based crawling at scale) is plausible.

**Given that gap, and that this project is still pre-launch with zero real users** (the same fact Phase 39 used to justify migrating for the wrong reason still applies, correctly, here) — migrating now, while it's cheap, is the right call. Waiting for "a second incident" (one research pass's suggestion) doesn't make sense when there's no live traffic being protected by waiting.

**Migration plan, scoped this session, not yet started:**
- **Real scope** (measured Phase 39, still accurate): 126 files call InsForge's SDK directly, 111 migration files, 13 `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC functions, 1 edge function. Schema/RLS/RPC layer is unusually portable — this project's migrations already use `auth.uid()`/`auth.users`, Supabase's own GoTrue convention, not InsForge-proprietary. **Payments has no direct equivalent**: InsForge manages Stripe webhook registration + catalog sync + fulfillment as infrastructure; Supabase has no such module — this becomes a hand-rolled Stripe webhook Edge Function + fulfillment logic.
- **Real techniques identified specifically because there are zero real users** (these do NOT apply to a normal production migration, and are the reason this is faster than a textbook migration): no data migration needed at all (nothing to export/reconcile); no dual-write/gradual-rollout cutover needed (a single env-var flip once code is ready); no auth-account migration needed (Supabase Auth gets configured fresh, no password-hash-transfer scheme required).
- **Schema step**: take a single `pg_dump --schema-only` snapshot of InsForge's current live schema and apply that once to Supabase, rather than replaying all 111 migrations in historical order — faster and avoids replaying steps that reference objects later altered/dropped. Verify first whether InsForge's internal `auth`/`storage` schema has any InsForge-specific extensions beyond the standard `auth.users`/`auth.uid()` convention already in use — those specific pieces would need translating, not blind dumping.
- **SDK rewrite (the 126 files, the part that does NOT shrink from lack of users)**: use the same scripted-bulk-transform-then-hand-fix-remainder technique this project's own Phase 32 already used successfully for a 35-file blast radius. Most `@insforge/sdk` call sites (`.from().select()`, `.rpc()`, `.auth.*`, `.storage.*`) should map close to mechanically onto `@supabase/supabase-js`'s equivalent shape (both PostgREST-based) — a ts-morph/jscodeshift codemod for the bulk, manual fixes for divergent call sites.
- **Stripe rebuild**: base it on Supabase's own official Next.js + Stripe subscription starter kit (a real, mature, documented pattern — Edge Function → verify webhook signature → upsert subscription state → RLS gates access) rather than inventing the architecture from scratch.
- **Revised estimate, grounded in this project's own git-commit-timestamp history** (Phase 36 ran ~2 hours, Phase 38 ran ~11.5 hours near-continuous and still shipped a live bug chain — session length here is bug-driven, not a fixed clock unit): **roughly 5–8 sessions**, down from an initial ~8–12 estimate for a generic migration — the reduction is entirely from removing data/cutover/auth-migration overhead; the SDK-rewrite volume and the inevitable live-bug-shakeout (this project has hit one in every single major phase to date, with no exception) do not shrink just because there are no users.
- **Next session, start here**: `EnterPlanMode` given the real blast radius (same practice already used for regional pricing and the AI model tiers), before touching any code.

### Thread 2 — Job-search volume/authenticity/consistency: real root causes found, a concrete fix plan, explicitly sequenced AFTER the migration above

**Direct user framing**: search results are both too few (24-47 raw / 8-21 genuine per search vs. a funded competitor "JobRight" showing ~100) AND inconsistent (the same search returns different numbers on repeat runs). Both were traced to real, code-verified causes, not guessed at.

**Root cause of low volume — architectural, not "need more sources"**: this project runs live, synchronous, quota-dependent provider calls per search (SerpApi capped at a practical ~100 results/query, shared across 3 free-tier accounts totaling 750 searches/month). Funded competitors (JobRight, and — with one specific quote unverified, see above — Simplify) run a decoupled background-ingestion index against a large ATS company seed list and serve search from their own pre-built index, never live-querying providers per request. This project's own proactive ATS crawl (built Phase 39, `lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts` + `proactiveAtsCrawlAsync` cron) is the right mechanism, just critically undersized: only ~80 organically-discovered companies, and — verified directly from the code — even that would take **~14 days for one full crawl pass** at the current cadence (`*/30 * * * *` cron, `CRAWL_BATCH_SIZE = 15` in [lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts](lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts:48), confirmed live in [lib/inngest/functions.ts:1200-1201](lib/inngest/functions.ts:1200)).

**The single highest-leverage, zero-cost fix identified and verified real** (not just an AI research claim — confirmed via direct `WebFetch` of datahub.io, not taken on faith): LastRound AI's "ATS Company Directory" dataset — **9,935 companies** mapped to ATS vendor (Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby only) + board slug, CC BY 4.0 licensed, published on Figshare with real DOIs. Ingesting this into `ats_registry` plus raising `CRAWL_BATCH_SIZE` (from 15 to ~150-200) and the cron frequency (from every 30 min to every 10-15 min) — both free, since these are unauthenticated public JSON endpoints already confirmed byte-identical to what [lib/atsProviders.ts](lib/atsProviders.ts) already calls (`boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/{slug}/jobs`, `api.lever.co/v0/postings/{slug}`, `api.ashbyhq.com/posting-api/job-board/{slug}`) — is the recommended first build, not yet started.

**A structural gap this specific fix does NOT close**: `CRAWLABLE_PLATFORMS` in `lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts` is deliberately limited to Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby (the only 3 with a genuine "list the whole board, no search term" endpoint) — and the LastRound directory itself is built from the same 3. **Workday/iCIMS/SmartRecruiters/Taleo employers are NOT reachable by this free fix at all** — and this matters specifically here, because those are exactly the platforms behind this project's own worst historical bugs (Manulife, TD, RBC, CIBC, Scotiabank — the Phase 38/39 bug chain). Closing that gap needs genuinely new engineering later (per-tenant search-based polling, since these platforms have no "list everything" endpoint), not a data-ingestion fix.

**Root cause of inconsistency — separate bugs, independent of data volume, all verified in the actual code**:
1. `fetchAndMergeAdzuna` ([lib/jobScraper.ts:814-847](lib/jobScraper.ts:814)) silently swallows any Adzuna failure to an empty array with just a `console.error`, no retry — a transient blip silently shrinks results with zero visibility.
2. The Legitimacy AI grade is a single non-deterministic LLM call that can permanently hard-hide a real job on one unlucky D/F — `is_hidden` is only ever set true, never reset, and repeat searches deliberately skip re-evaluating already-scored jobs (a Phase 38 cost-saving fix with this unintended side effect). A bad grade compounds forever instead of self-correcting.
3. Which fallback tier answers a search (SerpApi → TheirStack → JSearch → Apify) depends on live quota state at that exact moment — same query, structurally different data source, depending only on timing.

**Fixes identified, not yet built**: retry Adzuna once before swallowing; require 2 consecutive D/F grades before hard-hiding a job; add a periodic re-check backfill for AI-hidden jobs (reusing the exact pattern from the Phase 39 backfill migration `20260901142854_backfill-legitimacy-hide-reset.sql`).

**A real, live-money test run this session**: the user's own JobsPipe trial key was tested live against `api.jobspipe.dev/v1/jobs/search` (POST, `Authorization: Bearer jp_live_...`, confirmed via their own docs first to avoid a wasted call) — **HTTP 200, real production data**, not a stub. A 3-result sample (Toronto software engineer search) returned 1 genuine Ashby-direct ATS link and 2 Indeed aggregator-tier links, zero low-quality mirrors. Notably, every record already carries server-side freshness/removal-tracking fields (`status`, `closed_at`, `closed_reason`, `verified_at`, `last_seen_at`, and an unpopulated-in-sample `ghost_score` field) — this could reduce or eliminate the custom expiry-diffing engineering otherwise needed for anything sourced through them. **Verdict, direct and explicit**: real and useful as a bounded paid supplement (Builder $49/mo = 25,000 jobs, Scale $349/mo = 300,000 jobs; genuinely incremental since it draws from Workday + Indeed + Glassdoor + 30 ATS sources, not just Google Jobs again) — but explicitly **NOT a complete fix by itself**: still far below JobRight's claimed ~400k new jobs/day, doesn't touch the consistency bugs above (those live in this project's own evaluation logic, not any data source), and its non-North-American coverage is unverified. This test likely consumed ~3 of the trial's 1,000 monthly job-credits (billed per job record returned, not per request) — be deliberate about further test calls.

**Other sources checked this session, verdicts**:
- **JSearch/OpenWeb Ninja**: confirmed to source the SAME underlying Google for Jobs index SerpApi's own `google_jobs` engine already scrapes — a paid JSearch tier alongside SerpApi would be redundant spend, not incremental coverage. (Already correctly used as a rare fallback-only tier per Phase 39, not a routine source — that reasoning holds.)
- **Techmap**: re-confirmed (consistent with Phase 39's own live finding) to have the same LinkedIn-redirect-heavy authenticity mix as everything else despite cheap pricing (~€0.002/job direct API, or RapidAPI Free/$29/$99/$299 tiers) — not worth adding; cheap raw volume that still needs the same expensive rescue pipeline to become genuine is poor value specifically for the "genuine count" goal.
- **Careerjet** (`search.api.careerjet.net/v4/query`): verified real and free (registration-gated API key, rate-limited unless a higher limit is requested) — a genuine structured aggregator like Adzuna, recommended as a second free concurrent-merge source.
- **RemoteOK** (`remoteok.com/api`): verified real, fully free, zero-auth — narrow (remote-only) but zero-friction, worth adding as a low-effort supplement.
- **Reed.co.uk Jobseeker API**: verified real, free registration, 1000 requests/day — UK-only, relevant only if/when UK market expansion is prioritized, not now.
- **Recommended paid launch-budget stack** (the user explicitly cannot spend heavily pre-launch): pay for a real SerpApi tier to remove the free-tier fragility that directly caused this project's own quota-exhaustion incident (Production, $150/mo for 15,000 searches, suggested sizing absent real traffic data — re-size once there is real traffic); skip JSearch and Techmap subscriptions entirely; add JobsPipe's $49/mo Builder tier as the one genuinely incremental paid addition, and only after the free fixes below are built and measured, not bought upfront on faith.

**Explicit build order, direct user decision, end of session**:
1. Complete the Supabase migration (Thread 1) first — nothing in Thread 2 needs to wait on this technically (it's backend-agnostic app-level logic), but the user chose to sequence it after the migration rather than run both at once, consistent with this project's own established pattern of not mixing infrastructure changes with feature work in the same stretch (exactly what produced the Phase 38/39 bug chains).
2. Then, in order: (a) ingest the LastRound directory + turn up crawl throughput (free); (b) the consistency bug fixes (free); (c) measure the real remaining gap; (d) only then decide on JobsPipe, sized to whatever gap actually remains; (e) the full live-query-to-background-index architecture pivot (decouple SerpApi/Adzuna/Careerjet from the request path entirely, extend proactive coverage to Workday/iCIMS/SmartRecruiters, region-specific boards gated on real user demand) as its own later, separately-scoped phase — deliberately sequenced after the migration specifically so the same cron/query code isn't rewritten once for InsForge and again during the SDK migration.

**Calibration, stated directly to the user and worth repeating here**: high confidence in the diagnosis and that the free fixes will produce a real, measurable improvement — the causes are verified in this project's actual code, not inferred from research alone. NOT a guarantee of a complete fix or of matching JobRight's scale — every phase in this project's history, without exception, has surfaced real bugs only visible via live testing, and full parity with an 8M-job/$10.5B-funded competitor likely still requires paid data investment beyond what's scoped here.

## Phase 39 — Built the validated 3-phase redesign, found and fixed a real bug chain in it live, InsForge got paused mid-session, decided to migrate to Supabase

**Next session, start here, in order:**
1. **Resolve InsForge access first** — either the user upgrades InsForge to Pro (fast, ~$20-25/mo, zero migration risk) or has heard back from support about the lifetime-cap question. Nothing else in this project can be live-verified against the real DB until one of these happens.
2. **Then start the Supabase migration**, direct user decision at session end. Do NOT re-litigate whether to migrate — that was already decided. Do plan it properly before touching code (this session explicitly flagged that skipping planning here, under time pressure, is exactly what caused this session's own bug chain). Real scope, already measured this session: 126 files call InsForge's SDK directly (`grep -rl "@insforge/sdk\|createInsforgeServer\|createAdminClient\|createAdminDbClient"`), 111 migration files, 13 `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC functions, 1 edge function. The schema/RLS/RPC layer is unusually portable (this project's migrations already use `auth.uid()`/`auth.users` — Supabase's own GoTrue convention, not InsForge-proprietary), but the SDK-call layer across all 126 files is a real, manual rewrite. **Payments is the piece with no direct equivalent**: InsForge manages Stripe webhook registration + catalog sync + fulfillment (`fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()`) as infrastructure; Supabase has no such module — this becomes a hand-rolled Stripe webhook Edge Function + fulfillment logic, real new code, not a swap. No real users exist yet (never launched), so auth-account migration is not a risk factor — that's the one thing working in favor of doing this now rather than after launch.

**What shipped this session, before the InsForge pause hit** — the 3-phase redesign RESUME.md's own Phase 38 section validated but explicitly left unbuilt:
- **Phase 1** (`lib/reresolveApplyLink.ts`'s `verifyApplyLinksBeforeReveal`) — every job's apply link gets a free-tier resolution attempt, synchronously, before a search's results are ever revealed.
- **Phase 2** (`lib/evaluator.ts`'s `evaluateJobCompatibilityLite` + `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s `evaluateJobChunk` in `mode: "lite"`) — the list-view AI pass now only asks for score/reasoning/skills/Legitimacy grade, not the full 10-dimension write-up.
- **Phase 3** (`evaluateJobFullAsync`, triggered by `actions/jobs.ts`'s `requestFullJobEvaluation`, surfaced via `components/job-details/RequestFullEvaluationButton.tsx`) — the full rubric now only runs on-demand, when a candidate actually opens a job that's already lite-scored. Deliberately never overwrites the lite pass's score/grade/skills/reasoning ("score drift" — the original research flagged an inconsistent list-vs-detail score as an instant trust-breaker).
- **Proactive ATS crawl** (`lib/proactiveAtsCrawl.ts`, `proactiveAtsCrawlAsync` cron, `migrations/20260901120000_add-proactive-ats-crawl.sql`'s `discovered_postings` table) — background-crawls companies `ats_registry` already knows about, independent of live searches, and a live search's own enrichment step now also pulls from this cache via full-text match. Real, deliberately scoped-down for this session: only knows ~7-80 companies depending on when checked (whatever's been organically discovered), not a curated seed list — genuinely scaling this (the actual lever for JobRight-scale volume, per the discussion this session) still needs either a real company seed list from the user or a deliberate bulk-discovery pass, neither done yet.

**The real bug chain found live while testing the above, in the order discovered — each one is a real, separate mistake, not one bug with several symptoms**:
1. An early version of Phase 1 hard-hid any job the free link tier couldn't fix, unconditionally. Live-tested: collapsed a real "Financial Advisor"/Toronto search from 51 found to 16 visible.
2. Fix attempt #1 moved the paid-tier link rescue back to running only for jobs scoring ≥70 — this was a DIFFERENT, worse bug: a job's authenticity was now gated on how well it fit ONE candidate's profile. Confirmed live: a software-developer profile searching "Financial Advisor" scored everything ~20/100, so nothing ever qualified for rescue, and real official postings (Scotiabank, TD, CIBC) got hidden as if they were fake.
3. Fix #2 removed the score gate entirely, ran the paid rescue unconditionally, capped at 30/search as the safety valve — the cap itself was picked without checking the real budget. Confirmed live via `curl .../account.json`: all 3 SerpApi accounts are 250 searches/month each (750/month combined), and a cap of 30 meant a single search could cost up to 31 real calls — over 4% of the ENTIRE MONTHLY budget in one search. This is almost certainly what finished off the third SerpApi key's remaining quota the same day (confirmed live: `SerpApi key 1/3 exhausted`, `key 2/3 exhausted` in Vercel logs, all 3 keys later confirmed at 250/250 used). Fixed: cap dropped to 5/search, applies uniformly including to admin (the one cap in this pipeline that does NOT get an admin exemption — see its own comment in `reresolveApplyLink.ts` for why: it protects a SHARED account pool every user's search depends on, not a per-user allowance).
4. The lite Legitimacy grading prompt graded short descriptions as "generic boilerplate" (a real instruction, meant to catch ghost listings) — but Adzuna's own API only ever returns a ~500-char preview snippet, not the full posting, so real postings from real companies (confirmed: one had a genuine `job-boards.greenhouse.io` link, still got hidden) were being graded D/F purely for being short. Fixed: the prompt now explicitly requires a concrete red flag (unidentifiable company, scam language, self-contradiction) — brevity alone is never sufficient.
5. The SerpApi-exhaustion fallback chain (`lib/jobScraper.ts`) picked whichever ONE source (TheirStack, Adzuna, Apify) returned first, treating "any non-empty response" as success — so once SerpApi was dead and TheirStack happened to return even 3 results, Adzuna's real 263-result volume for the same query was never reached at all. Fixed: Adzuna now always merges into whatever the fallback tier returns, same additive relationship it already has with a healthy SerpApi.
6. Every search hardcoded `countryCode: "ca"` regardless of the actual searched location — meaning a search for "London, UK" would have queried Canada's Adzuna endpoint and Google's Canada-localized index. Fixed: resolved from SerpApi's own free (confirmed live: doesn't consume search quota, works even against an exhausted key) Locations API, which returns a real `country_code` per location.
7. **A real backfill was needed and run** (`migrations/20260901142854_backfill-legitimacy-hide-reset.sql`) — 70 jobs across all users that had been wrongly hidden by bugs #1-4 above (while they were still live) were reset (`is_hidden=false`, score cleared) so they'd get a clean re-evaluation under the fixed logic on their next search. Only touched jobs hidden BY AI judgment (`is_hidden=true AND match_score IS NOT NULL`) — never the separate, correct Phase 2 pre-filter hides.

**Also shipped**: JSearch/OpenWeb Ninja (`lib/jobScraper.ts`'s `jsearchProvider`) wired in as a second SerpApi-exhaustion fallback tier — same underlying Google for Jobs data SerpApi already queries (confirmed via JSearch's own product description), so deliberately NOT run concurrently on every search (would pay its PAYG cost for near-duplicate data) — only reached on quota exhaustion, same as TheirStack/Apify. Using a trial/PAYG key (`OPENWEBNINJA_API_KEY`, both in `.env` and on Vercel Preview) — swap for a dedicated key once the user has one.

**Researched, live-tested, NOT wired in**: Techmap (via RapidAPI, `daily-international-job-postings.p.rapidapi.com`) — real volume confirmed live (427 results for one title+city+month combo, well above anything else tested), but a live sample of 10 real results found the SAME authenticity mix as every other source (1 genuine ATS link, most LinkedIn redirects) — directly contradicts an earlier AI-research claim (from an `agy` pass) that Techmap has "a large share of direct ATS links." Lesson: that research was relayed to the user before being verified, and the correction had to happen after. A real outreach email was sent to `data@techmap.io` asking about their genuine PAYG pricing — but **it was sent from `sachishab.work@gmail.com`, not the user's own `ridham.doshi.rd@gmail.com`** — the Gmail MCP connector on this session is authenticated to the wrong account. Needs fixing before any further email is sent on the user's behalf; check connector settings.

**New portability infrastructure, direct user request** (prompted by "what if I open this on another account/device"): `context/SETUP.md` (a real bootstrap walkthrough — `.env`, InsForge/Vercel CLI linking, and an honest section on why skills/MCP servers can't be auto-installed by an AI session) and `scripts/verify-setup.mjs` (a real, reusable check — caught and fixed a genuine bug in itself before shipping: Windows CRLF line endings broke its own `.env` parser, so every real key silently reported as missing). Both wired into the top of `AGENTS.md` and this file. A real, tested dead-end worth remembering: Vercel's env var store looked like a possible secrets-retrieval mechanism but isn't — `vercel env pull` writes the literal string `[SENSITIVE]` for every "Secret"-type value instead of the real one (confirmed live against this project's own real keys); it's write-only by design, not a vault. Don't re-attempt that path.

**A real process lesson from this session, worth remembering going forward**: three of the bug-chain items above (#2, #3, #5) were each shipped, then found broken by the NEXT thing the user tried, then fixed again — a live, iterative debugging loop playing out in production (well, Preview) in front of the user, not caught by planning first. `tsc`/`eslint` clean and even direct live testing (repeated real searches) didn't catch the SerpApi-budget math error (#3) or the cross-source fallback race (#5) — those needed checking the REAL account limits and REAL log output specifically, not just "does a search return results now." Worth slowing down for real budget/capacity math before shipping a change that touches a shared, metered resource, even under time pressure.

## Phase 38 — Job-listing authenticity/volume/speed: real bug chain fixed, golden-record architecture shipped, next redesign validated but not built

**Next session, start here** — a 3-phase evaluation redesign was independently validated by a deep research pass (agy) as "highly recommended," but nothing below has been built yet:
1. **Fast link pass, no AI, before anything else.** The moment jobs are canonicalized, resolve/verify every job's apply link concurrently (plain HTTP, not throttled — link-checking isn't limited by an LLM provider's rate limit the way AI evaluation is). Free-tier resolution only in this pass — the existing paid-tier escalation for high-scoring jobs can't apply yet since no AI score exists at this point; that's a disclosed, accepted trade-off, not an oversight.
2. **Reveal the list, then a LITE AI pass** — once the link pass finishes, show the results (every visible job already has a verified genuine link). The list-view AI call shrinks to ONLY match score + one-line reasoning + matched/missing skills + the Legitimacy grade (still needed for the AI-side hide reason) — drop the full 10-dimension write-ups and all JD-extraction fields (responsibilities/requirements/niceToHave/benefits/aboutRole/salary/hiringProcess/seniorityLevel/yearsExperienceRequired/companyDomain/titleScopeMismatch) from this pass entirely.
3. **Full rubric, on-demand, only when a job is opened** — clicking into a job triggers a second, one-off AI call for just that job, generating the full write-ups and extraction. Reuses the same UX pattern `RequestScoringButton.tsx`/`requestJobEvaluation` already use for a never-scored job.

**Two real caveats the research flagged — design around these, don't discover them the hard way**:
- **The input-token fallacy**: for large-context calls, INPUT tokens (the raw job description in the prompt) often dominate cost/latency more than output length does. If Phase 2's "lite" call still stuffs the full raw job description into the prompt just to get a short answer back, the savings will be much smaller than expected — Phase 2 needs to trim what it *sends*, not just what it asks for back.
- **Score drift**: Phase 3 must treat Phase 2's match score as immutable context and only justify/extract around it — never let it independently recalculate a different score for the same job once someone's already seen the first one. That mismatch (85% in the list, 65% on open) would break trust instantly.

**The other validated-but-not-built piece, lower priority than the redesign above**: a standalone, scheduled Inngest cron that proactively crawls a curated, growing list of Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby endpoints into `job_sources` on its own schedule, fully decoupled from live user searches — this is the actual lever for closing the volume gap against a funded competitor ("JobRight" showed ~100 results for a search this app showed 24-47 raw/8-21 genuine for), not "add more real-time aggregators." Direct-ATS enrichment today is reactive-only (only polls a company's board if an aggregator already surfaced that company for THIS search), which structurally means it can never discover a company the aggregators haven't already surfaced, and real employer postings can sit unindexed by Google for 2-5 days before a reactive-only approach would ever see them. Full reasoning and a 3-way factor breakdown of the volume gap (proactive-index advantage / possibly-looser competitor authenticity bar / paid enterprise data feeds like LinkUp or CoreSignal being out of budget) is in this session's own research artifacts if needed — the summary above is what actually matters for the next build.

---
