**How this session started**: a user report that a live search was showing jobs with a fabricated "60% MATCH" score and a reasoning string literally admitting "Automated evaluation failed for this job." Root cause: several concurrent searches (this account had 3 running at once) were overwhelming the free-tier Gemini key's real, confirmed 15-requests-per-minute/500-per-day cap, pushing the model's retry chain onto weaker backup models that returned malformed JSON for whole 5-job batches at once.

**Scoring reliability fixes (shipped first, before the listing-architecture work)**:
- `evaluator.ts` returns `matchScore: null` on a genuine failure (never a fabricated number), retries once before giving up.
- The actual Gemini call moved into its own Inngest function (`evaluateJobChunk`) with a global `throttle` — enforced across every concurrent search from every user, not just paced within one run.
- Chunks (5 jobs each) fire concurrently instead of serially with a wasted 3s sleep between each.
- Repeat searches skip jobs that already have a real `match_score` instead of re-evaluating everything every time — this was also silently burning real AI cost/rate-limit budget on zero new information.
- A one-time backfill (`scripts/backfill-genuine-link-gate.mjs`, real, kept — not a throwaway script) applies the current rules retroactively to already-scored jobs from before a given rule existed, since a job only goes through the live evaluation gate once, at its first scoring, and the "don't re-score" fix above means it's never naturally re-checked. Re-run this (scoped to a user id as `argv[2]`, or unscoped for everyone) any time a hide/genuine-link rule changes and there's a real backlog of already-scored jobs that predate it.

**The golden-record listing architecture (decided after a multi-round research arbitration — job_sources-first was chosen over a lighter single-table approach specifically because a v1 matching bug WILL happen, and only raw per-source data lets you replay/reprocess to fix a bad merge after the fact)**:
- `job_sources` table — every raw scrape from every source (SerpApi, Adzuna, direct-ATS), appended, never overwritten.
- `lib/jobCanonicalization.ts` + `merge_job_source` RPC (`migrations/20260831233851_add-job-canonicalization.sql`) — a 3-tier match (ATS's own posting ID → normalized company+title+location fingerprint → description-hash similarity) merges each raw hit into one canonical `jobs` row, with source-priority precedence so a better later source can upgrade an existing row's data, never the reverse.
- `lib/jobPreFilter.ts` (Phase 2) — cheap, zero-AI-cost rejection before evaluation: near-empty descriptions with no salary, known staffing agencies, spam phrasing, and (added later this session) anything posted more than 60 days ago, uniform across every source.
- `lib/jobRelevance.ts` + `rank_jobs_by_relevance` RPC (`migrations/20260901000610_add-relevance-ranking.sql`) — when a search has more not-yet-scored jobs than ~20, ranks them against the candidate's own skills/desired titles using Postgres full-text search (the SAME infra full-text search already built on) and evaluates the most relevant first. An embeddings-based version of this was explicitly researched and rejected twice (by two different research passes) as unjustified at this product's current scale — this was the free, "good enough" replacement.
- The "genuine employer/ATS link, or a trusted major board — nothing else" hard-hide gate in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s persist-chunk step. Started as "LinkedIn only," which tanked visible volume for no real authenticity gain — widened same-day, on direct user pushback, to accept any of `applyLinkTrust.ts`'s existing curated `TIER1_SAFE_AGGREGATOR_HOSTS` list (LinkedIn, Indeed, Glassdoor, ZipRecruiter, CareerBuilder, government job banks, and others with real trust & safety teams) — still hard-rejects confirmed low-quality mirrors (BeBee, Jooble, Workopolis, etc.) and anything unclassified.
- `mergeWithAdzuna`/`fetchAndMergeAdzuna` in `lib/jobScraper.ts` — Adzuna now runs on EVERY search, concurrently with SerpApi, not gated behind "SerpApi's own result count looked thin." Real, measured live case that motivated this: SerpApi found 33 results for "Financial Advisor"/Toronto (comfortably above the old 25-result thin-threshold, so Adzuna was never even queried), while Adzuna alone had 267 for the identical query.

**Real bugs found and fixed while building/using the above, roughly in the order discovered**:
1. **Canonicalization data-loss bug**: SerpApi's own `posted_at` field is a relative string ("7 days ago"), not a timestamp — `merge_job_source`'s `p_posted_at timestamptz` column rejected it outright, and the resulting per-job RPC error was silently folded into the same "collision" counter as a genuine dedup merge. A real search that should have found 33 distinct jobs collapsed to 5, and the log line read like normal deduping, not data loss. Fixed with a real relative-date parser (`parsePostedAt` in `jobCanonicalization.ts`) and split error-vs-collision logging so this exact failure mode can never hide again.
2. **Stale back-button search results** — confirmed as real, documented Next.js 16 behavior (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/04-glossary.md`'s own Client Cache entry: "Pages are not cached by default but are reused during browser back/forward navigation"), not a bug in this app's code. Fixed with a `popstate` listener forcing `router.refresh()`, and the refreshed `initialJobs` prop is synced into client state during render (React's documented pattern for this — not an effect+setState, which this project's own `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule correctly rejects).
3. **Source-priority regression**: the canonicalization upsert only lets a new source overwrite `external_apply_url` when its `source_priority` is `>=` the row's stored value — but the link-repair pipeline (`reresolveApplyLink.ts`) was writing newly-resolved upgrades (e.g. a bebee.com mirror upgraded to a real Workday link) without ever bumping `source_priority` to match. The row stayed recorded at SerpApi's priority (50) forever, so the next time the SAME real posting got re-scraped (still SerpApi, still priority 50), the `>=` check silently reverted the good link back to the bad one. This was almost certainly the real cause of "different jobs/counts every time I search again." Fixed by computing and storing the real trust-tier priority (ats=100, employer=90, aggregator=50, else=10) on every successful resolution.
4. **Client-polling race**: `match_score` used to be written to the DB BEFORE the link-repair/hide decision, as a separate, later update. The client's polling stops as soon as every watched job has a non-null `match_score` — if a poll landed in the gap between "score written" and "link-repair/hide finished," the client froze on the stale, not-yet-fixed link forever (confirmed live from a real screenshot: a job showing a "Third-party source" warning that the DB already showed had a genuine Workday link). Fixed by moving link-repair BEFORE the main write and folding score + link + hide decision into ONE atomic update per job.
5. **The atomicity fix's own real slowdown**: making that one write atomic meant a chunk's 5 jobs were still processed one at a time internally in a plain `for` loop — job 2's own link-check (several real, sequential HTTP calls) couldn't even start until job 1's entire resolution had finished. Confirmed live: a 27-job search sat at 1 scored after 75 seconds. Fixed by running a chunk's 5 jobs concurrently via `Promise.all` instead — same atomicity, ~5x speedup bounded by the slowest single job instead of the sum of all 5. This exact tension (link-repair and AI scoring still coupled in one step) is what directly motivated the 3-phase redesign above.
6. **Live-hide gap**: the results-polling merge updated a job's score/fields but never checked `is_hidden`, so a job the evaluator hid mid-poll stayed visible until a full page reload. Fixed — polling now filters out any job that becomes hidden mid-poll.
7. **Vercel env drift, later ruled out as the actual cause but fixed defensively anyway**: `ADZUNA_APP_ID`/`ADZUNA_APP_KEY` were reset on the Preview environment to match `.env` exactly, after initially (wrongly) suspecting a stale credential was blocking Adzuna. The real cause of that specific "5 jobs only" incident turned out to be bug #1 above, not credentials — but the reset is harmless and now confirmed correct regardless.

**UI/UX changes, direct user requests, same session**:
- The "Third-party source" trust badge on a job card now only renders once a job is actually scored (`match_score !== null`) — it was showing on freshly-scraped, not-yet-evaluated jobs, reading as a final verdict on a link the app hadn't tried to fix yet.
- The results section now stays behind one full loader ("Scanning the field — X of Y evaluated") until at least 10 jobs (or all of them, if fewer) have actually finished evaluating, then reveals in one clean switch — instead of showing a wall of individually-"Scoring…" cards the instant a fresh search returns its raw, unevaluated jobs. Counts "finished" (scored, hidden or not), not "visible" — waiting for 10 visible specifically could mean waiting for nearly the whole batch on a search where most jobs end up hidden by the authenticity gate.

**Deliberately not built this session, disclosed, not forgotten**: the 3-phase redesign and the proactive-ATS-polling cron (see "Next session, start here" above) — both fully scoped and validated, zero code written yet. Also not addressed: `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx`'s own lazy apply-link-trust note (`ApplyLinkTrustNote.tsx`) doesn't share the new "only render once scored" timing fix the list card got — lower priority since the list view is the primary surface a candidate scans quickly during active search.

## Phase 37 — Homepage/pricing caching fix, search bar scoped off marketing homepage, job-detail mockup divergence resolved

**Homepage/pricing static-render fix**: `app/page.tsx` and `app/pricing/page.tsx` each called `getCurrentUser()` server-side purely to pick the Navbar variant — confirmed live, this forced the whole route dynamic (`Cache-Control: no-store` on every request, ~2.7s cold starts). Investigation found the bug was bigger than it looked: `CTASection.tsx` (rendered on both pages) had its own separate `getCurrentUser()` call, and `getPlansForPricing()` was using a cookies()-bound client for a plan-list read that never needed identity. Fixed all three: both pages are now static Server Components; new `AuthStateProvider`/`NavbarAuto`/`FreeTierCta`/`PlanUpgradeCta` (`components/auth/`, `components/layout/NavbarAuto.tsx`, `components/homepage/`) resolve the real auth state client-side instead — an optimistic cookie-presence read via `useSyncExternalStore` (no hydration mismatch, matches React's endorsed pattern for this — a naive `setState`-in-`useLayoutEffect` version was tried first and rejected by this project's own `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule), reconciled against the pre-existing (previously unused) `/api/auth/refresh` route once mounted. Traced a real SDK routing risk before shipping: the browser client's own `auth.getCurrentUser()` method would have posted to the InsForge backend's absolute origin instead of this app's own domain on a cold load — cookies scoped to this app's domain wouldn't ride along cross-origin, silently misreporting real signed-in users as signed-out. Worked around by calling `/api/auth/refresh` directly instead of the SDK method.

**Real, disclosed result — did NOT fully achieve the goal, caught by checking live headers rather than assuming success**: after deploying to Preview and running `curl -I`, both `/` and `/pricing` STILL show `Cache-Control: no-store` — `CTASection`'s regional-pricing lookup (`getRequestCountry()`, `lib/geo.ts`) reads `headers()`, and since this project has no Partial Prerendering enabled, one dynamic child (`CTASection`, rendered on `/` too via its own `#pricing` section) taints the whole page regardless of the auth fix. Net effect: the auth-related dynamism is genuinely gone and the code is cleaner/more correct, but **neither route actually got faster** — that needs a follow-up (e.g. move country detection to Edge Middleware, or make the price display itself a client-side fetch using the exact same optimistic-then-reconcile pattern just built for auth). **Explicitly deferred by direct user instruction this session** ("leave it as is for now") — not a forgotten gap, a deliberate one. Also disclosed and deferred: the "brief flash on a slow connection" trade-off was reasoned through but never actually watched happening — the Browser pane's tools don't expose network throttling.

**Search bar scoped off the marketing homepage**: direct user instruction — the persistent site-wide search bar (`GlobalSearchBar`, Phase 30) is an app-shell feature for after login/signup, not something `/` should show even to an already-signed-in visitor (e.g. clicking the logo, which now correctly shows the authenticated nav there per the fix above). New optional `Navbar`/`NavbarAuto` `showSearchBar` prop, default `true` (all 37 other pages that render `Navbar` directly are unaffected) — `app/page.tsx` passes `false`. Deliberately scoped to `/` only, not `/pricing` (user said "marketing homepage" specifically, and `/pricing` already serves signed-in users as a real upgrade destination too). Live-verified: signed in as the test account, `/` shows no search bar (`find()` for its real placeholder text returned zero matches) while `/dashboard` still has it.

**Job-detail mockup divergence resolved**: Phase 25's single-column Signal mockup vs. the live two-column sticky rail, flagged repeatedly since Phase 25, never reconciled either way. Direct user decision: the live two-column rail is fine as-is. Closed, no rebuild.

**Deploy note**: pushed to Vercel Preview only (`vercel --yes --scope sortie3`, then `vercel alias set` to re-point the stable `jobpilot-experiment-preview-sortie3.vercel.app` alias at it — **now a standing instruction, do this automatically after every future preview deploy**). Not pushed to `origin`, not promoted to production.

**A real correction mid-session, worth remembering**: initially told the user Gemini's smart tier was still blocked on Google Cloud Billing being linked — wrong, stale information from not re-checking `RESUME.md`/`.env` before answering. `609c33a` (Phase 32-ish, already committed) split Gemini into `GEMINI_API_KEY` (billing-linked, paid, smart tier) and `GEMINI_API_KEY_FAST` (free tier), both real keys already in `.env`. **Lesson: verify current `.env`/git log state before asserting something is still blocked, even when a memory or doc claims it is** — the doc was right when written, the world had moved on since.

## Phase 31 — Security fix: closed a real quota-reset bypass on usage_daily/rate_limit

Direct user request after a status check: "how we save ourselves with the VPNs and what about our site's security?" Ran a proactive audit via the `insforge-debug` skill (Platform-authenticated `advisor` scan came back empty/no-scan-yet, so fell back to the documented manual path: `db policies` + `metadata` + direct RLS-enabled checks via `pg_class`).

**Real, verified finding**: `usage_daily` and `rate_limit` — the tables backing this session's entire tier-scaling quota system — let the row owner `UPDATE`/`INSERT` via RLS with no `WITH CHECK` on the value. Since `lib/usage.ts`, `lib/subscription.ts`'s job-evaluation limit check, and `lib/rateLimit.ts` all write these tables using the caller's own session client (confirmed by reading the call sites, not assumed), any signed-in user could call the InsForge REST API directly with their own real JWT and reset their own quota counters to 0, bypassing every metered AI action's cost control app-wide. Confirmed via live exploit replication using the real test account, not just RLS-policy reading.

**Fix, live-verified**: migration `20260829120000_close-usage-quota-bypass.sql` — two `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs (`increment_usage_daily`, `bump_rate_limit`) that resolve the caller from `auth.uid()` and do an atomic check-and-increment server-side, then drop the raw client-writable INSERT/UPDATE policies so direct table writes are rejected outright. All 3 real call sites rewired to use the RPC. Full detail, including a real bug caught mid-fix (the first RPC version referenced a nonexistent `updated_at` column, which would have fail-closed every metered action app-wide — caught via live verification before it could ship) and a second write site found via a broader grep (`lib/subscription.ts`'s job-evaluation check, missed by the first pass), is in `progress-tracker.md`'s newest entry.

**Also audited and confirmed safe, no action needed**: every admin/internal table has RLS enabled with zero policies (default-deny to the anon/authenticated key); the `resumes` bucket is `public: true` but live-tested unauthenticated listing returns 401 (public only means readable-by-known-key, not an open directory); `support-attachments` is correctly private.

**Deliberately not fixed this phase, disclosed to the user, lower severity**: `agent_runs.status` has the same "owner can UPDATE via RLS with no value constraint" shape (a user could directly spoof their own run's status via REST) — but unlike the quota tables, this isn't a cost/revenue bypass (nothing reads `agent_runs.status` to grant additional resource access), and `scraper.actions.ts` writes it from many places across the live search flow, making a safe RPC conversion a larger, riskier refactor than the time this phase had budget for. Worth doing as its own dedicated pass if it comes up again — same RPC pattern would apply.

**VPN/geo-pricing arbitrage question, answered, not built**: explained the inherent spoofability of `x-vercel-ip-country`-based regional pricing (a VPN exiting through India will always see the India price) and that this is an accepted industry-standard tradeoff, not a fixable hole. Offered a non-blocking IP-vs-card-country mismatch logging feature for admin visibility; user chose to skip it for now.

**Commit-splitting note for future reference**: `lib/subscription.ts` and `lib/inngest/functions.ts` both had genuinely intermixed changes from two different efforts (bug fixes + the new feature). `functions.ts` split cleanly by hunk (no overlap). `subscription.ts` didn't — its `resolveProviderForUser()` addition and its `regionalPrices` field addition were adjacent in the same file with no clean hunk boundary, so it was split by temporarily reverting the feature-only lines via `Edit`, committing the reduced bug-fix-only version, then restoring the full content for the feature commit. Worked cleanly, `tsc` re-confirmed clean at both boundaries — a reusable pattern if this comes up again, safer than hand-editing raw patch files.

**What Phase 29 did**: re-read every diff, re-ran `tsc`/`eslint` clean, confirmed the DB migration + Stripe price are real and live, then live-verified the quota→modal→upgrade flow against the real DB (see `progress-tracker.md`'s Phase 29 update on the subscription-tier-scaling entry for the full method). **Real caveat, disclosed**: a genuine browser click-through was attempted and blocked by the Browser pane's `document.hidden`/"0×0 viewport" tooling issue (same bug Phase 24/28 already documented, this time confirmed as a real layout collapse via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not just a paint illusion) — verified instead by calling the real, unmodified quota-check functions directly against the live DB after a fully-reverted temporary test-account mutation (admin status + tier flipped off, usage seeded to the cap, zero real API spend, everything restored and confirmed after). Real output confirmed `reason`/`resetsAt`/`canUpgrade` are all correct. The React click-path itself (button → modal render) is still only code-reviewed, not watched happen in a live browser — worth a real click-through next time the Browser pane issue clears on its own.

**Next session, first steps**: commit the two fixes below (agent_runs status + resolveProvider), ask about pushing/deploying them too (same pattern as the rest of this session). If the Browser pane's `document.hidden` issue has cleared by then, a real click-through of the quota modal (search + "Score this job" on a non-admin/Recon-tier account) is still owed from Phase 29 — low priority, already verified via direct DB/script calls instead.

## Phase 30, cont'd — two real bugs fixed on direct user request (not cosmetic)

**`agent_runs.status` never flipping to `completed`** — the user had flagged this earlier in the session as "cosmetic, not blocking anything" while diagnosing the scoring-delay report; investigating it properly turned up something worse. Root cause: `evaluateJobsAsync`'s final "mark this run completed" write (`lib/inngest/functions.ts`) was plain code after the per-chunk `step.run()` loop, not its own step — a real, confirmed bug, not a guess: found a run (`1115c024…`, from 2026-08-22) with **20/20 jobs genuinely scored** but still sitting at `status='running'` six days later. A DB sweep found **78 total stuck rows** going back to mid-July. Also found and fixed a second, compounding bug while investigating: `app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s Recent Activity query selected/ordered/filtered on `agent_runs.completed_at` — **a column that doesn't exist on the table at all** (only `created_at`/`updated_at`/`started_at` do) — meaning that query has likely returned empty this entire time, silently hiding every "Found N jobs for X" entry from the dashboard. Fixed both: the final status write now happens inside its own `step.run()` (durable, independently retried) wrapped in try/catch so a failure there can never falsely fail an otherwise-successful evaluation batch; the dashboard query now uses `updated_at` (already set at the exact moment status flips, no new column needed). Also added `reconcileStuckAgentRunsAsync`, a new 15-minute Inngest cron (`lib/inngest/functions.ts`, registered in `app/api/inngest/route.ts`) that recomputes "is this run actually done" from the real `jobs` table rather than trusting the write ever landed — a self-healing backstop for this exact failure class, not just a one-time patch. Immediately backfilled the historical data too (same query the cron runs): 5 rows fixed instantly, confirmed zero remaining fully-scored-but-stuck rows via a direct DB check afterward.

**The `resolveProvider()` 2-arg tech debt — turned out to be a real, significant, revenue-affecting bug, not cosmetic debt.** `lib/access.ts`'s `resolveProvider(preferredModel, email, llmUnlocked?)` silently forces free-tier Gemini for every non-admin user whenever `llmUnlocked` is omitted — and a full codebase grep found **all 33 call sites, in every single file**, were using the 2-arg form. This means every paying Command/Ace/Vanguard subscriber has been silently downgraded to Gemini everywhere (résumé/cover-letter generation, bullet rewrites, job evaluation reads, career features, company research, weekly briefings — the entire AI surface of the app) instead of the GPT-4o/Claude access their plan actually promises (`llmUnlocked: true`), since Phase 21 first added the 3-arg signature without anyone actually switching callers over. Fixed centrally, not per-site: new `resolveProviderForUser(insforge, userId, email, preferredModel)` helper in `lib/subscription.ts` (fetches the user's real plan via the existing `getUserSubscription()`, then calls `resolveProvider()` correctly) — every one of the 33 call sites across `actions/` (agent, bragDoc, documents ×4, emailDrafts, jobs ×6, marketReadiness, outcomeInsights, outreachMessage, pipelineStrategy, profile ×4, referralCopy, resumes ×2, skillGapTracking), `app/api/` (agent/research, documents/analyze, documents/chat, documents/generate, resume/generate), and `lib/inngest/functions.ts` (×3, using the admin client) now goes through it. One site (`extractProfileFromBuffer` in `actions/profile.ts`, shared by two callers) needed a signature change to accept `insforge`/`userId` instead of just `userEmail` — its own two call sites (`actions/profile.ts`, `actions/resumes.ts`) updated to match. **Live-verified, not just code-reviewed**: a real script confirmed the exact bug (`resolveProvider('openai', <non-admin email>)` → `"gemini"`) and the exact fix (the real Command-tier test account, temporarily set to `preferred_model: 'openai'`, correctly resolves to `"openai"` via the new helper even for a non-admin email) — reverted the test account's `preferred_model` back to `gemini` immediately after, confirmed via a follow-up query. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across all ~24 touched files, including the admin-client (`createAdminClient`) call sites in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`, which structurally satisfy the same `insforge` shape without any type gymnastics needed.

## Phase 30, cont'd — Country/region-aware pricing, Phase 1 (Stripe-only), built end to end

**Research first, real course-corrections along the way** (all via `agy`, cross-checked when a finding seemed off): first pass concluded Stripe doesn't support Pakistan/Sri Lanka at all — a second, more precise pass corrected this: Paddle/Adyen/**Stripe itself** all accept BUYER-side payments from almost anywhere (only actual sanctioned/embargoed states are hard-blocked), the real friction in South Asia/Nigeria-style markets is each country's OWN bank blocking international/recurring card charges, not the payment processor. Confirmed InsForge's payments abstraction only supports Stripe and Razorpay (checked the actual skill doc folders directly, not just recalled) — Razorpay's standard merchant accounts are India/Malaysia-only, and the cross-border "Import Stack" product that would let Sortie accept Indian buyers via local rails (UPI/RuPay) needs a separate application with Razorpay directly, an external business step, not code. Given that, scoped this to **Phase 1: regional pricing via Stripe alone** (Sortie can already reach almost any buyer through its existing Stripe account) — Razorpay/local-rail payment methods stay a distinct, explicitly deferred Phase 2.

**Went through `EnterPlanMode`** given the real payments/money-handling stakes — a Plan agent's design pass caught a genuine bug before any code was written: `fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()` (the webhook fulfillment trigger) resolves which plan to grant by matching the invoice's Stripe Price ID against ONLY the base `stripe_price_id` column — once a regional Price existed, a renewal on it would silently charge the customer without ever granting the plan. Fixed in the same migration that adds regional pricing.

**Decisions confirmed with the user before building** (asked directly, not assumed): India gets its own real INR price (biggest single market); Pakistan/Sri Lanka/Bangladesh share one discounted-USD band for now (fewer Prices to create/maintain); existing India/Pakistan/Sri Lanka subscribers already on the US price stay grandfathered, no bulk repricing; the product owner sets the actual ₹/$ numbers themselves via the new admin UI once it's live — nothing hardcoded by this work.

**What shipped**: `regional_prices JSONB` column on `subscription_plans` (same "flat per-plan override map" pattern `daily_action_limits` already established) + the webhook-fulfillment fix, migration `20260828190000_add-regional-prices.sql`, **applied live** (confirmed via direct `SELECT` — all 4 plans have a clean empty `{}`). `lib/regionalPricing.ts` (`COUNTRY_REGION_KEY` — a code constant, not admin-editable, same reasoning `DAILY_LIMITS` already established; `resolveStripePriceId`/`resolveDisplayPrice`/`formatPriceCents`), `lib/geo.ts` (`getRequestCountry()` reads Vercel's `x-vercel-ip-country` header directly — confirmed via a Plan-agent-driven codebase read that `proxy.ts`, this app's Next 16 middleware equivalent, doesn't even run on `/pricing`/`/`, so no middleware changes were needed at all). `actions/billing.ts`'s `getPlansForPricing()` now returns a `PricedPlan` (base fields + resolved `displayPriceCents`/`displayCurrency`) and `createCheckoutSessionAction()` independently re-derives the region server-side at charge time — the client only ever sends `tier`, there is no client input to manipulate into claiming a cheaper region. `CTASection.tsx`/`SubscriptionTab.tsx` render the resolved price; `SubscriptionTab.tsx`'s upgrade-vs-downgrade classification deliberately stays keyed on the base `priceCents`, never the regional one, so a discount can't flip that logic. Admin UI: `PlansManager.tsx` gained a `RegionalPricingEditor` (same collapsible-rows pattern as the existing `DailyActionLimitsEditor`) — price/currency/Stripe-Price-ID per region, ships with every region blank.

**Two more real bugs caught and fixed during live verification, not just code review**:
1. **A genuine bait-and-switch risk**: the first version of `resolveStripePriceId()` fell back to the BASE US Stripe Price whenever a region's `stripePriceId` was blank — meaning an admin who sets a display-only regional price ahead of creating its real Stripe Price would show a visitor (say) ₹749 but silently charge the base $15 Price at checkout. Fixed before it ever shipped: a *configured* region with no Price ID now correctly blocks checkout entirely (`resolveStripePriceId` returns `null`) rather than substituting a different price/currency than what was shown.
2. **A camelCase/snake_case key mismatch in the webhook fix itself**: the migration's `fulfill_stripe_subscription_event()` patch initially checked `regional_prices`' nested key as `cfg ->> 'stripe_price_id'` (snake_case, matching top-level column-naming habit) — but `actions/admin.ts` passes `PlanInput.regionalPrices` straight through with no key transformation, so the REAL stored shape is camelCase (`stripePriceId`). The snake_case version would have silently matched nothing against real data, meaning the very bug this whole patch exists to fix would have kept happening for every regional renewal. Caught via a real DB round-trip test (set a fake regional Price ID, ran the exact deployed query, got zero rows; read back the function source, confirmed the key mismatch) — fixed and redeployed, re-verified with a real match.

**Live-verified, not just code-reviewed, end to end**: real DB test data set on the `command` plan (India, ₹749, first with no Stripe Price ID to test the block, then with a fake one to test fulfillment resolution) — `curl -H "x-vercel-ip-country: IN" /pricing` correctly rendered **₹749**; no header and an unmapped country (`DE`) both correctly rendered **$15** unchanged; a direct script confirmed `resolveStripePriceId` returns `null` (blocked) for the no-Price-ID case and the real base Price ID for both the no-region and unmapped-region cases; the corrected webhook query was tested directly against live DB data and correctly resolved the tier. All test data reverted immediately after — confirmed via a final `SELECT` that all 4 plans are back to a clean empty `regional_prices: {}`.

**A real CLI gotcha hit twice this session, worth remembering**: the InsForge CLI's `db query` command chokes on multi-line SQL passed through certain shell paths — not a length limit, a genuine issue with embedded newlines (confirmed by minifying to one line, which fixed it both times). The second time, minifying a freshly-added multi-line `-- comment` block that was indented (so it survived the usual `grep -v '^--'` top-level-comment filter) silently swallowed everything after it once newlines collapsed to spaces, since `--` comments run to end-of-line and there were no more lines. Lesson: when preparing SQL for this CLI, strip ALL comment lines regardless of indentation (`grep -v '^\s*--'`, not `grep -v '^--'`) before minifying, or just don't leave inline comments in code destined for this deploy path.

`tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across every file touched.

**Not committed yet** — the agent_runs fix, the resolveProvider fix, and this whole regional-pricing feature are all on disk, `tsc`/`eslint` clean, DB changes already applied live, ready to commit next.

**Logged mid-session, NOT started — direct user request to discuss once the above is wrapped up.**
2. **Revisit the AI model integration** — which models/providers are wired in now (`lib/models.ts` — currently Gemini/OpenAI/Anthropic per `scripts/verify-models.mjs`) and what the user wants to change. No specifics given yet on what's driving this (cost, quality, a specific provider gap) — surface that question when the conversation actually happens rather than guessing.

**Direct user decision — support-ticket automation PAUSED, staying manual for now.** The Phase 26 plan (a daily GitHub Actions cron auto-picking up `agent_status='ready_for_ai'` tickets and opening PRs — see the historical Phase 26 entry below) is explicitly NOT being built right now. Instead: the user will manually point to specific tickets each day/session, expects them worked and finished promptly, and marked with the right `agent_status` (`ai_in_progress`/`ai_done`) or human `status` as appropriate — same schema already shipped in Phase 26, just driven by the user instead of a cron. **Do not build the automated pipeline unless explicitly asked again** — this is a deliberate pause, not a forgotten task.

**Actually built this session, direct user instruction**: **Global full-text search**, a **persistent site-wide search bar**, and a start on **PWA/mobile polish**. All uncommitted on disk.

**Global full-text search** — real Postgres full-text search, not the old ILIKE title/company-only Cmd+K jump-to (`quickSearchJobs()`, kept as-is, still powers Cmd+K's own dropdown). New trigger-maintained `search_vector` tsvector column (title/company weight A; location/job-type/tags/personal-notes weight B; description/requirements/responsibilities/nice-to-have/benefits/private reflection weight C) + GIN index + a `search_jobs()` Postgres function (ranked via `ts_rank`, snippets via `ts_headline`) — migration `20260828180000_add-full-text-job-search.sql`, applied to the live DB via the same statement-by-statement `db query` workaround the Ace-tier migration needed (the CLI's `db query` parser rejects multi-statement blobs; also had to switch from a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS STORED` column to a `BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE` trigger — Postgres marks two-arg `to_tsvector(regconfig, text)` as STABLE not IMMUTABLE, which a generated-column expression rejects outright, confirmed live). New `actions/jobs.ts` `searchJobsFullText()` (had to cast the RPC's return type rather than use `.returns()`/`.overrideTypes()` — this codebase's untyped InsForge client makes postgrest-js's array-type-checking reject a valid array override, a real SDK-typing quirk worth knowing if another RPC gets added later), a dedicated `/search` page (`components/search/GlobalSearchView.tsx`), and a "Search all jobs for…" cross-link from Cmd+K. **Snippet rendering deliberately does NOT use `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`** — `ts_headline`'s output isn't HTML-safe (the source text is scraped third-party job postings, a real stored-XSS vector if rendered as trusted HTML), so `GlobalSearchView.tsx` string-splits on the literal `<mark>`/`</mark>` markers it chose as `StartSel`/`StopSel` and lets React escape everything else.

**Persistent site-wide search bar** — went through several rounds of direct live correction before landing: first tried inside the Navbar's own pill (rejected — "not on the navbar"), then a per-page hero (rejected in favor of one shared component), then correctly landed on "one shared component, rendered as a SIBLING of `<header>` in `Navbar.tsx`, not a child of it" — a real bug caught from a live screenshot: putting it *inside* the sticky header made it inherit `position: sticky` and follow the page down, overlapping list content below it. Now `components/layout/GlobalSearchBar.tsx` renders after `</header>` closes, at its natural in-flow position, scrolling away normally like any other page content — confirmed live both ways (overlap bug reproduced via screenshot, then fix confirmed via a real `getBoundingClientRect()` scroll test).

**Verification, both features**: real click-through was blocked again by the Browser pane's recurring `document.hidden` tooling issue (this session it manifested as a genuine DOM layout collapse on one dev-server instance — `getBoundingClientRect()` returning real `{0,0,0,0}`, unaffected by disabling CSS transitions or waiting). Worked around by starting this session's OWN fresh dev-server instance (the other one, port 3001, belonging to a different concurrent session, had stopped responding) and driving it via `location.href =` navigation instead of dispatched clicks — this reliably confirmed real ranked search results with real highlighted snippets rendering correctly in an actual browser page load (not just the standalone verification script). The literal keystroke-by-keystroke typing interaction in the search bar itself is still not click-verified — worth doing for real once the Browser pane issue clears.

Then did a real mobile-overflow audit at a genuine 375px viewport (`getBoundingClientRect()`-based detection, not visual guessing) across Dashboard/Missions/Career/Interview/Settings/Resume/Profile/Search — found and fixed one real bug: `app/missions/loading.tsx`'s skeleton had a fixed `w-96` (384px) subtitle placeholder overflowing the 375px viewport; applied the same defensive `max-w-full` fix to `app/career/loading.tsx`'s `w-80` one (not yet overflowing, same fragile pattern). Confirmed via DB query: multiple `agent_runs` from across the whole day were stuck in `status='running'` (several since as early as 14:08 UTC) despite their jobs eventually finishing scoring — meaning the run's OWN status field never gets flipped to `completed` even once, a real minor cosmetic bug worth its own fix later, but not the actual blocker. ## Phase 27 — Apply-link fraud fix closed out, Direct ATS adapters shipped, real search-pipeline bugs fixed, build-plan.md audited

**Started from a direct user report**: two real job apply links resolving to a job-board mirror (`workopolis.com`) and a scam/SEO-farm site (`cajobstoday.janhitjobs.com`). Investigation found the actual fix (trust classifier + lazy re-resolution — `lib/applyLinkTrust.ts`/`lib/reresolveApplyLink.ts`) had already been built earlier the same day by other in-flight work in this same folder; this session verified it live (confirmed a real job self-healed from `workopolis.com` → `ca.indeed.com`) rather than duplicating it.

**User then asked how competitors like JobRight get consistently accurate links, and asked for direct ATS board links.** Live-tested JobRight itself via the real Chrome extension (its own "Original Job Post" link was `careers.cognizant.com` for a company-posted job, LinkedIn only for an agency-posted one) — confirmed the real answer is direct polling of each ATS's own public job-board API, which turned out to be `build-plan.md`'s already-approved, never-built **Phase 8 — Portal Scanner**. Shipped: `lib/atsProviders.ts` (Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby, all 3 verified live before writing code), `target_companies` watchlist table + `/find-jobs/companies` page, `scanTargetCompanies()`/`addTargetCompany()`/etc. in `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts` (which also had its duplicated upsert/eval-quota logic extracted into shared helpers). Live-verified: added Stripe, scanned, got 578 real jobs with direct `stripe.com` links, then reverted the test data.

**Closing the residual gap ("do all 3" per direct request)**: tried adding more ATS platforms (SmartRecruiters/Workable/Recruitee/Breezy) — genuinely dropped after real verification effort, not shipped guessed code (Workable needs per-account auth, the others couldn't be confirmed working). Shipped instead: `components/job-details/ApplyLinkTrustNote.tsx` (a warning note + "Report a bad link" button reusing the existing Feedback ticket system, live-verified), and `functions/apify-job-search.ts` (a new Deno edge function, deployed, using Apify's free `rag-web-browser` Actor as a final fallback tier in the reresolve chain — deliberately stricter acceptance than the other tiers since a real test proved general web search can return true false positives).

**Then the user tested regular search themselves and surfaced 3 real, pre-existing bugs, all found and fixed live, none caused by the work above**:
1. A real search (jobs saved successfully) came back as a full 500 because `inngest.send()` threw uncaught when the local Inngest dev server wasn't running — wrapped in try/catch (`evaluateWithinQuota()`), a failed evaluation-trigger now degrades gracefully instead of failing already-saved jobs.
2. "Logos not showing properly" — first hypothesis (dev-only Fast Refresh artifact from this session's own many edits) confirmed and separate from the real issue the user pushed back on: `CompanyLogo.tsx`'s naive company-NAME-to-domain guess is wrong for most real companies (`"Royal Bank of Canada"` → `royalbankofcanada.com`, doesn't exist; real domain is `rbc.com`). Fixed with `lib/applyLinkTrust.ts`'s new `extractLikelyLogoDomain()` — reuses the job's own trust-classified apply-link domain instead of guessing from the name, wired into all 6 real render sites. Live-verified: RBC's real logo now loads.
3. "Back to jobs" reverting to an older search — `app/find-jobs/page.tsx`'s "last run" query excluded `status = 'running'` runs on the theory only "completed" meant real/finished, which is backwards (jobs save synchronously before async AI scoring even starts) — this was hiding the search the user just ran and silently falling back to an older one, the exact regression the query was written to prevent. Fixed by dropping the status filter. Verified surgically (test account hit its real 5/day search quota from this session's own testing, so a fresh end-to-end click-through wasn't possible — flipped a real run's status and confirmed the query still picked it up correctly).

**Then audited `build-plan.md`'s entire Master Feature Inventory (sections A–S) against real RESUME.md history and live codebase checks**, per direct user request ("do check it again"). Found and corrected ~10 real stale markers (Admin Panel's whole section header said "not yet built" despite being substantially shipped across Phases 21-26; negotiation scripts, 90-day plan, Google One Tap, job comparison view, detail drawer, and the Chrome extension row were all marked unbuilt despite being real; found and removed 2 literal duplicate contradictory rows). Confirmed genuinely still open: structured job APIs beyond SerpApi/ATS (TheirStack/Serper), DOCX/résumé export, résumé version-manager backend, LinkedIn OAuth, split contact fields, EEOC fields, general bulk multi-select on saved/Kanban jobs, global full-text search, profile completeness meter, PWA/mobile polish, accessibility pass, B2B/white-label, the Live-Fire voice interview simulator.

Deliberately did NOT "fix" the Navigator FAB overlapping scrolled-past dashboard content — that's standard fixed-FAB behavior (same as any Intercom-style launcher), not a defect.

Then a direct user report that the horizontal-scroll tab strip gave mobile users "no idea" there were more tabs — `Tabs.tsx` now measures real overflow and shows an edge fade + tap-to-scroll chevrons only on the side that actually has more content (user explicitly chose "add arrow buttons" over "wrap to multiple rows" or "fade alone is enough" via a direct question).
4. **Feedback/Bug-Report system, Phase 1 of a 3-phase plan** (`C:\Users\ridha\.claude\plans\atomic-churning-giraffe.md`) — the user wants non-technical test users to file bugs/feature requests/change requests/feedback with screenshots, triaged by an owner/admin into a "Ready for AI" queue that a future session works through on request (explicitly NOT autonomous polling — "you can pick up when I say so"). Researched via `agy` against Canny/Featurebase/Instabug/Linear/Sentry patterns first (`C:\Users\ridha\.gemini\antigravity-cli\brain\097de192-c2d1-4a32-b114-99bf4168b476\sortie_internal_systems_design.md`). **Deliberately extends the existing `support_tickets` system (migration `20260819090000`) rather than building a parallel one** — checked for existing overlap before designing anything, per this project's own hard-won "check existing state before building" lesson. Full technical detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s newest entry — summary: `category` (bug/feature_request/change_request/feedback/billing/support — billing added mid-build per direct request) + `agent_status` (none/ready_for_ai/ai_in_progress/ai_done, orthogonal to the existing human open/pending/resolved status) + `ops_note` on tickets; `image_urls text[]` per message; new private `support-attachments` bucket (confirmed via the `insforge` skill docs that InsForge's auto-installed owner-only storage RLS + admin bypass already covers this correctly, no custom RLS needed); `profiles.is_tester` gates the richer submission form for real users ahead of full rollout (owners/admins get it automatically via their existing `admin_users` role — confirmed live, not assumed). Also fixed two real, disclosed bugs along the way: the pricing-card button misalignment (`CTASection.tsx`) and Google OAuth failing on the Vercel preview (missing `allowed_redirect_urls` entry — no stable preview alias existed, so one was created).

**Phase 3 (the PR-gated daily-cron automation pipeline) — schema/UI groundwork shipped, the cloud piece itself deliberately NOT built.** `agent_status` gained a real `pr_open` value (between `ready_for_ai`/`ai_in_progress` and the human-only `ai_done`), migration applied, admin UI updated. Went to actually create the scheduled cloud routine (via the `schedule` skill / `RemoteTrigger`) and hit a real, structural blocker: `feature/signal-redesign` had never been pushed to GitHub (pushed it this session, `git remote -v` confirms `origin/feature/signal-redesign` now exists) — but even with that fixed, a cloud routine runs in an isolated sandbox with **no access to local env vars or `.insforge/project.json`** (both deliberately gitignored), and there's no InsForge MCP connector connected. The only way to give the routine real DB access right now would be pasting the raw `INSFORGE_API_KEY` (a full-access admin credential) into the routine's prompt text — confirmed via the tool's own docs that run logs are retrievable, so that's not "server-only" storage, it's a real credential-exposure risk. **Given a direct choice, the user chose to hold off** rather than embed the key. `CronCreate` (this session's own local scheduling tool) was also checked and ruled out separately — it's session-only, dies when the session ends, and auto-expires after 7 days regardless, not durable enough for a persistent daily job either way.

**Real path forward, confirmed same session — this is where Phase 3 resumes**: searched the MCP registry directly (`search_mcp_registry`, keywords "insforge"/"database"/"postgres backend") — zero results, InsForge has no MCP connector today, so that path is genuinely off the table, not just untried. The actual answer: **GitHub Actions, not a Claude cloud routine.** The repo is on GitHub now (pushed this session), so a scheduled workflow using GitHub's own encrypted repo secrets (never exposed in logs, unlike a routine prompt) is the standard, secure mechanism here. Concretely:
1. User adds two GitHub repo secrets: `INSFORGE_API_KEY` (already in local `.env`) and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
2. Build `.github/workflows/feedback-pipeline.yml` — daily cron (e.g. `0 13 * * *` ≈ 9am Toronto), which queries `support_tickets` where `agent_status = 'ready_for_ai'` using the InsForge secret, then for each one runs Claude Code non-interactively (`claude -p "..." --dangerously-skip-permissions` — safe here, isolated CI runner not a shared machine) with a self-contained prompt built from the ticket's subject/body/ops_note/screenshots, has it implement the fix in a new branch, verify with `tsc`/`eslint`, `gh pr create`, then post an admin reply linking the PR and set `agent_status = 'pr_open'`.

Presented this plan directly; **user said "we will continue this later"** — nothing built yet beyond this plan itself. Start here next time, not by re-deriving the InsForge-MCP dead end.

**Start here on the next session**: the user asked for a site-wide `/impeccable polish` pass as the very next thing — start there. Real ground covered this session, in order:

1. **Job-detail page, every tab** — Overview's "The Role" section merged from 5 same-style boxes into one card with internal dividers (same fix pattern reused for the new Tracking tab, which merged 4 boxes into one). The 10-dimension evaluation block was redesigned twice on direct live feedback: first into a divided list with a colorful per-dimension distribution strip and neutral icon chips (only the grade badge carries color — the doubled-up solid-color-block look was the original "cheap" complaint), then made each row clickable, opening a **position-tracked popover** anchored to the clicked row (not a centered modal — that was a real live bug report, fixed the same session). `JobDescription.tsx` gained a "Show full description" toggle — `about_role` is a deliberate AI-cleaned 2-4 sentence summary (see `lib/evaluator.ts`'s own comment), not the full posting; the toggle reveals the real `jobs.description` on demand rather than replacing the summary. `ResumeFitSection`'s Model/Theme selectors were removed (Model dropped entirely — no more per-job override, always the account default) and Theme moved to `DocumentGenerator`'s own header, where it's actually relevant. `DocumentSwitcher.tsx` (résumé ⇄ cover-letter cross-link) no longer redirects to job-details when the other document doesn't exist yet — it opens a `ConfirmDialog` offering to generate it in place (`ConfirmDialog` gained a reusable `error` prop for this). New shared primitives: `AnimatedScoreValue.tsx` (count-up, respects reduced-motion), `AiReadsCard.tsx` gained a `style` passthrough for staggering.
2. **A real, app-wide dark-mode bug found and fixed everywhere**: `bg-accent-muted`/`bg-accent-light` resolve to a muddy near-black brown (`#2c1a08`/`#451a03`) in dark mode — fine as a badge wash, broken as a "selected/active" indicator. Fixed in ~17 places across `EvaluationBreakdown`, the résumé/cover-letter workspaces' tab switchers, `StyleTab`, `DocumentSwitcher`, `QuestionBankPanel`, `PracticeSandbox`, `FilterBar`, `MissionsFilterBar`, `CommandPalette`, `SubscriptionTab`, `LimitReachedModal`, `OnboardingWizard`, `ResumeManager`, `ResumeAnalysisView` — always the same fix, `bg-accent/15` (a live opacity-mix of the real token, not a separately-hardcoded dark value).
3. **Résumé/cover-letter editor workspaces** — `ResumeWorkspace`/`CoverLetterWorkspace`/`ResumeSlotWorkspace`'s internal tab switchers and several flat-wash "strap" notes redesigned to match the rest of the app. Fit-score gauge and ATS-score badge redesigned with tone-aware color and real grow-in/count-up animation. Tab switches now crossfade (keyed remount) instead of snapping.
4. **Missions page** — `KanbanCard`/`UpcomingDeadlines` got entrance stagger; drag-and-drop reconfirmed live to still work after the animation additions (dnd-kit's own inline `transform`/`transition` only apply during an active drag, so a mount-only CSS entrance animation doesn't conflict — verified, not assumed).
5. **Interview page — full restructure, not just polish**, on a direct report that users didn't know a live code editor existed. Root cause: reaching it took 6 clicks (search → open a question → scroll past hints and a spoiler reference solution → find a small button). Ran a real `agy` research pass (LeetCode/AlgoExpert/Pramp/Educative patterns) before rebuilding: the question list now shows a "Code editor" badge directly on technical rows; the study view now opens the editor as the FIRST thing shown (hints/reference solution demoted below a divider — also fixes a real spoiler-before-practice ordering bug); the page header gained a capability strip (Question Bank / Live Code Editor / STAR Stories) so the features are discoverable before any interaction; the Question Bank's blank search form was replaced with a real company-card grid built from the user's own tracked jobs (real logos/titles, one click straight to results — the manual form is now a secondary "search a different company" fallback). `QuestionBankPanel` API changed: `suggestedCompanies` never shipped, went straight to `quickStartJobs: {company,title,logoUrl}[]`.
6. **Marketing homepage** — was the one surface with zero animation anywhere; added real entrance/hover motion throughout (`Hero`, `SuccessStory`, `BentoFeatures`, `AntiAutoApply`, `TheLifecycle`, `CTASection`). Hero's evaluator preview card was rebuilt twice on live feedback: first because it had drifted from the real, currently-live `EvaluationBreakdown.tsx` (was still the old grid-of-badges shape), second because its "AI Navigator reads" card was still on the `compact` tier while every other instance in the app had already been upgraded to `hero`. New `FullToolkit.tsx` section lists real shipped features that had no homepage mention at all — the interview page's question bank/code editor/STAR builder, company research dossiers, the offer/negotiation toolkit, the weekly briefing.
7. **One real, disclosed data side effect**: verifying Kanban drag-and-drop didn't break involved a live drag on the test account's real Amazon job, which — per this app's own design — permanently logs an `application_events` "applied" row even after dragging back. User was asked and chose to leave it (`Applied just now` will show in that job's Tracking-tab history until manually cleared).

**Still open, carried forward**: the "known divergence from the mockup" question below (single-column vs. the live two-column sticky rail) was never actually resolved — Phase 25 was told explicitly to keep the rail and redesign around it, which is what happened, but the underlying mockup-vs-live question is still unreconciled if a future session wants to revisit it. `npm run build` has still never been run on this branch. Missions/Career/Settings still haven't had the same close design pass job-detail/job-search/interview/homepage got this session and Phase 25 — worth a read-only check before assuming they're at the same bar.

### Real, recurring gotcha this session: Turbopack's dev-server CSS cache went stale repeatedly

At least 4 separate times, an edit to `app/globals.css` (or a new class added there) did not show up live even after the page reloaded — the served CSS chunk kept its old hash and old content. Each time this was confirmed, not assumed: `curl` the served `/_next/static/chunks/[root-of-the-server]*.css` chunk and grep for the new class name — if it's not there, the code is right and the server is stale. It always cleared on its own eventually (a `npm run dev` restart forces it immediately). Don't trust a "nothing changed" visual result without checking the served CSS first — several rounds of user-reported "this doesn't look different" this session turned out to be this, not a real code problem.

### Known divergence from the approved Signal mockup — flagged, not reconciled

The mockup's own `page-jobdetail` section (read mid-session, hadn't been checked before) is a **single-column** layout: a small verdict pill, then title+meta+Save/Apply together in one compact row, then a 4-tab strip (Overview/Company/Offer Tools/Interview Prep) directly below — no sidebar at all. What's actually live now is a **two-column layout with a sticky identity rail** (`JobIdentityRail.tsx`) on the right, 5-6 tabs (Overview/Company/**Resume Evolution**/**Tracking**/Interview Prep-conditional/Offer Tools), built earlier this session before the mockup's job-detail section had been read. The user's subsequent direction (icon+title section headers, the Resume Evolution/Tracking tab splits, "keep it in fragments") was layered on top of the sticky-rail structure, not a rewrite to match the mockup's single column — so the current shape is deliberate-by-continuation, not deliberate-by-comparison. Next session should either get explicit confirmation the two-column rail is preferred over the mockup's single-column, or treat reconciling them as a real fragment of its own. Don't silently assume either is "correct."

### What shipped this session, in order

1. **Navigator FAB fixed site-wide** — `.btn-signal` (from Phase 24's first pass) declared `position: relative`, which silently beat the FAB's own `fixed` (both unlayered, same specificity, source order decides — `.btn-signal` comes later in the file). The launcher was computing to `left:-24px, bottom:896px` — off-screen on every authenticated page. Fixed by removing `position` from `.btn-signal` entirely (verified: no call site needed it — the FAB's own `fixed` supplies the containing block for its icons). Verified live: `position: fixed`, on-screen, clickable.
2. **Dashboard retheme** — heatmap cells (`ActivityHeatmap.tsx`) were `bg-info` blue, now agent-teal and sized via `repeat(N,1fr)`+`aspect-ratio` instead of a fixed 12px square (was "too small", now fills the card). Match-distribution bars (`AnalyticsCharts.tsx`) were `--color-success` green, now agent-teal (AI-scored data). Action Center (`AIActionCenter.tsx`) was two tinted-card styles including info-blue, now neutral `.signal-track` rows with an amber warm-pulse dot. Recent Activity (`RecentActivity.tsx`) lost its green/blue pulse dots in an early pass, then got them back per direct user correction — restored as a single agent-teal `.signal-pulse-dot`/`.signal-pulse-dot-hollow` pair, **pulsing only on row hover** (a second correction — first version pulsed ambiently, user wanted hover-only). Pipeline funnel (`PipelineFunnel.tsx`) now fills from zero on load via a pure-CSS `--fill` custom property + `.signal-fill-in` keyframe (no JS — the funnel and the settings seat-meter both render in server components, so a `useEffect`-based animation wasn't an option).
3. **Toasts migrated to Sonner**, rendered headless via `toast.custom()` (`components/ui/ToastProvider.tsx`) — token-styled `.signal-toast`/`.signal-toast-agent` instead of fighting Sonner's injected CSS with `!important`. `useToast().showToast()` kept as the public API so the two existing call sites (`AddToCompareButton`, `ShareJobLink`) needed zero edits.
4. **`AiThinkingCard`/`SignalProgressBar`** (`components/ui/SignalLoaders.tsx`) built and, after explicit user approval over the original mockup's own loaders (compared side-by-side on `/preview/loaders`), wired into 6 real AI flows that previously showed only button-text changes: Pipeline Strategy, Skill Gaps, Market Readiness, Outcome Insights, Negotiation Script, 90-Day Plan.
5. **`ConfirmDialog` restyled** to the mockup's modal (blur-in scrim, lift-in card, serif title) with a new `tone="neutral" | "danger"` prop — danger (red, default) is unchanged for all 20 existing destructive-action call sites; neutral (amber `.btn-signal` + check-in-a-puck) is for genuinely reversible actions, applied to `SubscriptionTab`'s downgrade confirm (was wrongly `danger` before — downgrading isn't destructive).
6. **Job-detail page redesigned in fragments**, per explicit "small pieces, not all at once" direction:
   - `JobIdentityRail.tsx` (sticky, right column) — title/score/salary/location/type/found/Apply, so the primary action never scrolls away. `JobActionBar.tsx` lost its own Apply button (was duplicated) and the now-unused `applyUrl`/`company` props.
   - Overview tab split into three-to-four sections (Fit & Evaluation / The Role / Offer & Negotiation-conditional / Network), each with a `SectionHeader.tsx` icon-chip+title (this app's own established Career-page pattern — **not** a new jump-nav pill row, which was tried, explicitly rejected as decorative/redundant, and fully removed — zero `signal-jump-nav` references remain).
   - New **Tracking** tab: `ApplicationHistory`/`JobTagsAndNotes`/`WhyILeftReflection`/`JobDeadline` — pulled off the top of the page per direct request (personal tracking data, not job content, shouldn't lead).
   - New **Resume Evolution** tab: `ResumeFitSection`/`DocumentGenerator`/`EmailDrafts` — pulled off the bottom of the page (previously the literal last thing on a 40+-component page, easy to miss).
   - Deleted `JobInfo.tsx` and `FloatingApplyButton.tsx` — both fully orphaned once the rail replaced them (confirmed via grep before deleting, not assumed).
7. **`AiReadsCard.tsx`** extracted as the one shared "AI Navigator reads" component, two tiers (`variant="hero"` full `.ai-hero-card`, `variant="compact"` the mockup's `.ai-mini` tint) — replaces ~24 files' worth of hand-rolled `border-l-2 border-agent bg-agent-light` blocks.
8. **Job-search page + `JobResultCard.tsx` redesigned** — search box was below the fold on load (two duplicate page headers + a full Recently Viewed strip above it); now one slim eyebrow line, a compacted "Run a sortie" console, Recently Viewed moved below the search bar. Nav labels fixed: `Recommended`→`Search`, `Liked`→`Saved` (the old labels promised something the destination didn't deliver). `JobResultCard` rebuilt as a dense row (mockup's `.job-row`) instead of a big padded card:
   - Match score enlarged to 28px with a column divider (direct reference match), Eye/Heart/Ban action icons moved to sit *below* the score in the same column, given one shared `text-accent/70` resting color (user was explicit: "a color", singular — not three different hues, which would just reopen the blending problem this whole pass was meant to fix).
   - Meta-row icons (briefcase/trending/dollar, amber/blue/green) were **removed then restored** — a real self-correction. The "colors blending" complaint was about accent-amber being reused for four unrelated meanings on one card (location pin, job-type icon, user tags, primary-CTA color, all at once), not about these three meta icons, which read as distinct and legible in practice. Don't re-remove them without a specific new complaint about them by name.
   - "More options" dropdown converted from a plain absolute child to a **portaled, position-tracked panel** (`MoreOptionsMenu`, mirrors `JobActionBar.tsx`'s `StatusMenuPanel` exactly) — a real bug, not cosmetic: once `overflow-hidden` was removed from the card (itself a fix for the dropdown being clipped), the dropdown had nothing stopping it from painting over the *next* card down in the list. Verified live with a real mouse click (a scripted `.click()` call gave a false-positive "this navigates the page" result — don't trust synthetic clicks for this kind of test, always confirm with a real click before reporting a click-handler bug).
9. **`FindJobsForm`'s "Run a sortie" console decoupled from the `overlay` tokens** — those are deliberately fixed-dark-in-both-themes (Navbar chrome), and per direct instruction the Navbar keeps that; the console card was the one thing wrongly sharing those tokens, so it stayed a dark card in light mode. Now plain `bg-surface`/`text-text-primary` tokens, which already flip correctly with the site theme. `FilterBar.tsx`'s pills (rendered inside the same visual card) got the same swap. Verified in both themes live.

### Real bugs found and fixed along the way (not the main ask, caught while verifying)

- `SubscriptionTab.tsx`'s plan-tag badge (`Current plan` / `Limited — N seats`) was in a `justify-between` row with the plan name — wrapped into an unreadable blob at the card's actual width. Moved to its own line above the name (mockup's real placement), confirmed live.
- The dashboard funnel/settings seat-meter both used to animate via `width`, a real layout-thrash the design-review hook flagged — switched to `transform: scaleX()` (mockup and this app's two other meters did the same); the `--fill` custom-property trick above builds the load-in animation on top of that without JS.

Copied them all from local `.env`/`.env.local` into Vercel's Preview scope via `vercel env add ... preview` (values never printed to any tool output). `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` keys need `--no-sensitive` not `--sensitive` — Vercel rejects sensitive-visibility on public-prefixed vars with a real API error, confirmed live not guessed. 4 keys were missing locally and skipped (`BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID`, `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY`) — harmless for UAT purposes, worth filling in before this Preview scope is relied on for anything Browserbase/PostHog-dependent.

### What actually shipped (`tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`npm run build` all clean throughout; live-verified via the real dev server + real test account across `/`, `/login`, `/dashboard`, `/find-jobs`, `/find-jobs/[id]`, `/career`, `/missions`, `/settings`, `/profile`, `/`)

**Token + font foundation** (`app/globals.css`, `app/layout.tsx`) — the highest-leverage change: `--color-accent`/`--color-agent` shifted to the Signal amber/teal pair (`#F59E0B`/`#D97706` accent, `#2DD4BF`/`#0F766E` agent, dark/light respectively), every tint/shade neighbor re-derived from Tailwind's own amber/teal scale rather than hand-picked. Because every component already reads colors as CSS variables (an invariant this codebase already enforced), this single change re-colored buttons, badges, grade pills, and every Agent-Content Callout site-wide with zero component edits. Inter + Fraunces self-hosted via `next/font/google` (`--font-sans`/`--font-display`), replacing the mockup's runtime `@import` — real system-stack fallback if the webfont fails.

**New reusable primitives in `globals.css`** (documented in `context/ui-registry.md` — read that before building anything new that might want them): `.btn-signal` (gradient fill + layered inset/outer glow + real press/hover feedback, using new `--ease-out`/`--ease-in-out` theme tokens), `.ai-hero-card` + `.ai-eyebrow-dot` (agent-teal radial hero callout with a pulsing dot — for the rare genuinely page-leading AI moment, NOT a replacement for the small inline Agent-Content Callout used everywhere else, which is untouched and still correct). Every `color-mix()`-bearing property is isolated into its own single-property rule per this file's own 4×-confirmed Lightning CSS gotcha.

**`.btn-signal` applied exhaustively across every genuinely-primary CTA on the consumer product** — confirmed via repeated fresh grep sweeps that nothing real was missed. Covers: homepage (Hero, both CTASection pricing CTAs), auth (`EmailPasswordForm`), onboarding (all 4 step buttons), find-jobs (search button, `JobActionBar`'s "Apply at {company}" — the single highest-traffic button on the job-detail page), the Navigator FAB (floating AI launcher, visible on every authenticated page), dashboard (`PipelineStrategyCard`), career (`BragDocGenerator`/`MarketReadiness`/`SkillGapTracker`), interview (`QuestionBankPanel`/`StarStoryMatrix`/`InterviewDebrief`/`InterviewPanel`/`PracticeSandbox`), documents (Resume/Cover-Letter/Resume-slot Download, `AIRewriteTab`, `DocumentGenerator`), job-details (`InsiderConnectionsButton`, `ResumeGapAnalysis`, `CompanyResearch`, `FloatingApplyButton`, `AddToCompareButton`, `JobDetailDrawer`), profile (`SectionModal`'s Save — shared across every profile-editing modal, `ResumeManager`, `ResumeAnalysisView`, `ConnectedAccounts`, `ResumeSection`), settings (`SubscriptionTab`, every integration tab: Notion/Google Calendar/Outlook Calendar/Push/Referrals/Support), the public `/ats-checker` tool, `NetworkSignals`, `LimitReachedModal`, `ExternalJobForm`. **Deliberately excluded**: admin/back-office CRUD forms (`PushBroadcastForm`, `BroadcastEditor`, `ExpensesDashboard`, `TeamRoster`, `UsersTable`, etc. — the premium treatment is for the consumer product, not internal tooling) and small per-item repeated actions (`EditorTab`'s per-bullet rewrite, `OnboardingWizard`'s step-indicator dots) where it would just be visual noise.

**`WeeklyBriefingCard`** upgraded from the plain inline callout to the new `.ai-hero-card` — the one real page-leading AI moment on the dashboard. **`EvaluationBreakdown`**'s overall-grade box got the agent-teal tint (it's AI output, same invariant as the per-dimension grades) plus a real staggered entrance on the 10-dimension grid (transform+opacity, 280ms, 40ms/card, reduced-motion respected — fires once per page load, not per-interaction). **`KanbanCard.tsx`** had a dead `glass-panel` class reference (retired 2026-07-27, silent pre-existing bit-rot, not introduced this session) replaced with the real `card-interactive-glow` hover-lift already proven on `JobResultCard`. **`font-display` (Fraunces)** applied to every real page-title `h1` that lacked one — Missions, Resume, Career Record, Interview, External, Saved Jobs, Settings, Pricing, Methodology, the job-detail page's job title (`JobInfo.tsx`), and Find Jobs' "Run a sortie" hero heading, the single most-requested visible fix (direct user feedback that Find Jobs/job-detail "didn't look changed").

**Design-skill self-review** (`animate`/`emil-design-eng` skills): found and fixed one real defect — `.btn-signal`'s hover transition was using the weak built-in `ease-in-out` in the wrong bucket (a hover-lift is a discrete state-entry, wants `ease-out`); added real `--ease-out`/`--ease-in-out` @theme tokens and fixed it. Deliberately did **not** touch `card-interactive-glow`'s matching-looking `ease-in-out`, because its own comment shows that value was reference-researched against Raycast's real measured site in an earlier phase — a generic skill heuristic loses to a researched decision, doesn't override it.

**Two real design-scope calls, not deferrals**: (1) Kanban/Inbox's structural "rail-card" visual language from the mockups was deliberately NOT built — real `@dnd-kit` drag logic makes distinct bordered drag targets the likely-correct UX for a real interactive board, unlike a static mockup; this is a considered decision, revisit only with real user testing, not by default. (2) Settings was assumed mid-session to need a tab-rail restructure — it doesn't, `SettingsPanel.tsx` already had one (`NAV` array + sidebar grid), already token-driven, already fine.

**impeccable design hook**: 9 `side-tab` findings, all traced individually to the pre-existing, documented Agent-Content Callout pattern (or its error-toned twin), not new AI-slop — narrow per-file ignores added to `.impeccable/config.json` (git-tracked) with real reasons, not blanket-suppressed.

**Honest verification-tier note**: the Kanban board and Inbox table specifically were never screenshot-confirmed — this session's browser-automation tooling intermittently reported a `0×0` viewport (confirmed via `window.innerWidth` returning `0` — a tooling/display-compositing quirk, not an app bug: `document.querySelectorAll` still found real DOM content with real data at the same moments `innerText`/`getBoundingClientRect` read empty). Cross-checked instead via `curl` with a real session cookie and via the same test account's real Inbox tab (128 real jobs) rendering correctly once checked directly.

## Phase 23 — Design-overhaul: researched, mocked, converged on "Signal" — NOT implemented yet

**Direct user request, continued from Phase 22's parking note**: a mid-turn mega-brief asking for a full design-system overhaul (new colors, 21st.dev components, animations, loaders, "premium feel"). Correctly did NOT build any of it live into the app first — real research, then mockups, then iterated on user feedback across several rounds, entirely in an Artifact. Full blow-by-blow is in this session's transcript; this is the fast-orientation summary of what to trust going in.

**Research, in order** (don't re-do this — it's real and still valid):
1. Live-audited the actual running app first (`getComputedStyle()` on real dashboard cards) — found `--shadow-card` is genuinely invisible on dark surfaces, confirmed **deliberate** (see `app/globals.css`'s own comment: elevation comes from the background/surface/border gray-step difference, matching Linear/Raycast, not from shadows — a real prior decision, not a bug). Also found **zero real webfont loaded anywhere** (`next/font` unused in `app/layout.tsx` despite `--font-display` implying one exists) — this is a real, still-unfixed gap.
2. First research pass anchored on Raycast (a dev-tool reference, since Sortie's existing dark neutral ramp was literally pulled from Raycast's real site in an earlier phase) — **the user correctly rejected this**: Sortie is a professional AI job-search tool, not a dev tool, and referencing Raycast pulls it toward the wrong category entirely.
3. Re-ran research via `agy` targeted at the right reference class (Teal/Huntr/Otta, Notion AI/Perplexity/Superhuman, Mercury/Ramp) — full report saved at `C:\Users\ridha\.gemini\antigravity-cli\brain\82947cd8-e764-4e45-86fc-163060c56259\premium_design_research.md`. Verdict: layer clinical (data-dense core UI) with warm (brand/AI-voice/win-moments), use accent sparingly, avoid generic SaaS blue and the purple/blue "AI-gradient" cliché.
4. Cross-checked against `ui-ux-pro-max`'s local palette/font-pairing dataset (independent source, not web research) — **confirmed the same finding twice**: its own "AI product" archetypes default to purple, reinforcing that Sortie's teal Agent-color is a real differentiator worth keeping, not a weakness. Its best SaaS-category font match was **Calistoga + Inter**, not the Fraunces pairing first proposed — worth knowing the mockup's current headline face (Fraunces) was a judgment call the data didn't fully back, if a next session wants to swap it.
5. Installed real 3rd-party design skills the user asked for by name: `emilkowalski/skills` (12 skills — `animate`, `ask-sonner`, `emil-design-eng`, `review-animations`, etc., genuinely authoritative — used `animate`'s real easing/duration/frequency-gate specs throughout the mockup) and `Leonxlnx/taste-skill` (13 skills, mixed quality — `high-end-visual-design` specifically was **not** used as-instructed since its landing-page mandates (banned Inter, heavy blur/purple-glow bento grids) directly contradict Sortie's own already-researched, evidence-based decisions; `redesign-existing-projects` was genuinely useful and drove real fixes). Also installed `npx impeccable install` (a comprehensive design skill+hook, `/impeccable init` never run — real setup step still open if a future session wants its PRODUCT.md-driven workflow).

**Mockup iteration** (all in the one Artifact URL above, all in one file `sortie-design-mocks.html` under this session's now-gone scratchpad — the URL is what persists, not the local path):
- v1: 3 mocks, same DOM/layout, just swapped color/font tokens — **user rejected as "barely a redesign."**
- v2: 3 *structurally* distinct redesigns (different layout, hierarchy, card patterns per direction) — Cockpit (dense Ramp/Linear Operate-mode), Quiet (Mercury quiet-luxury), Signal (AI-insight-leads, warm-recast colors). User picked **Signal**, but flagged the buttons as "very plain" — turned out to be a real bug (the button CSS had been dropped entirely in a rewrite, not just under-designed).
- v3: fixed buttons with real depth (gradient fill, inner highlight, tinted glow, nested nudge icon), added a live progress bar + an "AI thinking" indeterminate loader (recreates the real `GenerationProgress.tsx` pattern), ambient hero glow, hover states throughout.
- v4: user caught a real design-system-correctness bug — the "AI Navigator reads" evidence badges (Shortlisted/Interviewing/score) all used one invented teal color. Fixed by pulling the **real** per-stage colors from `lib/applicationStatus.ts`'s `STATUS_CLASSES` (shortlisted = muted, interviewing = agent-teal — genuinely teal in the real app too) and giving scores their own treatment matching `JobResultCard.tsx`'s real `scoreTierClass` tiering, instead of a third invented pill color.
- v5 (current): user converged fully on Signal — dropped Cockpit/Quiet from the mockup entirely, expanded Signal into 5 real pages (Dashboard, **Missions** — the real Kanban recast as rail-columns, Inbox, **Settings** — the real Recon/Command/Vanguard plan cards from Phase 22's actual Vanguard work, and a Components library with a real dropdown menu + modal added).
