## Phase 22 — Vanguard lifetime tier + Inbox/Pipeline split, both shipped and deployed

**Two direct user requests, same session**: "implement other subscriptions" (clarified via a follow-up question to mean a new tier, not Razorpay) — the user then pasted a full spec mid-turn for a $149 one-time "Vanguard" lifetime tier capped at 250 seats, race-condition-safe. Separately, "also that draft thing" — the Draft/Pipeline-inflation bug flagged at the end of Phase 21 (see the historical entry below), which turned out to need the full `agy`-researched Inbox/Pipeline structural split, not just the narrower stale-listing filter fix first attempted. Full technical detail for both in `progress-tracker.md`'s two newest entries — this is the fast-orientation summary.

**Vanguard**: `subscription_plans` gained `max_seats`/`seats_claimed` + a `lifetime` billing period (generic — any future plan can be scarcity-capped, not hardcoded to this one tier). A real Stripe one-time Price created via the CLI, seeded with Command's exact limits so the existing plan-driven circuit breakers needed zero new logic. The real overselling guard is a race-safe `claim_plan_seat()` (atomic row-locked UPDATE) run at Stripe *fulfillment* via a new `checkout.session.completed`(mode=payment) trigger — not the pre-checkout seat check, which is only a best-effort UX nicety. Pricing/Settings UI generalized to render every real paid plan (was hardcoded to one), with a scarcity progress bar + Sold Out lockdown. A new daily cron rolls each lifetime holder's usage period forward since there's no recurring Stripe invoice to do that automatically.

**Inbox/Pipeline split**: `jobs.application_status` gained `inbox` (new DB default, pre-pipeline, hidden from the Kanban) and `shortlisted` (the real first Kanban stage). **Direct user decision**: all 1,315 existing `draft` jobs were backfilled straight into `inbox`. New `InboxTable.tsx` — a dense table (not `JobResultCard`-per-row), its own bulk Shortlist/Archive, wired into Missions as a third Board/List/Inbox tab. A new daily cron auto-archives any Inbox job still untouched after 14 days. Every downstream consumer (career timeline/export, outcome insights, the job detail page's status dropdown, the dashboard Pipeline Funnel and AI Pipeline Strategy Read) updated to the two-stage split.

**Both live-verified end to end on real production data**, including a real Stripe test-mode Checkout Session confirmed showing the correct product/price (payment not completed — that would've consumed a real seat), and a real Inbox→Shortlist round trip watched live on the dashboard (the funnel went from a fake "Draft: 99" bottleneck to a real "Shortlisted: 1"). `tsc`/`eslint`/`npm run build` all clean throughout. Committed as `5f3fe38`, pushed, deployed — see the session-status line above.

**Real, disclosed follow-ups, not done this session**: the ~17 `resolveProvider()` 2-arg call sites flagged back in Phase 21 are still unchanged. Razorpay is still parked. The design-system-overhaul conversation the user opened mid-session (full dashboard/token rewrite, 21st.dev components) was deliberately deferred to its own session per direct agreement — see that exchange earlier in this session's transcript before picking it back up; don't re-litigate the sequencing question, just pick up where it left off (whether the existing teal `--color-agent` system gets extended or replaced was left as "let's see" — get a real answer before building).

## Phase 21, cont'd — loading-state redesign attempted, reverted — real redesign needed next session

**Direct user request, same session**: after the monetization/Stripe work shipped, asked for loaders to be "part of the design of the site" for AI-triggered actions "wherever fill it possible," referencing a `/preview` mockup with two distinct patterns: `GenerationProgress` (single-artifact generations — usage-count chip, one status line) and a richer tip-teaching card (`DemoLoadingState` in `app/preview/page.tsx` — rotating "While you wait" educational tips, meant specifically for the search/evaluation flow).

**What actually happened**: built `GenerationProgress` for real (already shipped, still live — used in `DocumentGenerator.tsx`, `BragDocGenerator.tsx`, `AnalyzeResumeFitButton.tsx`, `CompanyResearchAutoLoader.tsx`; nobody asked for these to be reverted, they stay). For the search flow specifically, first wired the wrong component (`GenerationProgress` instead of the tip-teaching one — a genuine implementation mismatch caught mid-session), then built the correct one (`LoadingStateWithTips.tsx`, matching the mockup exactly), then hit a real, separate bug while trying to verify it live: `FindJobsForm.tsx`'s loading UI was gated on `jobs.length === 0`, meaning it could never render for any account past its first-ever search (existing jobs always present) — traced and fixed live (`isExplicitSearch` state, distinct from the `jobs.length` proxy). This whole detour was compounded by real environment instability across the session (the sandboxed dev server hit `ECONNREFUSED`/`ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED` repeatedly, needing two restarts; the real Chrome extension connection dropped once; this session's own automated Browser pane gave self-contradictory viewport measurements at multiple points) — several rounds of "why can't I see it" were genuinely caused by the account hitting its real 5/day search cap from repeated live testing (confirmed via `db query` — reset once to unblock verification), not by the component itself.

**Final direct instruction: remove it entirely.** After finally getting it visibly working live, the user rejected the design outright ("you had this loaders but you are not implemented the way I wanted") and asked to redesign from scratch in a future session. Reverted fully: `SearchLoadingState.tsx` and `LoadingStateWithTips.tsx` both deleted, `FindJobsForm.tsx`'s search flow back to just the button's own "Scanning..." state, no card, no gap. The `isExplicitSearch` bug fix itself was NOT part of what got rejected (it's orthogonal to which visual design is used) but became moot once the whole card was removed — worth remembering if a future loading-state redesign reintroduces this same component: the `jobs.length === 0` gating bug is real and will silently prevent it from ever showing for a returning user again unless re-fixed.

**Next session should**: ask the user directly what they actually want for the search loading state before building anything — don't assume the `/preview` mockup's tip-teaching card is still the target design, since it was tried faithfully (confirmed matching the mockup exactly) and still got rejected. Get concrete direction on what "redesign" means this time before writing code.

## Phase 21 — Freemium subscription model (Recon/Command), circuit breakers, admin plan management

**Direct user request**: "architect and implement the monetization logic, database schema, and API circuit breakers for Sortie's subscription model" — the exact item Phase 20's close-out flagged as where the next session should start. Scope grew substantially through the session via a series of direct follow-up requests (admin unlimited access, live-editable plan pricing/content, add/remove tiers, per-user feature overrides, usage notifications) — final architecture is meaningfully more general than the original 2-tier spec.

**What shipped, `tsc`/`eslint` clean**:
- **Schema** (3 migrations): `subscription_plans` (admin-managed, addable/removable — `tier` is a free-form slug, not a fixed enum; seeded with `recon`/`command`), `user_subscriptions` (per-user tier + billing period, FK to `subscription_plans.tier`, RESTRICT on delete so a plan in use can't be removed out from under real subscribers), `api_usage_metrics` (monthly-rolling counters for Apify/Browserbase, tied to each user's own subscription period, not a shared calendar month).
- **`lib/subscription.ts`** — the circuit-breaker core. `checkUsageLimit()` gates insider-connections (Apify) and company-research (Browserbase) before either external API is ever called; `checkJobEvaluationLimit()` gates the AI 10-dimension evaluator itself (new, plan-driven daily cap — Recon capped, Command/overridden unlimited), checked at both the search-batch trigger (`lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`, partial-batch degradation — jobs beyond quota still save, just go unevaluated) and the manual-paste trigger (`lib/externalJob.ts`). All limits are read live from `subscription_plans` every call — an admin price/cap edit takes effect immediately, no redeploy, no cache to invalidate.
- **LLM router** — `lib/access.ts`'s `resolveProvider()` now takes an optional `llmUnlocked` boolean (backward-compatible; sourced from the user's plan) instead of a hardcoded admin-only gate. Wired into `app/find-jobs/[id]/page.tsx` as the first real call site; the other ~17 `resolveProvider(...)` call sites across the app (grep `resolveProvider\(` for the full list) still pass 2 args and default to gemini-only — a real, disclosed follow-up, not silently done.
- **Admin/owner true unlimited access** — a real code-level bypass (`isAdminUser` checked first in every circuit breaker), not just Command's cap. Confirmed this doesn't accidentally depend on the "command" plan row existing/being named that, since admins get a synthetic in-code plan, never a DB lookup.
- **Per-user feature overrides** — `profiles.feature_flags` (previously unused) now backs 4 override keys (`insider_connections_override`, `company_research_override`, `job_evaluation_override`, `llm_unlocked_override`), settable per-user from `/admin/users/[userId]` independent of their plan. Applied inside `getUserSubscription()` so both the server-side gate and any UI reading `plan.*` see the same answer.
- **Usage-limit notifications** — hitting a monthly cap (not the free-tier "never had it" case) writes a real row to the existing in-app notification inbox, deduped per billing period so repeated blocked clicks don't spam.
- **UI** — new `components/shared/LimitReachedModal.tsx` (upgrade vs. monthly-cap-reached are visually distinct states). `InsiderConnectionsButton` pre-gates the click (never fires the network call for a Recon user). `CompanyResearch.tsx` pre-gates the auto-loader itself (this feature fires on mount, not a click, so the gate has to happen before that component even mounts) with a static upsell card instead. New `/admin/billing` page (`components/admin/PlansManager.tsx`) — full plan CRUD, live-editable price/caps/marketing bullets/LLM-unlock, delete blocked by the DB FK if a real subscriber is still on the plan. `/admin/users/[userId]` gained a Subscription section (plan selector + the 4 override toggles).

**Update, same session, later — real Stripe billing shipped and live-tested.** Test-mode Stripe is fully connected (user configured real keys themselves, per this agent's own credential-handling policy — never entered by the agent). Built: a real Command Product/Price ($15/mo), `actions/billing.ts` (checkout session + billing portal, using `insforge.payments.stripe.*` — note the SDK had to be upgraded `^1.3.1` → `^1.5.2` mid-session, since the pinned version's `payments` API shape didn't match this backend after an `update-version` bump), RLS on `payments.stripe_checkout_sessions`/`customer_portal_sessions`, and a `payments.webhook_events` fulfillment trigger (`fulfill_stripe_subscription_event`) handling `invoice.paid` (grant/renew), `invoice.payment_failed` (grace period, `status='past_due'`), `customer.subscription.deleted` (revert to recon), and `customer.subscription.updated` (pause/resume — `status='paused'`, circuit breakers correctly drop to recon-level limits while paused without losing the real `tier` value). **Completed one real $15 test-mode purchase end-to-end** — Checkout → webhook → `user_subscriptions` row correctly updated to `tier='command'`. Two real bugs found and fixed via this live test, not caught by `tsc`: (1) the invoice line item's price lives at `lines.data[0].pricing.price_details.price`, not `lines.data[0].price.id` as first written — the wrong path silently no-opped via the trigger's own `RAISE WARNING` branch, confirmed via direct payload inspection after `payments.webhook_events` showed `processed` but `user_subscriptions` stayed empty; (2) `createCheckoutSessionAction`/`createBillingPortalSessionAction` wrapped `requireUser()` inside their own `try/catch`, silently swallowing Next.js's `NEXT_REDIRECT` signal as a generic error string shown to the user — this exact gotcha was already documented in `actions/profile.ts`'s own comments and I missed it; fixed to match the established `requireUser()`-outside-try/catch pattern. New Settings "Subscription" tab (`components/settings/SubscriptionTab.tsx`) — live plan comparison, upgrade/downgrade (downgrade routes to the real Stripe portal, never a custom duplicate flow), a real `?upgraded=1` success banner. Razorpay stays parked — user hit real, unresolved `Authentication failed`/`PAYMENT_CONFIG_INVALID` errors on write operations (catalog reads worked, item-create didn't) after two key regenerations; deferred per direct user instruction ("put a placeholder for razorpay for now").
- **~17 `resolveProvider()` call sites** still on the old 2-arg (gemini-only-unless-admin) form — unchanged, still a real follow-up.
- **A real, separate Settings-panel layout bug found via the user's own real Chrome browser (not this session's automated Browser pane, which gave self-contradictory measurements — real device beat the tool)**: the panel's width was silently tracking whichever settings tab was currently visible instead of staying fixed. Root cause: `body` is `flex flex-col` (`app/layout.tsx`), and `main`'s `mx-auto` (auto margins on the flex cross-axis) disables stretch alignment per the flexbox spec, so it fell back to content-based shrink-to-fit sizing — and since `SettingsPanel.tsx` keeps inactive tabs mounted with `display:none` (a deliberate choice, not itself the bug), only the visible tab's content counted toward that sizing. Fixed with `w-full` alongside `mx-auto max-w-4xl` in `app/settings/page.tsx`. Also fixed along the way: nav labels overflowing past the sidebar column (`min-w-0` + wrapped `<span>`), and the content pane given a genuine fixed `h-[520px]` (not `min-h`) per direct user request that the panel never resize on tab switch at all.

**A separate, real bug found and diagnosed mid-session — FIXED in Phase 22, see the top of this file.** The Pipeline Funnel widget and the AI "Pipeline Strategy Read" narrative both counted raw `application_status = 'draft'` jobs as active backlog — confirmed via `lib/pipelineStrategy.ts`/`actions/pipelineStrategy.ts` that neither filtered `dropped_from_search_at`/`marked_unavailable_at`, unlike the Action Center's "125 jobs may no longer be available" banner, which already tracked that same staleness signal. One real test account had ~973 Draft jobs, and the AI was calling a large chunk of that a "bottleneck" when much of it was dead postings. A real `agy` research pass (user-approved `--dangerously-skip-permissions`) on how Huntr/Teal/JobRight/swipe-apps handle this — verbatim recommendation: split into a pre-pipeline "Inbox" (hidden from the Kanban, dense table + bulk actions, TTL auto-archives after ~14 days untouched) feeding a Pipeline that only starts at an explicit `to_apply`/shortlisted stage the user triages into — was implemented exactly as recommended in Phase 22 (`inbox`/`shortlisted` statuses, `InboxTable.tsx`, the 14-day auto-archive cron, full backfill).

## Phase 20, final stretch — Sentry live, Calendar/Outlook integrations, Zapier export, full login/signup redesign, monetization research logged

**Direct user request, continued from "what's still open?"**: worked through the remaining blocked-on-user items one at a time, plus a couple of drive-by fixes and a full auth UI redesign the user asked for partway through.

1. **Sentry — now genuinely live**, not just wired. Found via research it was already fully coded from an earlier session (just missing a real DSN) — fixed two real bugs while auditing it: removed the deprecated `disableLogger` flag (its replacement is webpack-only, this project runs Turbopack) and restored `onRouterTransitionStart` in `instrumentation-client.ts` (a prior session wrongly concluded `Sentry.captureRouterTransitionStart` was invented — it's real, just declared in the SDK's client-only type entry). User created a real Sentry org/project (`sortie-g1`/`javascript-nextjs`) and supplied the DSN; wired into `.env` and Vercel prod env vars, deployed, confirmed no more startup warnings.
2. **Google Calendar interview detection** — real Google Cloud OAuth app (`Sortie Integration`, `calendar.events.readonly` only — the lighter Sensitive-tier scope, not Gmail's Restricted/CASA-audit tier). `lib/googleCalendar.ts`, `actions/googleCalendar.ts`, Settings → Google Calendar tab. Checks the next 14 days of events against tracked job companies (naive substring match, genuinely sufficient at this app's scale).
3. **Outlook Calendar interview detection** — mirrors the Google Calendar feature exactly via a real Azure AD app registration (multitenant + personal accounts, `Calendars.Read` only). User initially landed in their work Azure tenant by accident — redirected to a personal Microsoft account for the app registration, correctly avoiding a company-IT entanglement.
4. **Gmail ingest + Gmail application-status detection — explicitly deferred**, direct user decision. `gmail.readonly` is a Google Restricted scope requiring an annual paid CASA security audit past a handful of test users; parked alongside the voice interview simulator for a future session.
5. **Proactive weekly match-digest email** — reuses `generateWeeklyBriefingsAsync`'s already-computed summary as the email body (no new AI call), same CAN-SPAM gate as the existing marketing-broadcast sender. **Inert until `MARKETING_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS` is set** — a real compliance requirement, not something to fabricate.
6. **Zapier / Google Sheets export** — new `GET /api/integrations/jobs`, bearer-authed with the same personal API key already used by the browser extension. Zapier's own "Webhooks by Zapier" trigger polls it, then the user chains a native "Google Sheets: Create Spreadsheet Row" action inside Zapier itself — no direct Sheets OAuth built on our side.
7. **"Recent News cards" turned out to already be shipped** — `CompanyResearchDossier.recentUpdates`, deliberately labeled "Recent Updates" not "Recent News" since it's grounded in a free site crawl, not a real news API. Corrected the stale build-plan marker instead of duplicating the feature.
8. **Full login/signup redesign, done in two passes** (first a straightforward icon/provider addition, then a real agy-researched restructure per direct user request):
   - **Google One Tap** — real `signInWithIdToken({provider:'google', token})` call, confirmed real (not guessed) via a clean `tsc` pass against the installed SDK's types. Same Google Client ID InsForge already uses for the existing Google login, so no new Google Cloud app needed.
   - **LinkedIn, Microsoft, Apple sign-in** — all three added via InsForge's **shared/managed OAuth keys** (the user found this option in InsForge's dashboard mid-session) — genuinely zero external app registration needed for any of the three, a much lighter path than originally expected.
   - **Real brand icons** — replaced generic lucide stand-ins (Globe/GitBranch/Briefcase/Grid2x2) with authentic Simple Icons SVGs (CC0-licensed), fetched once and embedded inline as `components/auth/BrandIcons.tsx` — no runtime CDN dependency, monochrome by design (matches this app's own strict color-token discipline rather than each brand's real multi-color mark).
   - **Layout redesign per `agy` research** (Linear/Vercel/Raycast-style dev-tool auth patterns): single centered card replacing the old split hero/form layout (research flagged split-screens as reading "enterprise B2B"); Google as one full-width primary provider with GitHub/LinkedIn/Microsoft/Apple as a secondary icon row below it (the research's "hybrid" pattern for 4+ providers). Form-first ordering was kept per the user's own reference screenshot, overriding the research's general OAuth-first default.
   - **A real signup-flow gap found and fixed while investigating "signup only asks email+password"**: the non-verification-required signup branch hardcoded a redirect to `/profile`, bypassing the new onboarding wizard entirely — dead code today (this project requires email verification), but fixed for real defensive correctness using `getPostLoginRedirectPath`, same as every other real auth path. Also added a "Full name" field to signup (direct user decision after being offered 3 options), confirmed accepted end-to-end via a real disposable-email signup call (`requireVerification: true` returned correctly).
   - **Dark-mode autofill fix**: Chrome/Safari inject a stark white background into autofilled inputs that ignores plain `background-color` — added the standard inset-box-shadow override using this app's own surface/text tokens (not raw hex).
9. **Monetization/pricing strategy — researched via `agy`, logged, NOT decided or built yet.** Full artifact at `C:/Users/ridha/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/01fc43f8-b289-49fe-8f95-b1f2a2c20ac5/sortie_monetization_strategy.md` — read that before building anything here. Fast summary: two tiers, **"Recon" (free)** and **"Command" ($29/mo or $144/yr)**; free tier keeps the retention engine (Missions tracker, Career OS, job discovery, metered AI tailoring at 3/day); paid tier gates the high-compute/high-cost/high-urgency features (10-dimension evaluator, Interview Prep Sandbox, Insider Connections, negotiation scripts, company dossiers, the 3 integrations just built). Also floated one-time "Airlift" credit packs and a high-ticket "Debrief" human-reviewed teardown. Payment gateway recommendation: **Lemon Squeezy** (Merchant of Record, handles global VAT/tax automatically, 5%+$0.50/transaction, zero monthly fee, solo-dev-friendly) over raw Stripe (cheaper at ~2.9%+$0.30 but leaves you legally responsible for foreign tax filing) — **but note InsForge's own backend already has native Stripe/Razorpay payment support built in** (confirmed via the SDK's own types: `CreateCheckoutSessionBody`, `CreateCustomerPortalSessionBody`, Stripe webhook projections) — worth comparing against Lemon Squeezy before deciding, since reusing what InsForge already provides could mean much less integration work than either third-party option the research suggested. **Next session should start here**: decide the tier structure/pricing/payment gateway, then build.

**Verification tiers, disclosed honestly**: Sentry/Calendar/Outlook integrations are `tsc`/`eslint`/build-clean but not live-tested end-to-end with real connected accounts (each needs a real external account only the user has — ask them to click through Settings and report back). The login/signup redesign WAS live-tested on the real dev server (real signup call, real DOM inspection of both card layouts, real console-error check) short of completing full email verification (needs real inbox access).

## Phase 20, cont'd — 2 real editorial blog posts, researched via `agy` (this session, most recent)

**Direct user request**: "I also want you to have some blogs on your own with your research with the agy cli call and make it seo friendly." Checked existing infra first before building anything — the `pages` table + `/blog`/`/blog/[slug]` routes already existed (Phase 18's GEO content engine), already wired for real SEO: dynamic `metaTitle`/`metaDescription`, real Article JSON-LD, and automatic inclusion in `/sitemap.xml` for any `status='published'` row. Nothing new needed there — this was purely a content-writing task, not a build.

Ran two separate real `agy -p` research passes (no `--dangerously-skip-permissions` needed this time — external web research doesn't touch local repo files, unlike the earlier marketing-homepage research pass that did): one on why auto-apply/mass-application tools underperform (recruiter sentiment, detection/penalization, response-rate data), one on ghost jobs and job-posting red flags (real named surveys: Resume Builder 2024, Clarify Capital 2026). Wrote both posts by hand from that research — not published raw — grounding claims in the named sources where the research actually cited one, and deliberately softening language ("industry reporting suggests," "recent surveys report") around the auto-apply research's own stats, since that pass didn't attach specific named sources despite being asked to, and this app's standing honesty discipline (never present a number as harder fact than the research backing it actually supports) applies to blog copy exactly as much as it does to any in-product AI output.

Inserted both directly into the `pages` table via `db query` (base64-encoded body content through `convert_from(decode(...,'base64'),'UTF8')`, to sidestep shell/SQL quote-escaping on ~4.5KB of markdown containing apostrophes and quoted phrases — confirmed byte-length-identical after insert, no corruption). **Live-verified on the real dev server**: both list correctly on `/blog`, both render correctly on their own `/blog/[slug]` pages, real `metaTitle` confirmed as the actual browser tab title, real `meta_description`/Article JSON-LD confirmed via direct DOM inspection, both appear in `/sitemap.xml`. **Then confirmed live on real production immediately** (`curl` against both slugs → `200`, titles present in the served HTML) — since this is DB content read at request time, not a code change, there was nothing to deploy.

Titles/slugs: "Why Auto-Apply Tools Are Quietly Sabotaging Your Job Search" (`/blog/why-auto-apply-tools-hurt-your-job-search`), "How to Spot a Ghost Job (and Other Red Flags) Before You Waste Your Time" (`/blog/how-to-spot-ghost-jobs-red-flags`).

**Also corrected while in this section**: `build-plan.md`'s entire §I table had 4 of 5 rows still marked 🆕/stale despite being shipped in Phases 18-19 (ATS checker, programmatic SEO/GEO engine, sitemap/robots, shareable eval link) — fixed alongside the new editorial-content row so the table stays trustworthy for the next backlog scan.

## Phase 20, cont'd — real onboarding wizard, dimension tooltips, customizable dashboard widgets

**Direct user request**: asked for a status list of what was left in the build plan beyond the Gmail/Calendar/Outlook integrations already flagged as blocked; picked 6 items off that list ("go ahead with the buildable"). Before writing any code, a research pass found 2 of the 6 were already fully shipped and stale on `build-plan.md`'s own tracking (**profile completeness meter** and **recently-viewed jobs** — both fully built, live, and documented already; don't re-build these if they resurface on a future backlog scan). The other 4 turned into 3 real, shipped pieces:

1. **Real onboarding wizard** (`/onboarding`) — replaces reliance on the static `/preview/onboarding` mockup with a real 3-step flow (target roles + work auth → résumé upload/AI extraction → seniority + "how did you find us?"), gated by a new `profiles.onboarding_completed_at` column checked ahead of the existing `is_complete` gate in both `lib/auth.ts`'s `getPostLoginRedirectPath` and `app/(auth)/callback/route.ts`'s local copy. **Backfilled to account-creation time for all 8 pre-existing users** (confirmed via `db query`, needed an explicit user confirmation since the bulk UPDATE hit the permission classifier) so only brand-new signups see it. Résumé step reuses the existing `uploadResume`/`extractProfile` actions directly, not the newer multi-slot `ResumeManager` (confirmed via research that onboarding only ever needs the one base résumé). New `profiles.acquisition_channel` column folds "how did you find us?" into the wizard's own step 3 rather than a separate feature. **Live-verified end to end** on the real test account: nulled its gate column, confirmed a real login redirected to `/onboarding`, clicked through all 3 real steps (skipped résumé to avoid mutating test data further), confirmed the DB row held the exact real picks, confirmed the correct conditional redirect (`/profile`, since the profile was still genuinely incomplete with no résumé), confirmed a second direct `/onboarding` visit correctly bounces away. Real side effect: this overwrote the test account's own `job_titles_seeking`/`work_authorization`/`experience_level`/`acquisition_channel` with test values — prior values weren't snapshotted first, so can't be restored exactly (low-stakes fields, not destructive to anything else).
2. **Contextual tooltips for the 10 evaluation dimensions** — the copy already existed (`EvaluationBreakdown.tsx`'s `DIMENSION_EXPLANATIONS`), rendered via a plain native `title` attribute. Upgraded to a real positioned hover/focus panel via new `components/ui/Tooltip.tsx` — **this codebase's first Tooltip primitive**, hand-rolled (no Radix/headless-UI dependency exists anywhere in this app), Escape-to-close built in from day one. **A real environment gotcha hit during verification, not a code bug**: dispatched synthetic `mouseenter`/`mouseover`/`focus` DOM events did NOT trigger React's synthetic handlers in this session's Browser pane — confirmed the component for real instead by calling the attached React prop functions (`element[__reactProps$...].onMouseEnter()`) directly, which rendered the correct real copy and correctly closed on a simulated Escape. If a future hover/focus-driven component ever "does nothing" under this same browser-automation setup, try this direct-prop-call method before assuming a real bug.
3. **Customizable dashboard widgets, show/hide only, no reorder** — deliberately narrower than a full drag-and-drop customization: the bento-grid layout (build-plan.md §P) was its own researched design decision (which widget spans how many columns, next to which other one), and reordering would mean either reinventing that span logic for arbitrary combinations or flattening it into a plain list. New `profiles.dashboard_hidden_widgets` (`text[]`), a "Customize" button on `/dashboard` (`components/dashboard/CustomizeDashboardModal.tsx`, reuses `SectionModal.tsx`'s chrome). `app/dashboard/page.tsx` recomputes each row's layout from whichever widgets remain visible — the two combinations the row was actually designed for keep their exact original spans; any other combination (only reachable by explicitly hiding something) falls back to a simple CSS `auto-fit` equal-width reflow, so hiding a widget never leaves dead whitespace and the untouched-by-anyone default view is pixel-identical to before. **A real near-miss caught before it shipped**: `DASHBOARD_WIDGET_KEYS`/`DashboardWidgetKey` were first drafted inside the new `"use server"` `actions/dashboardLayout.ts` file — the exact same invariant violation (`progress-tracker.md`'s Phase 15 `actions/referralCopy.ts` bug: only async functions may be exported from a `"use server"` file) already documented once in this project. Caught via re-reading my own new file before running `tsc`, moved the const/type into a new plain `lib/dashboardWidgets.ts` instead. **Live-verified** on the real test account: toggled a widget off, confirmed the DB write, confirmed the real re-rendered dashboard correctly dropped that widget and reflowed the row with no dead space, then restored the test account's prefs back to `{}` afterward.

**Committed (`b2e9f69` onboarding, `b72708c` tooltip+dashboard), pushed, and deployed to production** — see the updated session-status line above.

## Phase 20, cont'd — "AI heavy dashboard" (direct user request), both parts shipped and deployed

Before building, flagged a real tension: the dashboard's `AIActionCenter` is deliberately deterministic (its own comment says a per-visit live LLM call is "exactly the wrong place"). Asked the user how cost-heavy they wanted this — they chose opt-in widgets first, then a proactive pre-generated briefing. Both are done. Full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s two newest entries.

1. **`PipelineStrategyCard.tsx`** on `/dashboard` — an opt-in AI synthesis widget over the CURRENT pipeline snapshot (per-stage job counts + average match score, strong-match jobs sitting untouched in Draft). Checked `lib/outcomeInsights.ts` first to confirm this doesn't duplicate `/career`'s existing rejection-pattern synthesis — it doesn't, genuinely new ground. Same zero-AI-aggregation-then-one-opt-in-call shape as Market Readiness/Skill Gap Tracker; new `pipeline_strategy_read` usage action (5/day).
2. **Proactive weekly briefing** — a new Monday-9am Inngest cron (`generateWeeklyBriefingsAsync`, `lib/inngest/functions.ts`) pre-generates one real summary per user with genuine recent activity or an upcoming deadline (fully dormant accounts cost nothing), stored on new `profiles.weekly_briefing`/`weekly_briefing_generated_at` columns. `WeeklyBriefingCard.tsx` just displays the stored value — zero AI cost per page load, which was the whole point.

**A real environment obstacle worth remembering**: manually invoking a cron-triggered Inngest function through the local dev server's UI (`localhost:8288`) doesn't reliably work in this environment — several synthetic-click strategies produced zero effect. A standalone `tsx` script also failed (`@insforge/shared-schemas`'s package exports don't resolve outside Next.js's own bundler). What worked: a temporary, clearly-named API route inside the real Next.js app that runs the exact cron logic once, hit via `curl`, deleted immediately after (confirmed via `git status` it was never committed). **If a cron function ever needs manual local verification again, use this route method directly — don't spend time on the Inngest dev server UI or a standalone script first.**

**Both new columns were added via plain `db query` (`ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`), not `db migrations`** — per this project's own recorded gotcha, the migrations-tracking workaround is only needed when a new table's DDL references `auth.users(id)` directly; plain columns on an existing table don't need it.

**Committed (`0d38847`, `ee8c5c5`) and deployed to production** — `npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3`, followed by `PUT /api/inngest` (200) to re-sync the new cron function with Inngest Cloud. Post-deploy checklist green.

**Same-session follow-up (still Phase 20) — all 4 homepage fast-follows closed out, "ya go ahead" from the user.** All committed and deployed. Full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s newest 4 entries; fast map:
1. **Features mega-menu** — real dropdown grouped Discovery/Application/Interviews, deep-linking to new anchor `id`s on the actual feature cards.
2. **Dedicated `/pricing` page** — reuses `CTASection.tsx` directly, nav now points here instead of the `/#pricing` anchor.
3. **Conversion tracking** — `marketing_cta_clicked` fires on every "Start for free" click; confirmed via `vercel env ls production` that PostHog is genuinely configured in prod even though local `.env` has an empty key by design.
4. **Homepage performance pass** — found and fixed 2 real pre-existing bugs: `CommandPalette.tsx` had zero public-route gating at all (Cmd+K opened auth-only links on the marketing homepage), and `NavigatorLauncher.tsx`'s own route list had gone stale and missed `/methodology`/`/pricing`/`/ats-checker`. New shared `lib/publicRoutes.ts` fixes both, and both `Loader` wrappers now gate at the `next/dynamic()` level so a public-page visitor never even downloads either JS chunk.

**A real environment lesson worth remembering going forward, found while chasing what looked like a 3rd stale-Turbopack-graph recurrence**: it wasn't the server at all — it was the **browser tab's own Next.js Router Cache**, which lives in the tab's memory and survives a full dev-server restart + `.next` wipe since nothing forces the tab to discard it. **Try a fresh browser tab before escalating to a `.next` wipe** — cheaper, faster, and may explain some of this session's earlier "stale graph" diagnoses too. This bit twice in a row this stretch: once diagnosing `CTASection.tsx` (looked like a real "Link is not defined" bug, wasn't), once diagnosing the Cmd+K regression check on `/dashboard` (looked like the new gating broke authenticated pages, didn't — confirmed clean in a fresh tab).

## Phase 19, fourth (final) batch — the last 5 items, and a real InsForge outage caught + waited out mid-verification

Deployed (`npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3`, no permission prompt). Post-deploy checklist green: `GET /` → `200`, `GET /find-jobs`/`GET /missions`/`GET /career`/`GET /dashboard` → `307`, `PUT /api/inngest` → `200`, `GET /api/inngest` → `401`.

**This closes out the full Phase 19 queue — all 24 items from the original "complete the remaining free tasks" instruction are now done.**

1. **Global search** — extended the existing Cmd+K command palette (didn't build a new search surface) with a debounced real-jobs search, merged in as a "Jobs" result group. Scoped to jobs only, this app's highest-value searchable entity.
2. **"Today"/focus view — investigated, deliberately NOT built as a new page.** The dashboard's existing "Action Center" already IS that surface; a separate page would have duplicated it (the same near-duplicate mistake this project has caught before — per-job Ask Navigator vs. global Navigator). Extended the existing widget with the one genuinely missing signal (upcoming deadlines) instead of building something new.
3. **Loading states that teach** — Find & Evaluate's search flow. Verified the new copy's claims ("results appear progressively") are actually true by tracing the real `loading`/`setJobs` state sequencing, not assumed. NOT live-tested with a real search — would have spent a real, arguably redundant paid SerpApi call to confirm a copy change already verified in code; disclosed as a real, lower-confidence tier.
4. **Skeleton loaders + optimistic UI, audited.** Optimistic UI: confirmed already broadly covered (42 components already follow the pattern) — not a real gap. Skeleton loaders: only `/find-jobs` had one; added matching ones for `/missions`, `/dashboard`, `/career`.
5. **Job detail drawer / split view** — the last item. A deliberately lightweight summary panel (not a replica of the real job detail page's 25+ sub-components), wired only into Find & Evaluate via a new optional `onQuickView` prop on `JobResultCard.tsx`.

**A real InsForge backend outage hit mid-verification of the drawer, correctly diagnosed as infrastructure, not a code bug**: `/find-jobs` briefly stopped loading any jobs, with `getUnreadNotificationCount`/other server actions failing `InsForgeError: Request timed out after 30000ms`, cascading into spurious auth-check failures (real `/login` redirects, page loads up to 2.3 minutes) — confirmed via the dev server's own logs, not assumed from symptoms. Recovered on its own within a few minutes; re-verified cleanly afterward. Matches this project's own previously-documented pattern of transient InsForge instability (Phase 13, Phase 16) — if `/find-jobs` or similar ever silently stops loading again with no obvious code change nearby, check server logs for this exact error before assuming a regression.

**A second real cross-session gotcha, worth remembering**: a previous session's shared dev server had gone away partway through this session; starting a fresh one of this session's own meant a genuinely logged-out browser (no cookie carried over) — the first attempt at verifying global search was silently invalid because of this (tested against `/login`, not the real app). Always check `document.cookie`/`window.location.href` before trusting a "no results" finding as real.

## Phase 19, third batch — bulk actions, homepage research logged, and a real accessibility audit

Deployed as of this checkpoint (`npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3`, no permission prompt this time — confirms the classifier behavior is genuinely inconsistent run to run, not predictable by pattern). Post-deploy checklist green: `GET /` → `200`, `GET /missions`/`GET /career` → `307`, `PUT /api/inngest` → `200`, `GET /api/inngest` → `401`.

1. **Bulk actions on Missions** (List view) — multi-select checkboxes on `JobResultCard.tsx` (optional prop, every other call site unaffected), bulk archive + bulk tag-append (fetches each job's own existing tags first, never a blanket overwrite). Live-verified through the real UI both ways.
2. **Missions filter/sort/group parity, closed** — audited first and found most of the original spec already shipped in earlier phases; added the two genuinely missing pieces (sort by company A-Z, a List-view "Group by company" toggle).
3. **Marketing homepage research, logged per direct user request** — see `build-plan.md`'s new §S. Real environment note: `agy -p` needs `--dangerously-skip-permissions` to read local files non-interactively, and that flag hit this session's own permission classifier and needed an explicit confirmation — same "stop and ask" category as a deploy, don't try to route around it.
4. **Accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA) audit, via the design:accessibility-review skill** — found and fixed a real, systemic keyboard-operability gap: none of the app's 11 custom dropdown/popover components closed on Escape (only click-outside/scroll did). Fixed all 9 files. Live-verified on 2 real instances (Missions' Sort popover, JobActionBar's status menu) via genuine dispatched Escape keydowns, not code-review-only. Also found and FLAGGED (deliberately not silently fixed) a real `text-muted` contrast gap (~3.3:1 on white, below the 4.5:1 AA minimum) — a deliberate hand-tuned design-token color that needs a real visual check before changing, logged in `ui-tokens.md` with a verified fix candidate.
5. **App-level theme customization (accent/density) — investigated, deliberately not built.** Accent customization would conflict with the app's own strict semantic-color invariants (`--color-agent` is exclusively reserved for AI content, etc.); density mode would need a real spacing-token architecture that doesn't exist yet (confirmed via grep: zero CSS-variable-driven spacing in `globals.css`, every component hardcodes static Tailwind spacing classes). Both are real future initiatives needing a design pass, not quick wins.

**A recurring environment lesson worth remembering for next time**: this session's Browser pane never composited frames, which makes `computer`-tool clicks silently no-op (zero error, zero network trace) — the fix used throughout was dispatching real DOM events via `javascript_tool` instead (debugging-only use). A second, separate gotcha hit multiple times: reading DOM state synchronously in the same script block immediately after a `.click()` call reads PRE-render state, since React hasn't flushed yet — wrap in an `async` IIFE with a short `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 300))` between the action and the check, every time.

**Still open, next up**: a job detail drawer/split view, global search, a skeleton-loaders + optimistic-UI pass, "loading states that teach" copy, and a "Today"/focus view — all bigger, more novel UI builds than what's shipped so far this session, better picked up fresh rather than rushed at the end of a long session. Task list tracked live via TaskCreate/TaskUpdate this session.

## Phase 19, second batch — 4 more items, including a real production data-integrity bug

Deployed as commit `e11de12` (`npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3` — this time went through without hitting the permission classifier, unlike the first batch). Post-deploy checklist green: `GET /` → `200`, `GET /admin`/`GET /career`/`GET /profile`/`GET /jobs/external` → `307`, `PUT /api/inngest` → `200`, `GET /api/inngest` → `401`.

1. **Skills radar chart** on `/career` — real matched-skill frequency (reuses the existing `aggregateSkillGaps` helper against `matched_skills` instead of `missing_skills`), `recharts` already a dependency. Honest verification-tier note: the real test account's `matched_skills` is genuinely empty (sparse profile), so the non-empty chart render wasn't visually confirmed with real populated data — confirmed correct via a clean independent `npm run build` and code-identity with the already-proven `SkillGapTracker`.
2. **Feature-announcement/waitlist modal — investigated, deliberately not built.** This is a captured competitor design pattern (JobRight's "Introducing Agent [Beta]"), not a concrete feature spec — `/waitlist` (checked) is a different, already-built thing (an at-capacity account gate). Grepped the whole app for remaining `<ComingSoon>` placeholders (the natural home for this pattern) and found none left. Nothing to attach it to right now.
3. **Direct mid-session user request: "the mobile version of profile page"** — real bug found in the SHARED `components/ui/Tabs.tsx` (used by Profile, job detail's Overview/Company/Offer Tools, both document workspaces): the tablist had zero overflow handling and sat right at the edge of a real 375px viewport with zero margin. Fixed with `overflow-x-auto` on the tablist itself — fixes every page using `Tabs.tsx`, not just Profile. Live-verified: confirmed real overflow before, zero page-level overflow after, at an exact 375px viewport, across all 4 tabs plus the edit modal.
4. **Dedup audit (§37) — found and fixed two real, separate duplicate-job-creation bugs.** (a) `lib/externalJob.ts`'s `createExternalJob` (manual paste + extension capture) had zero dedup at all — fixed with a same-user-url pre-check. (b) The primary SerpApi search path's existing `external_id`-based dedup doesn't actually work, because Google Jobs issues a fresh `job_id`/token per search run even for the identical real listing — confirmed via real production data (**one account had the same job duplicated 11 times**). Fixed with a title+company+location fingerprint pre-check that refreshes the existing row instead of inserting a new one. **Deliberately did NOT retroactively merge the duplicate rows already sitting in the data** — real data-loss risk (which row's status/tags/notes wins), needs an actual product decision on merge semantics. This is a real, still-open item if a data cleanup is ever wanted.

**A second real environment finding, worth remembering**: production deploys via `vercel --prod` hit the permission classifier unpredictably — the first batch this session got blocked and needed an explicit `AskUserQuestion` confirmation; the very next deploy in the same session went through with no prompt at all. Don't assume a deploy will be blocked or won't be — just attempt it, and if blocked, stop and ask rather than finding a workaround (this is one of the few actions in this project that should NOT be pushed through on a general "keep going" instruction).

**6 real features shipped, committed, deployed to production, and live-verified this stretch** (commits `fa9cdf6`→`822a9f5`, deployed via `npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3` after explicit user confirmation — production deploys hit the auto-mode permission classifier this session, unlike migration/DB commands, so that one action needs a real check-in even under a "keep going" directive):

Investigated build-plan.md's original "JobPosting structured data" ask first and deliberately did NOT build it as scoped — no public job-listing surface exists in the app to put it on, and republishing scraped third-party job descriptions to manufacture one raises a real licensing question outside this agent's authority. Folded the legitimate SEO value into the share page instead.
2. **Visible usage consumption in-editor** — reuses Settings' existing Credits & Usage action, no new backend.
3. **`job_decisions` table** — the Q3 fast-follow left open when the outcome loop first shipped. Hiding a job now optionally captures why (quick-pick reasons); a real "applied" transition is recorded the same way. Surfaced as a new "Applied vs. skipped" stat on `/career`.

6. **Real in-app notification inbox**, closing the `/notifications` ComingSoon placeholder. v1 writes from one source only — real application-status milestones (interviewing/offered/rejected) — plus a real unread-count badge on the navbar bell.

**Also investigated and resolved without building anything** (already covered or genuinely blocked, not skipped carelessly): JobPosting schema (see #1 above), résumé page counter (already covered by the PDF viewer's native toolbar), logout escape hatch during onboarding (no real onboarding flow exists yet to attach it to — only a `/preview/onboarding` mockup), dual homepage/nav CTAs (already shipped under different copy), and the dashboard's 10-dimension hover popup (the dashboard v1 redesign's own header comment already documents choosing real navigation over this exact idea, deliberately — respected that prior call rather than overriding it).

**Two real bugs found and fixed via live testing, not caught by `tsc`**: a hydration mismatch in `ShareJobLink.tsx` (`window.location.origin` read inline during render — undefined server-side, real client-side); and a gating bug in `OutcomeInsights.tsx` where the whole section stayed on its empty state whenever the older interview-rate stats lacked data, even when the new job_decisions data had enough on its own.

**A real environment artifact re-confirmed, not a new bug**: this session's Browser pane isn't compositing frames (same signature as Phase 18's documented "phantom bug" — a `screenshot` call fails with the exact same error), which additionally means `computer`-tool clicks silently no-op with zero error and zero network trace. Worked around by dispatching real DOM `.click()` calls via `javascript_tool` for verification (debugging-only use, not implementation) — confirmed this actually fires the real server actions correctly. If a click-driven test shows zero effect in this environment, check for a fired network request before concluding the feature is broken.

**Post-deploy checklist all green on real production**: `GET /` → `200`, `GET /admin`/`GET /career`/`GET /notifications` → `307` (correct unauthenticated redirects), `GET /blog` → `200`, `PUT /api/inngest` → `200`, `GET /api/inngest` → `401`, `GET /share/nonexistent-token` → `404`.

Article JSON-LD added to public blog pages. **Fully live-verified**: real generation → real published page → real JSON-LD confirmed, then reverted to draft after testing (a human should choose when it goes public for real).
2. **"Success Story" content repurposing pipeline** — new `social_drafts` table, triggers off a real `application_status -> 'offered'` transition, anonymized drafts land in a new approval inbox on `/admin/marketing`. **Fully live-verified**: real trigger → real anonymized draft → approved through the real admin UI.
3. **Referral system (new)** — `profiles.referral_code`/`referred_by_code`, `referral_rewards` table, a Settings → Referrals tab, admin overview on `/admin/marketing`. Reward is a capped `custom_usage_multiplier` bump, not a new credits system. **Settings tab live-verified** (code generation + stats render); the capture/claim flow (`?ref=` link → localStorage → post-login claim) is built and type-checked but not click-tested — needs a second account to test the real claim path properly.
4. **AI-personalized referral copy** — depends on #3, `lib/referralCopy.ts`, new `referral_message` usage action. **Fully live-verified**: real Gemini draft, honest content, real link appended correctly.
5. **Signal-based outreach automation** — the one item genuinely gated on a paid decision (Clay/Apollo) no agent can make. Shipped the free half for real (`lib/hiringSignal.ts`, a genuine hiring-velocity signal from the app's own job data, surfaced on the job-detail Company tab) and the paid half as an inert Sentry/Resend-style scaffold (`/admin/marketing`'s new "Outreach signal enrichment" card). **Admin settings card live-verified**; the job-detail card itself is confirmed correct too — see the resolved "phantom bug" below, not an open item.

**A real bug found and fixed via live testing**: `actions/referralCopy.ts` (a `"use server"` file) re-exported a type — same invariant this project already learned once before (`actions/careerEvents.ts`, Phase 15: only async functions may be exported from a `"use server"` file). Caused a real client-side `ReferenceError` on every Settings page load. Fixed by importing the type from `lib/referralCopy.ts` directly at every call site instead.

**A scary-looking "bug" chased at length and fully resolved as a testing-tool artifact, not a real defect — important to not re-chase this next session.** `/find-jobs/[id]` appeared to render only the navbar (both locally and on a completely fresh production deploy), with the real page content present in the DOM but stuck invisible inside Next.js's own streaming-reveal container (`<div id="S:0" hidden>`, the standard React/Next server-streaming boundary mechanism). Root-caused by manually invoking the browser's own already-loaded reveal function (`window.$RV(window.$RB)`) from the console — it succeeded instantly and revealed the real content, proving the app's data/logic/JSX were always 100% correct. The actual cause: this session's Browser pane was never displayed/composited to a real screen (confirmed separately by a `computer{action:"screenshot"}` call failing with "the Browser pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames"), and `requestAnimationFrame` — which Next's streaming reveal schedules through when no explicit reveal-timeout is set — never fires for a non-composited document. A real user's actual visible browser tab hits this reveal within one frame (~16ms); it only manifests in this exact background/non-displayed automated-testing configuration. **No code fix needed or made.** If a page ever again looks stuck showing only a loading skeleton in this environment, check `window.$RB`/`window.$RV` in the console before assuming a real bug — this is the signature.

**Direct mid-session redirect from the user**: *"we will complete the admin panels functions as we can today and we will back to the user end features which we still have in the pipeline... change your thinking pattern as a owner of this product."* — actioned by finishing admin-side verification first, then pushing/deploying, then returning to consumer-side testing.

**Committed, pushed, AND deployed to production** — commit `b0389a1` on `origin/feature/my-experiment`, deployed via `npx vercel --prod --scope sortie3`, aliased live at `https://jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app`. Post-deploy checklist all green: `PUT /api/inngest` → `200` (`modified: true`, picked up the 2 new functions), `GET /api/inngest` → `401`, `GET /admin` → `307`, `GET /career` → `307`, `GET /` → `200`, `GET /blog` → `200`. 5 new migrations were already live on the shared InsForge backend from earlier in the session.

**Consumer-facing verification, now confirmed on real production** (the "phantom bug" investigation doubled as this testing): the job-detail Outreach Signal card renders correctly (confirmed via the manual-`$RV` workaround revealing real, correct page content). Settings → Referrals tab and its AI-copy generation were already live-verified pre-deploy. **Referral claim flow's self-referral guard live-verified for real**: set a pending `?ref=`-equivalent code in `localStorage` matching the signed-in user's own code, navigated to an authenticated page, confirmed `ReferralClaimer` fired and cleared the pending state — then confirmed via direct DB query that `profiles.referred_by_code` stayed `null` and `custom_usage_multiplier` stayed `1` (no reward leaked through). **Still genuinely untestable solo**: the real cross-account success path (a second real person's referral actually landing a reward) — needs a second real account, not simulable safely from one.

## Admin console expansion — COMPLETE, pushed and deployed to production

**Direct user request, spanning Phase 16-17**: turn the internal admin panel into a real, Shopify-shaped business console — vertical icon nav, genuinely useful for running the business, not just an ops dashboard. Every item below was researched before building (Shopify's real Polaris/nav structure, `agy` CLI architecture research, live Resend API testing rather than guessing constraints).

**No separate admin login, by design** — `/admin` reuses the exact same InsForge session as the rest of the app (`lib/admin/auth.ts`'s `requireAdmin()`), gated purely by an `admin_users` row tied to the logged-in `user_id`. No second credential store; confirmed directly to the user when asked.

**Current admin sidebar (`components/admin/AdminSidebar.tsx`)**: Home, Users, Team & Roles, Expenses, Content, Support, Marketing — one entry per shipped section, extend only as new pages actually ship.

### What's built — full detail lives in `progress-tracker.md`'s many Phase 16/17 entries, this is the map

1. **Team & Roles** (`/admin/team`, Phase 16) — `role`-gated admin invites (`owner`/`admin`/`support_readonly`), `requireRole()` enforced on every write action.
2. **Expenses** (`/admin/expenses`) — `business_expenses` + `ai_cost_rates` (seeded with real current per-action rates for the 6 `UsageAction`s with genuine external cost), joined against `usage_daily`.
3. **Content/CMS** (`/admin/content` + public `/blog`) — `pages` table, `react-markdown`, AI-first-draft button, `MarkdownContent.tsx` shared between admin preview and public render.
4. **SEO redirects** — plain `next.config.ts` array, no DB table, seeded with 2 real renamed routes (`/pipeline`→`/missions`, `/agent`→`/dashboard`).
5. **Support** (`/admin/support` + Settings → Contact support) — `support_tickets`/`support_ticket_messages` (the one table pair with REAL RLS, not service-role-only, since users manage their own tickets). Extended with: real email round-trip via Resend (outbound reply-as-email + inbound email-to-ticket, both genuinely wired but INERT until a domain is verified — confirmed live that Resend restricts pre-domain sends to the account owner's own email), manual ticket creation, delete, reassign-to-any-admin, click-to-edit subject, an SLA/stats dashboard (open/pending/resolved counts, avg first-response time, SLA breach count at a 24h default threshold), and an AI-draft-reply button (live-tested — good drafts, but caught inventing an ungrounded "5MB" file-size limit once, a real disclosed LLM gap, which is why replies stay draft-then-review, never auto-send).
6. **Marketing** (`/admin/marketing`) — `marketing_broadcasts` with real per-user unsubscribe tokens, Inngest-chunked batch send, a real CAN-SPAM gate (refuses to send without `MARKETING_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS` configured — live-verified it actually blocks). Extended with: AI-first-draft button (same pattern as Content), real behavioral segmentation off `usage_daily` (all/active-7d/inactive-30d, math hand-verified against live data), and click/open-analytics scaffolding (Resend tag-based, same inert-until-domain constraint).
7. **Push notifications** — second Marketing broadcast channel, **genuinely usable today unlike email, and now live in production** (VAPID needs no external account/domain, just a generated keypair — confirmed inlined into the real deployed bundle). `push_subscriptions` (real user-owned RLS), a service worker, a Settings tab, an admin send-immediately form with its own AI-draft button (structured title/body JSON output, respects the real 50/120-char push length limits). Live-verified further than email could: a real `fcm.googleapis.com` subscription was captured and a real send completed cleanly via a locally-started Inngest dev server.
8. **Admin Navigator** — a separate floating AI chat for owner/admin, grounded in real admin data (support/usage/expenses/broadcasts). Checked `lib/agentAssistant.ts`'s full architecture first (a duplicate Navigator chat was built and torn out once already in Phase 16 — the standing lesson) and confirmed this is a genuinely different data domain, not a duplicate. Read-only/drafting-only in v1, no mutation actions (a real v2 candidate, not an oversight). Also fixed a real gap along the way: consumer Navigator's FAB had been showing on every `/admin` page all session — now excluded via `isAdminRoute()`. Live-verified with 2 real Gemini calls (an accurate support/user-count answer, a real broadcast draft).

### Real environment gotchas from this stretch, worth knowing next session

- **Two chats can't share a dev server port, but you don't need to fight it.** Next.js 16/Turbopack refuses a second `next dev` instance in the same directory at all, even on an explicit different port. If another session's server is already running, drive the Browser pane at that same `localhost:3001` directly (same on-disk code, same hot-reload) instead of spinning up a second instance.
- **Inngest needs a local dev server to test any background function** (`marketing/broadcast.send`, `push/broadcast.send`, etc.) — `INNGEST_DEV=1` serves nothing by itself. Start one fresh each session that needs to verify anything Inngest-triggered: `npx inngest-cli dev -u http://localhost:3001/api/inngest` (backgrounded; check `localhost:8288` for its own dashboard/API once up).
- **Resend's real pre-domain sandbox restriction, confirmed via a live API call, not assumed**: sends from `onboarding@resend.dev` only deliver to the Resend ACCOUNT OWNER'S own verified email, rejecting even this app's own real test-account email with a 403. Applies to both Support's outbound reply email and Marketing's broadcast send — both stay inert until a real domain is purchased and verified in Resend.
- Stale-Turbopack-graph recurred once this stretch (phantom syntax error not matching real file content) — a full `.next` cache wipe was needed, a bare restart wasn't enough.

### Not yet done — real, still-open items

- **Production env vars still needed, not yet set in Vercel**: `SUPPORT_FROM_EMAIL`/`SUPPORT_INBOUND_DOMAIN`/`MARKETING_FROM_EMAIL`/`MARKETING_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS`/`RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` all stay unset until the user owns a domain — that's fine, every feature needing them degrades safely (support/marketing email + inbound webhooks stay inert, confirmed live both locally and in production). Push's own VAPID keys are DONE — see above.
- **Standing note for later, not now**: once subscription/monetization (§J, deferred to the end of the roadmap) ships, the user wants subscription management added for users afterward — noted, not started.

## Next session starts here — direct user instruction ("we will start with this listed items here")

**Direct user request, raised right after the admin console expansion shipped**: make the whole admin panel — especially Marketing and Support — "AI Driven and AI heavy." Researched via `agy` (deep pass) plus independent `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` verification of `agy`'s specific claims before repeating any of them (one claim `agy` made turned out to be materially wrong — see the Canva section below). Nothing below is built yet; this is the queue, in the priority order the research landed on.

1. **Programmatic SEO / GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) content engine — highest ROI, start here.** An Inngest cron job triggers Gemini to draft structured, schema-rich "Job Market Report"/"Interview Guide" pages from the app's own real job-market data (already collected, no new ingestion needed), landing in a "Review & Publish" queue in `/admin/content` (the `pages` table already exists and already supports draft/published + meta fields — this is a content-generation pipeline feeding an existing system, not a new one). Job search is a long-tail, AI-search-dominated query space (Perplexity/ChatGPT/Gemini overviews) — structured extractable content is a real acquisition channel a solo founder can actually execute alone.
2. **"Success Story" content repurposing pipeline.** Trigger: a user's `application_events`/`compensation_events` show a real milestone (landed a job, etc.) — Inngest + Gemini drafts a 3-part thread / LinkedIn post from their (anonymized) journey into a new "Social Drafts" approval inbox in `/admin/marketing`. Same "AI drafts, human approves" discipline as everything else built this session.
3. **Signal-based outreach automation** — lower priority, needs a paid third-party enrichment API (Clay/Apollo) this app doesn't have yet. Don't start without that decision first.
4. **AI-personalized referral/growth-loop copy** — smallest, lowest priority, no referral system exists yet to attach it to.

**Canva integration for posters/social images — researched, a real external gate found, nothing built.** The old embeddable "Canva Button" (iframe editor inside the app) is confirmed dead — sunset outside China as of Dec 30, 2025, no new signups accepted (`agy` got this part right, independently confirmed via `WebSearch`). The only remaining paths both need something only the user can unlock:
   - **Private integration** (no Canva app-review needed) — but genuinely requires a paid **Canva Enterprise plan**. `agy`'s report claimed no Enterprise plan was needed for a "private/internal" integration; that's WRONG — independently checked against Canva's own current docs and Enterprise is the real gate here, not app review. Flagging this clearly since it changes the actual cost/feasibility picture `agy` presented.
   - **Public integration** — free to build, but needs Canva's own app-review/approval before it can go live for real users — a genuine external approval process, same shape as the email-domain-verification blocker already documented above.
   - **No code was written for either path** — building OAuth scaffolding for something gated on a cost decision or an external review queue isn't the same "build now, activate later with zero code changes" pattern used for email/push (those needed a domain the user will eventually just buy; Canva needs an ongoing subscription or a multi-week review, a bigger decision).
   - **Available today with zero new engineering**: this session has a live, connected Canva MCP tool (visible in the tool list) — real posters/social images can be generated on request right now, in-conversation, without needing either Canva gate. Offered to the user as an immediate option; not yet exercised. A good first move next session if the user wants to see real output before committing to either paid path.

**Test account credentials**: `.env`'s `TEST_ACCOUNT_EMAIL`/`TEST_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD` (not `.env.local`) — sign in via the plain email/password form below the OAuth buttons on `/login`. Also saved to cross-session memory (`reference_test_account_credentials.md`).

**Not yet deployed to production**: commit `d400353` (Team & Roles) is pushed but not deployed — deploy it before or alongside whichever of items 1-6 above ships next, matching this session's established pattern of deploying in small batches rather than one giant wave at the end.

Two real bugs found and fixed live, not assumed: (1) `app_settings` needed a read-only RLS policy added — the other admin tables' "zero policies" lockdown doesn't work here since every normal user's own client needs to read the global flag; (2) a hydration mismatch in the join-date formatter (`toLocaleString` renders "PM" server-side vs "p.m." client-side — a real Node/browser ICU divergence, not fixed by pinning locale, only by dropping to a 24-hour clock). **A real stale-Turbopack-graph gotcha recurred** (Phase 9's documented bug class) — an invalid `export type` from a `"use server"` file survived a source fix, a file-touch nudge, AND a hard browser reload; only a full dev-server restart cleared it. If a "use server" file's error message doesn't match what's actually in the file anymore, restart the dev server before debugging further, don't trust the error.

**A real InsForge platform outage hit mid-session** (~20:08 GMT-ish, same day) — `db query`/`diagnose` intermittently 500/502/503 (some with a native CLI crash), the live app itself logged the test account out mid-session. Matches the Phase 13 precedent exactly: resolved on InsForge's own side over a few minutes, no app-side fix needed. If `db query` or the live app start failing broadly again, run `diagnose` first (checks DB connections/cache-hit/recent-errors directly) before assuming app code — a clean `diagnose` with 0 recent errors across all log sources is a good signal the core DB is fine even if the CLI itself is still flaky.

**Practice Sandbox rework — committed and pushed** as commit `f11c750` on `origin/feature/my-experiment` (not yet deployed to production). Direct user feedback opening the session: "I didn't like the way we have implemented the coding practice." Researched via `agy` (twice — once for the grading/UX critique, once for concrete Java/C# implementation feasibility) before writing any code, per this project's standing research-then-build pattern. Shipped and live-verified this phase:
1. **Strict pass/fail grading** replacing the old "soft verification, eyeball both outputs" design — `lib/practiceSandbox.ts`'s `gradeAnswer()` runs the reference solution as runtime ground truth, compares exactly against the candidate's output. Live-verified end to end on a real Stripe technical question: starter code correctly graded 1/2 (the unimplemented placeholder legitimately fails one real test, coincidentally passes the trivial one), then after fixing the code in the live editor, re-ran and got 2/2 — confirmed via the actual DOM, not assumed.
2. **Console tab separating captured stdout from the graded return value** — live-verified with a real `console.log` call showing up in the Console tab while the Result tab kept showing the graded value untouched.
3. **localStorage persistence** — live-verified by editing code, confirming the exact edited value was written to `localStorage` under the right key, then doing a REAL full page reload and confirming the editor hydrated from storage instead of reverting to `starterCode`.
4. **A real Ruby gotcha found AND fixed via live isolated-Worker testing, not just reasoning from decompiled source**: `DefaultRubyVM` has no public stdout-callback API, so `console.log` must be overridden *before* the VM is created (its default printer captures a function reference once, at creation time) — verified this actually works by running the exact worker source standalone in the browser, not by inference. Caught a second real issue in the same test: Ruby's own startup boilerplate goes through `console.warn`, not `console.log` — an initial version that captured both leaked "RubyGems were not loaded" etc. into the Console tab on every run. Fixed to only capture `console.log`.
5. **A real, separate pre-existing bug fixed** (flagged in Phase 15, not fixed there): behavioral-question study cards could crash with a raw Zod validation error dump shown to the user, when the AI over-generated a `redFlags`/`edgeCases` array past its schema's `.max()` limit. Fixed at the root (`.max()` → `.transform(slice)`, truncate instead of reject) and defensively (`lib/errors.ts`'s `toUserMessage()` now has an explicit `ZodError` case, so no future schema-validation failure anywhere in the app can leak raw JSON to a user either). Verified by actually running both fixes against a real over-long array, not assumed from reading the Zod API.

**Java/C# were both requested this session too** ("include java, js and c# as well") — researched in real depth, **both explicitly skipped/parked by direct user decision, not built.**
- **Java**: licensing is genuinely resolved free (CheerpJ's Community License covers "commercial applications developed by one-person companies... whether they generate income or not" — confirmed against their real license page; Sortie is solo). But live-testing hit a real wall: compiling raw Java source needs a `tools.jar` file Leaning Technologies doesn't host publicly (user was going to provide one from their own JDK 8 install, never got to it), and more importantly, **CheerpJ's compile-and-run flow only works on the main thread — confirmed live to fail every time inside a Web Worker** (identical code, only that one variable changed: `compileExit: 0` main thread vs `1` in a Worker), conflicting with this app's rule that every sandbox language stays isolated off the main thread. Full CDN/API reference (loader URL, exact working `cheerpjInit`/`cheerpjAddStringFile`/`cheerpjRunMain` calls) recorded in `progress-tracker.md`'s Phase 16 entry — don't re-derive from scratch if this gets picked up again.
- **C#**: no free client-only path exists at all. Would need a self-hosted Piston judge on a free Oracle Cloud Always Free VM (real VM, root/Docker access, 2 OCPU/12GB RAM as of an Aug 2026 spec change) — provisioning that VM is a real account-creation step this agent can't do. Zero code written.
- **Don't restart either without being asked** — both are genuinely parked, not just deprioritized mid-task.

## What shipped this session (Phase 15), in order

**Tier 1 (fully done):**
1. Per-job Application History panel — `application_events`/`interview_events`/`compensation_events` were only ever written from the job detail page, never read back on it. New `listJobEventHistory` + `ApplicationHistory.tsx`.
2. Tags & personal notes on jobs — `jobs.tags`/`jobs.personal_notes`, `JobTagsAndNotes.tsx`, chips on `JobResultCard`.
3. Interview debrief capture — `logInterviewEvent` existed since §Q1 with zero UI ever calling it; new `InterviewDebrief.tsx`.
4. Job card quick-action "Generate Résumé" — pattern-matched to proven sibling code, not independently click-verified (a browser-automation ref issue hit a filter toggle instead during testing).
5. Recently Viewed jobs — `after()`-based fire-and-forget write on the job-detail page, `RecentlyViewed.tsx` widget on `/find-jobs`.

**Tier 2 (fully done):**
6. Deadline tracker / application calendar — `jobs.next_deadline_at`/`next_deadline_label`, deliberately separate from `interview_events` (a log of the past, not a schedule of the future). `JobDeadline.tsx` + `UpcomingDeadlines.tsx`.
7. "Job requirements split" and "match sub-scores" — turned out already shipped; corrected the stale `build-plan.md` markers instead of re-building.
8. "Should I apply?" quick verdict — deterministic, zero AI cost, synthesizes grade/legitimacy/scope-mismatch/listing-staleness into one Apply/Consider/Long shot/Skip verdict at the top of the job page (`lib/applyVerdict.ts`).
9. Command palette (Cmd+K) — `components/ui/CommandPalette.tsx`, global shortcut + navbar button, full keyboard nav. Caught and fixed 2 real bugs (an eslint `setState`-in-effect error, and a live-verified stale-query-on-reopen bug).

**An unplanned but user-driven detour, triggered by direct questions about the extension:**
10. Extension source-tagging fix — every extension capture (any of the 10 supported sites) and the manual-paste flow were all collapsing into the same generic `jobs.source = "url"`, and `job.source` was never rendered anywhere. Fixed end to end, surfaced as brand-colored "via {Platform}" badges + a Missions Source filter.
11. Real brand colors + real logos for LinkedIn/Indeed (hit and fixed a real `unavatar.io` 429-block along the way — Indeed's badge now fetches straight from `indeed.com`'s own favicon).
12. 8 more job-board integrations for the extension (SimplyHired, Dice, CareerBuilder, RemoteOK, Monster, We Work Remotely, Built In, ZipRecruiter) — most via a new shared `extractFromJsonLd()` helper, 3 via bespoke DOM selectors, all confirmed against real live pages. Monster's selectors are the one exception — genuinely not independently verified (hit its bot-detection wall mid-research), built on CareerBuilder's confirmed pattern instead as a documented best-effort.

**Tier 3 (all shipped except 3 genuinely-blocked items):**
13. Dashboard redesign v1 — bento-grid `/dashboard` replacing the flat 4-stat-tile layout: `AIActionCenter.tsx` (deterministic, not a live AI call), `PipelineFunnel.tsx` (links into a pre-filtered Missions list via a new `?stage=` param), `ActivityHeatmap.tsx` (hand-built 12-week grid, no new dependency), `UpcomingInterviews.tsx`/`RejectionRadar.tsx` (reuse-only).
14. Dashboard redesign v2 — a visual-hierarchy pass, triggered by direct user feedback that v1 "is not looking as I thought." Researched via `agy`; kept the legitimate widget-craft techniques (micro-bar funnel glow, receding heatmap zero-cells) but substituted agy's ambient-gradient "hero wash" suggestion for this project's own established token-step approach (that exact gradient technique was already tried and reverted 2026-07-27 after real Raycast reference research). Also fixed a real Agent-content color Invariant violation on Rejection Radar (was warning-orange, should have been agent-teal — it displays genuinely AI-generated content). Hit and fixed a 4th confirmed instance of the documented Lightning CSS `color-mix()` gotcha along the way.
15. Practice Sandbox inline code editor — the last unblocked Tier 3 item. Monaco (`@monaco-editor/react`, the only real new npm dependency) + client-side execution, expanded same-session from 2 to 5 languages (JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Ruby, SQL) per direct user follow-up request. TS transpiles via a CDN-loaded real TypeScript compiler; Python/Ruby/SQL each run via a real WASM port (Pyodide/ruby.wasm/sql.js) loaded from CDN inside a Worker — none are npm dependencies, their real payload is WASM/data files npm doesn't bundle either. SQL needed a real architecture adjustment (it doesn't fit the "call a function with fixed args" shape the others share — `runSQL()` now runs the whole editable buffer as one script and returns the last statement's result). "Soft verification" only: runs the candidate's code and the AI's real reference solution against the same input, shows both side by side, never a pass/fail grade. **C#/Java researched twice via `agy` and deliberately NOT built** — both need either a 20-30MB client-side compiler payload or a real server-side execution API (Piston/Judge0); no lighter-weight subset/transpile option exists for either language (confirmed via a dedicated second research pass hunting specifically for one). A real, separate pre-existing bug was found (not fixed, spawned as a background task): behavioral-question study cards can crash with a raw Zod validation error when the AI over-generates a `redFlags` array past its schema max. Gating decision (from the original 2-language build): researched via `agy` whether to gate visibility by the viewing user's own profile background — no, real coding-practice products gate by question category, never by inferred profile signals (a documented anti-pattern with real false-negative risk). Every new execution engine (TS/Ruby/SQL) independently verified with real running code before being wired into the UI, not assumed.

**What's left**: the other 3 Tier 3 items are genuinely blocked on decisions only the user can make, not just unstarted — see the Tier 3 line further down for the same detail:
- **Comp benchmarking** — no free/cheap salary data source exists (already researched and ruled out).
- **Voice interview simulator** — needs a real pricing go/no-go (genuine per-minute API cost, not free).
- **Passive market-watch** — needs the pipeline itself rescoped before any UI work makes sense.

Beyond those 3, the entire Tier 1-3 backlog from this session's start is now done. Next session should either tackle one of those 3 blocked items (after getting the needed decision from the user) or move into whatever comes after Tier 3 in the broader roadmap (admin panel §R, monetization §J + design revamp — both still explicitly deferred per the standing sequencing decision, see further down this file).
