**A real, previously-latent bug was found and fixed this session while testing §Q5**: `addExternalJob`'s (now `lib/externalJob.ts`'s `createExternalJob`) `inngest.send()` call had no error handling — a send failure threw and made the whole call read as failed, even though the job row had already saved successfully. This bug predates this session (same code, just relocated during the §Q5 refactor) and would have silently affected the existing "paste a job from anywhere" flow too under the same failure condition. Now wrapped in try/catch, fire-and-forget, matching the pattern already used elsewhere (`addAccomplishment`'s Inngest trigger, the capture route's own `last_used_at` update).

**Four real environment gotchas hit this session, all worth knowing before the next one:**
1. **The session's own auto-mode Bash classifier blocked plain `mv`/`mkdir` calls used for the documented migrations-tracking workaround** (moving `migrations/*.sql` files aside so `db migrations up --all` only sees the new file) — retried via the PowerShell tool instead and it worked with the exact same commands, twice more this session. If Bash gets blocked on a benign file-move/mkdir inside the repo, try PowerShell before concluding the operation itself is unsafe. (Also: two of this session's three new tables — `star_stories.interview_event_id`'s column and `user_api_keys` — didn't need this workaround at all, since neither statement's DDL referenced `auth.users(id)` directly; plain `db query` worked fine for both. The workaround is only needed for the auth-FK case.)
2. **This session's dev server on port 3001 was started by a different, concurrently-active chat session, not this one** — worked fine for a while (hot-reloaded this session's own file edits correctly), then **stopped mid-session** (the other session's process exited) with no warning beyond `navigate` starting to fail — check `netstat -ano | grep :3001` before assuming a dead-looking preview is this session's own fault, and restart it yourself via `preview_start` with the `job_pilot_dev` launch config if so. Real Inngest background-job testing separately needs a local Inngest Dev Server (`npx inngest-cli dev -u http://localhost:3001/api/inngest`, since local dev runs `INNGEST_DEV=1` and nothing serves that by default) — without one running, `inngest.send()` fails with `ECONNREFUSED` (caught safely by the try/catch fix above, but the background function itself never fires). Start one fresh each session that needs to verify anything Inngest-triggered; don't assume a prior session's is still up.
3. **`db query` DELETE statements hit a transient CLI crash twice this session** (`OSS request failed: 502` + a Node/libuv `Assertion failed` native crash), both times on the exact same statement that succeeded cleanly on an immediate retry — `SELECT`s were unaffected. Root cause unconfirmed (didn't reproduce a third time to isolate it); if a `db query` DELETE fails this way, just retry once before assuming a real backend outage.
4. **Sleep/hibernate/monitor-timeout were disabled on this machine this session, per direct user request** (`powercfg /change standby-timeout-ac 0` etc., all four AC+DC variants) — the machine will no longer sleep or blank its screen on its own. Not reverted; leave as-is unless the user asks.

Fixed by excluding the FAB from the outside-click check too, same as the panel. **General lesson, applies to any future toggle-trigger + click-outside-to-close component**: the trigger element must be excluded from the outside-click check.

**Real migration-tooling gotcha hit twice this session, recorded in InsForge project memory (`migrations-tracking-was-never-used-plus-db-query-auth-fk-rejection`) — read that memory before touching migrations again, and expect to repeat the workaround on every future migration, not just once.** This project's remote `db migrations` tracking table had 0 applied migrations despite ~48 tables already live (all prior schema changes were actually applied historically via `db query` raw SQL, migration files kept only as documentation). `db migrations up --all` demands validating and applying EVERY local file in `migrations/` in order — any file older than the current remote head but never itself remote-applied errors out ("X is older than remote head and not applied remotely"), it is NOT silently skipped. **This was wrongly assumed fixed after the first migration** (`20260817162834`) — the very next migration (`20260817170948`) hit the identical blocker. The workaround has to be repeated every time until the ~48 legacy untracked files are dealt with some other way: temporarily move every `migrations/*.sql` file except the new one to a sibling directory, run `db migrations up --all` (applies just the new file, extends the tracked head), then move the historical files back. Separately: `db query` explicitly rejects any DDL whose column `REFERENCES auth.users(id)` ("rejected for security reasons") — any table referencing the auth schema must go through `db migrations`, not `db query`, even for a read-only FK reference.

**Admin panel — researched in depth this session, explicitly NOT built, logged in full in `build-plan.md` §R.** User wants it UI-heavy, multi-admin-capable (someone else can run it while the founder is away), and structured for future tool integrations. Two independent research passes (a user-supplied Perplexity write-up, then a live `agy` cross-check grounded in this app's real InsForge/Next.js stack) produced genuinely different architectures — agy's original "single hardcoded admin email, no audit log" advice was explicitly overridden by the user once multi-admin access was in scope. Synthesized v1 architecture: `admin_users` table (real multi-admin auth, not an env var) + `admin_audit_log` (kept in, against agy's original minimal advice) + a service-role-key client bypassing RLS entirely inside `/admin` (agy's core insight, kept) + a real dashboard UI (not "unstyled data tables") + route-grouped modules under `/admin` for future integrations. Read §R in full before starting — do not start building it without being asked, per the sequencing decision above.

**One tool-install note**: `github.com/DeusData/codebase-memory-mcp` (a native code-intelligence MCP server) was attempted but its Windows installer fails with "activation could not reserve exclusive access" — ruled out leftover lock files, stray processes, admin elevation, and OneDrive redirection, all clean. Left unresolved, skipped per explicit user direction. Don't re-attempt without a different approach (e.g. the manual MCP-config path in its README, bypassing the native `install` command's activation step) or new information from their GitHub issues.

Date Posted is the only one that costs a real SerpApi call (goes to the `chips` param), debounced 700ms so settling on an option only fires once. **3 real bugs caught live by the user, not code review**: the "More filters" panel's outside-click listener checked the wrong DOM container and closed on any click inside it (fixed by relying on the already-correct shared `FilterPanel` check instead of a duplicate buggy one); a genuinely empty Date-Posted-narrowed search hit "Insforge upsert did not return any saved jobs" (empty result treated as a hard failure — fixed in `lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts` to return `[]` early, same empty-state-not-error principle as the SerpApi-level fix); the visa-toggle switch read as "just changes color" with no visible sliding knob (fixed with explicit positioning + a bordered/shadowed knob).

New `lib/careerTimeline.ts` groups accomplishments under their parent work-experience role via date-range containment (auto-computed — no manual role picker); `CareerTimeline.tsx` rebuilt around collapsible per-role `EpochCard`s plus separate Unassigned/Education/Job-Search-Activity sections; a new inline `QuickAddBar` (single input + Enter, no modal, always dated today) closes the page's old "no way to add anything from itself" gap. **Live-verified with real seeded test data** (InsForge CLI raw SQL — 2 roles, 3 accomplishments spanning both ranges plus one pre-career date, all correctly bucketed; cleaned up after).
9. **`formatDate()` timezone bug fixed** — the real, separate bug flagged during the Career restructure. Bare `"YYYY-MM-DD"` calendar-date strings (accomplishment dates, work-experience start/end dates) were parsed as UTC midnight then rendered in local time, rolling the displayed day back by one on any machine behind UTC. Fixed by detecting a bare date string and parsing via the local-time `Date(y, m, d)` constructor instead — real timestamps (`found_at`, `application_status_updated_at`) are untouched, since converting those to the viewer's local time for display was already correct. Live-verified: a seeded "2022-07-01" start date now shows "July 1, 2022", not "June 30".

Test job reverted after. **v1 scope only** — STAR Story Matrix, Trap Door Predictor, and the Interrogation Plan are explicitly deferred, see `build-plan.md` §N.

9. **A real InsForge platform outage hit mid-session** (~21:42 GMT, confirmed via direct network unreachability to the backend host, InsForge's own automated "InstanceDown" alert email, and a 404 on their own dashboard for a verified-correct project ID) — affected production and local dev identically, resolved on InsForge's own side with no app-side fix needed. **Not a Sortie bug** — if you see "We could not start that sign-in method" or jobs stuck on "Scoring…" again, rule out a live InsForge incident (`curl` the backend host directly) before assuming it's app code.

**Phase 11 (2026-08-13) — job-scoring poll fixed live on production, a 3-key SerpApi fallback chain wired in, professional error-handling sweep shipped, uploaded résumé slots got full editing parity with tailored résumés, a standing mobile-responsive rule was added after a real user-caught bug, and every résumé/cover-letter AI generation prompt now bans AI-writing tells and follows Google's XYZ bullet formula.** Full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s top entries. Fast map:
1. **Real production bug, fixed and live-verified**: `getJobsByIds` (`lib/actions/scraper.actions.ts`) used an unauthenticated anon-key client instead of the cookie-authenticated `createInsforgeServer()` — RLS on `jobs` silently returned zero rows on every 3-second poll tick, which is why cards stayed on "Scoring…" until a full page reload. Deployed and confirmed live with real searches.
2. **SerpApi fallback — now a 3-key chain**, not just one fallback. `lib/jobScraper.ts`'s `serpApiProvider` tries `SERPAPI_KEY` → `SERPAPI_KEY_FALLBACK` → `SERPAPI_KEY_FALLBACK_2` (three separate SerpApi accounts) in order, advancing only on a real quota-exhaustion error (text-detected, since SerpApi returns HTTP 200 + a `data.error` string) — any other failure surfaces immediately rather than burning every account's quota on one doomed request. All three keys registered in both `.env` and Vercel production env. **DataForSEO researched via `agy` as the real longer-term second *data source*** (same Google Jobs structured data, pay-as-you-go, no subscription — not yet implemented, needs a funded account) — the 3-key chain buys time, it doesn't replace that. **Jooble live-tested with a real key and rejected**: country-level-only location (`"Canada"`, never the actual city, confirmed on 2 separate real queries) and apply links that always redirect through `jooble.org` instead of the employer's page — doesn't fit this app's city-precision filtering or its "prefer the employer's own link" rule. Full comparison in `build-plan.md` §25.
3. **Error-handling sweep** — new `lib/errors.ts` (`toUserMessage()`, denylist-based: only rewrites text that looks like a leaked vendor/technical error, passes through this codebase's already-good hand-authored copy unchanged), new `app/error.tsx` root boundary, SerpApi's "no results" now returns `[]` instead of throwing (a real empty-state, not a failure — `FindJobsForm.tsx` got an actual empty-state message for it), ~14 API routes/actions routed through the normalizer instead of leaking raw vendor text or a cold "Internal server error". Verified via mocked tests + `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
4. **Uploaded résumé slots now have the same Editor/Style/ATS-score workspace tailored résumés have** — new `resumes.sections`/`style` columns, `lib/resumeSections.ts`'s `buildSectionsFromExtractedData` adapter, `EditorTab.tsx` refactored to accept an injected bullet-rewrite function instead of a hardcoded `jobId`. New `ResumeSlotWorkspace.tsx`. Deliberately excludes the job-specific fit-score gauge/chat/regenerate (no job to revise against) — whole-résumé Quality Grade surfaced instead. **2 real bugs caught live by the user's own testing, not code review**: the ATS card's combined score silently caps at 50/100 with no keyword data (every résumé slot), making a perfectly-clean résumé read as "High risk" with zero explanation; and a zero-formatting-issues result showed only a bare checkmark with no confirming text. Both fixed in `ATSAuditCard.tsx`. Also, per 2 rounds of direct user feedback: the preview shows the real original uploaded PDF first (never silently substituting the AI-reconstructed version), and stays reachable afterward via an in-place "Live edit"/"Original upload" toggle (not a new-tab link — the first version used one, changed after feedback). `ResumeManager` (upload/sync/list) also moved onto the Profile page, always visible above the tab bar (not its own tab — moved there after feedback too). Verified with a real seeded test résumé (InsForge CLI, since this environment's browser automation can't drive a native file picker) plus `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
5. **Mobile-responsive bug fixed, now a standing rule, not a one-off.** `ResumeManager.tsx`'s résumé list was a plain `<table min-w-[640px]>` in an `overflow-x-auto` wrapper — user caught live that reaching the row-actions menu ("Sync to Profile", etc.) required horizontal scrolling on a phone. Fixed with a real `sm:hidden` stacked-card layout carrying the same data/actions (table now `hidden sm:block`); the shared menu-open state needed a `view: "mobile" | "desktop"` tag since both layouts render simultaneously in the DOM (one CSS-hidden) and would otherwise both react to the same open state. New "Mobile / Responsive" section in `ui-rules.md` makes this a standing rule for every future component — check any new list/table/grid at a real 375px width before calling it done. **Scope explicitly narrowed by the user mid-session**: not a full-app mobile audit this session (other pages "seem accessible" per the user's own spot check) — just this real bug plus the standing rule for future builds.
6. **Every résumé/cover-letter AI generation prompt humanized, per explicit user direction.** New `lib/writingStyle.ts`, two shared constants wired into all 4 of `agent/documents.ts`'s generate/revise prompts plus `actions/documents.ts`'s `rewriteResumeBullet` and `actions/resumes.ts`'s `rewriteResumeSlotBullet` (6 call sites total): `HUMANIZED_WRITING_RULES` bans the concrete AI-writing tells pulled directly from Wikipedia's real "Signs of AI writing" essay (fetched live, not recalled from memory) — em dashes for asides, the "delve/boasts/crucial/meticulous/tapestry/pivotal/leverage/foster/seamless"-class vocabulary, "not just X but Y" contrast constructions, vague unquantified "-ing" sentence tails, mechanical rule-of-three lists, copula avoidance ("serves as" instead of "is"). `BULLET_QUALITY_RULES` applies Google's XYZ formula ("Accomplished X as measured by Y, by doing Z") to work-experience bullets, requires quantifying real achievements with no negative framing, and mirrors the target job posting's own key action words. **Deliberately scoped to actual résumé/cover-letter generation only** — `actions/profile.ts`'s 3 base-profile bullet tools (`rewriteBullet`/`splitBullet`/`generateBullets`) were touched then explicitly reverted per direct user correction: the profile page is source-of-truth data entry, not the polished output surface these rules are for. **Live-verified via a real `generateBullets` call** (Work Experience tab, "Generate bullet options from a note") — output had zero em dashes, zero banned vocabulary, no "not just X but Y" constructions, result-first framing, and correctly did not invent a metric the input note didn't provide.

**Career page (`/career`) deep-dive research logged, 2026-08-13, not built yet** — see `build-plan.md` §E's new research addendum. Via the `agy` CLI (per the tool-priority correction below — the `gemini-mcp-tool` MCP path hit a daily quota mid-session and should NOT be used as a substitute for `agy`, even as a fallback). Per explicit user decision, every idea from the research stays a real build candidate ("we will build all possible features") — agy's kept/rescope/cut verdicts are recorded as context on data-honesty tradeoffs only, not as things to skip. Structural finding worth reading first: the current flat timeline is the wrong shape (career data is epoch-based, accomplishments belong nested inside their role) and the page has no way to add anything from itself — both flagged as the highest-leverage fix before any new feature.

**Phase 9's dev-server-cache issue is resolved** — a real process restart at the start of this session (killed a stray leftover `node` process that had port 3001, started fresh) confirmed the Missions rename, List/Board toggle, and 5-stage status dropdown all work correctly live, zero errors. **The same stale-Turbopack-graph class of bug recurred mid-session** after several rapid edits to `EquityDecoder.tsx` (a compile error citing a closing tag that had already been fixed on disk, confirmed via direct file read + a clean `tsc`) — resolved the same established way, a full stop/restart. If you hit a build error that doesn't match what's actually in the file, restart the dev server before debugging further — don't trust the error until you've ruled this out.

**Dev server always runs on port 3001, not 3000** — `package.json`'s `dev` script now pins `next dev -p 3001` explicitly (previously unpinned, relying on Next's auto-bump-if-3000-taken behavior, which this session found was silently masking a stray leftover process). `.claude/launch.json` matches.

**The app is now live in production** — `https://jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app` (Vercel project `sortie3/job_pilot-experiment`, linked via `.vercel/project.json`, gitignored). Background jobs run on real Inngest Cloud (Vercel-Inngest marketplace integration, auto-injects `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY`/`INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` — no more manually starting a local Inngest dev server). InsForge's `allowed_redirect_urls` was updated to include the production `/callback` and `/login` paths (see the Phase 10 summary below for the exact real bug this fixed) — **don't set this to bare origins without a path, InsForge matches the exact redirect target, not just the domain.** Local dev (`localhost:3001`) is unaffected and still works exactly as before.

**ATS Compatibility Score shipped for résumés AND cover letters** — both zero-cost, always-live, no button/usage-cap, no new DB columns.
- **Résumé** (`lib/atsChecker.ts`'s `analyzeATSFormatting`/`computeATSScore`, `components/documents/ATSAuditCard.tsx`): a deterministic formatting audit (multi-column template risk, non-standard section headers, missing contact fields — computed straight from `ResumeStyle`/`ResumeSection[]`, no AI call) plus a keyword-match score that reuses the existing `scoreJump.matchedKeywords`/`missingKeywords` already computed for the fit-score gauge, rather than a second AI call re-deriving the same thing. Formatting half updates instantly on structural changes; keyword half updates on the same 900ms-debounced autosave round-trip the fit-score gauge already uses.
- **Cover letter** (`analyzeCoverLetterATS`, `components/documents/CoverLetterATSAuditCard.tsx`): fully deterministic (no keyword-reuse equivalent exists for cover letters, so this stays 100% rule-based rather than adding a new AI call) — word count (200-400 sweet spot), whether the letter actually names the company anywhere, a generic-salutation check ("To Whom It May Concern" and variants), and the same multi-column `"split"`-template sidebar risk, confirmed real for cover letters too by reading `CoverLetterPDF.tsx` directly (it genuinely renders a contact/skills sidebar for that one template, not assumed from the résumé behavior).
- **Both live-verified with real, hand-checked math**: résumé case scored exactly 14/100 then 34/100 after a live template switch (see the entry below); cover letter case (a 41-word, "split"-template, no-company-mention, "To Whom It May Concern" letter) scored exactly 15/100 = 100 − 20 (short) − 30 (multi-column) − 20 (no company mention) − 15 (generic salutation).
- **Deployed to production** — both features are live on `jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app`, not just local-dev-verified.
- Cover letters have no existing fit-score/keyword infrastructure the way résumés do, which is exactly why that half was scoped out rather than faked — an honest 100%-formatting score, not a padded one.

**⚠️ If job scoring ever silently stops working on the production deployment again** (`/find-jobs` shows real results but every card is permanently stuck on "Scoring…"), check these three things before anything else, in order — all three bit this session, all silent, none shows an error to the user:
1. `curl https://jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app/api/inngest` — if it returns an open, unauthenticated JSON blob with `"mode":"dev"`, the Inngest client is in local-dev mode in production. Root cause last time: `.env` got uploaded in a `vercel deploy` despite being gitignored, and Next.js loaded its `INNGEST_DEV=1` since nothing else had set that var. `.vercelignore` now excludes it — if this recurs, check `.vercelignore` wasn't reverted/deleted.
2. `curl -X PUT https://jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app/api/inngest` — (re-)registers the app's functions with Inngest Cloud. The Vercel-Inngest integration is supposed to do this automatically on every deploy; it didn't fire reliably this session. Cheap and safe to run after any deploy just in case — confirm via `vercel logs` showing real `POST /api/inngest` invocations after a search.
3. **`vercel logs` showing `Duplicate step ID "..." detected across parallel chains (AUTOMATIC_PARALLEL_INDEXING)`** — a real, now-fixed bug in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`'s per-chunk loop reused the same `step.run`/`step.sleep` ID on every iteration, which Inngest Cloud's stricter engine (unlike the local Dev Server) silently stalls on after the first chunk. Fixed by keying both step IDs on the loop index. **If you ever add a new `step.run`/`step.sleep`/`step.*` call inside a loop in this codebase, its ID MUST include the loop index or another per-iteration-unique value** — this is a real, sharp-edged Inngest requirement that produces zero user-facing error, just permanently stuck jobs, and it only reproduces against the real Cloud engine, not local dev.

**✅ Strategic Moat Briefing (and Interview Panel Topology's interviewer-research lookup) is fixed and live-verified, after 3 compounding root causes:**
1. **The Perplexity API key was dead** (`401 invalid_api_key`, confirmed by calling Perplexity's API directly with the literal `.env` value — failed locally too, not a deployment artifact). User regenerated a fresh key at perplexity.ai/settings/api; pushed to both Vercel's `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY` (removed the dead one first, then added the new one) and local `.env`. Server-only env var, so no redeploy was even needed for this half — Vercel injects it per-request.
2. **The free Jina-Reader-scrapes-a-Google-search-page path was being CAPTCHA-blocked by Google** (confirmed by fetching the exact same URL directly — Google returned a real "unusual traffic detected" wall, ~1000 characters). This specific pattern (build a `google.com/search?q=...` URL, fetch via Jina) is used by `researchStrategicMoat` and `researchInterviewerBackground` in `agent/research.ts` — the main Company Research dossier and Leadership lookup fetch real company/Wikipedia URLs directly instead of scraping Google, so they were never affected. **Deliberately did not attempt to work around Google's CAPTCHA wall itself (rotating user agents, proxies, etc.) — that's scraping-evasion territory this project stays out of.**
3. **The actual code bug, on top of the dead key**: Google's block page (~1000 chars) sailed past the `sourceText.length < 200` "is this real content" check, so the Perplexity fallback never even triggered — even with a working key, the free path silently swallowed the CAPTCHA page as if it were real content. Fixed with a new `looksLikeSearchBlockPage()` helper (`agent/research.ts`, checks for "unusual traffic"/"maybe requiring captcha") added to both call sites' fallback conditions. **Live-verified end to end** after all three fixes: a real Strategic Moat Briefing for Microsoft came back with specific, current, correctly-grounded strategic priorities (Azure/Copilot AI capacity, agentic workflows, custom silicon scaling, AI-led governance) — not generic filler, not an error.
4. **A separate, minor real bug found and fixed in passing**: the Company Research auto-loader crashed with `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'join')` if a job's `matched_skills`/`missing_skills` were `null` instead of `[]` — no null guard on `job.matched_skills.join(...)`. Real scraper→evaluator jobs never hit this (always populated as real arrays); only surfaced via a raw-SQL test job. Fixed with `(job.matched_skills ?? []).join(...)`-style guards.

## Phase 10 session summary (2026-08-12) — Equity & Cap Table Decoder + Post-Offer Leverage Synthesizer shipped (closing out the §M differentiation batch), plus a user-requested US/Canada Tax Calculator

**The two features left unbuilt from Phase 9's differentiation batch are now shipped**, plus a third feature added mid-session at the user's direct request. Full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s top entry; this is the fast map.

1. **Equity & Cap Table Decoder** (`lib/equityDecoder.ts`, `components/job-details/EquityDecoder.tsx`) — pure calculator on user-typed offer numbers (base/signing bonus/bonus target/equity type/shares/strike/FMV/vesting/cliff/total shares outstanding). No AI call, no funding-data lookup. Computes year-1/steady-state comp, grant value, exercise cost, spread (with an underwater warning), ownership %.
2. **Salary Tax & Take-Home Calculator** (`lib/taxCalculator.ts`, `components/job-details/TakeHomeEstimator.tsx`) — added mid-session, not originally scoped. US federal + all 50 states + DC + FICA; Canada federal + 10 provinces + 3 territories + CPP/EI. Single filer / basic personal amount only — bracket data is a best-effort estimate from recently published rates, explicitly disclaimed in the UI as not a filing tool.
3. **Both calculators share one card via tabs** (`OfferWorkspace.tsx`, new) — per direct user request ("make the tab for tax calculator beside equity cap"), using the same `Tabs.tsx` switcher already used for Overview/Company. Each still persists to its own DB column so the two tabs' saves never clobber each other.
3a. **Then made available to every user, not just Offer-stage jobs** (second direct user request, same session) — `OfferWorkspace` moved out of the gated-on-`offered` block and into its own always-visible top-level page tab, "Offer Tools", alongside Overview/Company. `LeverageSynthesizer` deliberately stayed gated to `application_status === "offered"` — it's grounded in real offer-stage timing data (days at the Offer stage) that doesn't exist before an offer is made, unlike the two calculators which anyone can use anytime to plan ahead.
4. **Post-Offer Leverage Synthesizer** (`lib/leverageSynthesizer.ts`, `components/job-details/LeverageSynthesizer.tsx`) — same honesty-scoped shape as `lib/rejectionIntelligence.ts`, grounded only in this job's own stored data (match score, evaluation, missing skills, timing, reappearance signal), system prompt explicitly forbids fabricated market benchmarks. Real AI call (free-tier Gemini), 5/day usage cap. Stays a separate card below the tabs (it's AI output, gets the "Agent read" treatment; the two calculators above deliberately don't).
5. **All three fully live-verified**, not just compile-checked: a disposable test job was inserted via SQL, moved through both calculators and the AI synthesizer with real automation clicks, every computed figure hand-verified against the actual bracket/vesting math (all matched to the dollar), saved, reloaded to confirm persistence, then deleted.
6. **3 new migrations** (`offer_details`, `leverage_synthesis`, `tax_estimate_inputs` — each its own jsonb column pair), all applied live via InsForge CLI first per this project's established pattern.
7. **First real production deployment, per direct user request** (his wife needed browser access from her own laptop). Deployed to Vercel (`sortie3/job_pilot-experiment`, project linked via `vercel link`) — `https://jobpilot-experiment.vercel.app`. Every non-local-only env var copied from `.env` into Vercel's production environment via `vercel env add` (piped through stdin, never printed to a transcript). Inngest switched from local-dev-only mode (`INNGEST_DEV=1`, dummy `"local"` keys — confirmed as the actual cause of "I have to start the ingest server every time") to real Inngest Cloud via the official Vercel-Inngest Marketplace integration (user installed it themselves; auto-injects real `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY`/`INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY`, no manual key copying). **A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not assumed**: OAuth login failed in production (`We could not start that sign-in method`) — `insforge.toml`'s `auth.allowed_redirect_urls` needed the exact `/callback` and `/login` paths, not just the bare origin (confirmed via `vercel logs`, which surfaced InsForge's real underlying error: `".../callback is not in the allowed redirect URLs"`). Fixed and **live-verified end to end** — clicking "Continue with Google" on the production URL now correctly reaches Google's real sign-in page. `config apply` also surfaced a pre-existing, unrelated gap: the Brevo SMTP password was originally set via a raw admin API call (Phase 9), never registered as a named InsForge secret, so any future `config export`→`apply` round-trip involving `[auth.smtp]` will hit `auth.smtp.password references env(SMTP_PASSWORD) but no such secret exists` until someone runs `insforge secrets add SMTP_PASSWORD "<value>"` with the real Brevo password (not currently known/available — get it from the Brevo dashboard under SMTP & API). Worked around this session by omitting `[auth.smtp]` from `insforge.toml` entirely when applying unrelated auth changes — confirmed InsForge's diff/apply treats an omitted section as "leave live state alone," not "reset to defaults." **Wife's account**: per explicit user decision, she'll create her own separate account (not share his), so this was intentionally not pre-provisioned.
8. **ATS-scoring research done, zero code written — a real next-session build candidate.** Researched via `agy` (Gemini) per this project's established research-tool priority: a hybrid architecture (deterministic, zero-cost structural/formatting checks — column layout, font, section-header naming, all things this app already fully controls via `ResumePDF.tsx`'s own template/theme state — plus one grounded Gemini call for keyword-match/quantification scoring, same shape as `lib/evaluator.ts`) rather than any paid third-party ATS API. Full rubric and a draft `lib/atsChecker.ts` came back from the research pass but nothing was implemented — see the conversation for the full architecture if picking this up.
9. **Three more real production bugs found and fixed, all caught live by the user actually using the deployed site, not by me assuming success after a green deploy:**
   - **`.env` was silently leaking into the Vercel build**, forcing the Inngest client into permanent local-dev mode (`mode: "dev"` on `/api/inngest`'s own diagnostic endpoint) even though the real Cloud keys were correctly set in Vercel's dashboard — `dotenv`-style loading doesn't override an *already-set* var, but `INNGEST_DEV=1` wasn't set by Vercel at all, so the uploaded `.env` file was free to define it fresh. Root-caused by directly reading the Inngest SDK's own source in `node_modules` (`components/Inngest.js`'s `get mode()`), not guessed. Fixed with a new `.vercelignore` (`.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.*.local`, `.insforge`) forcing Vercel to never upload these regardless of what `.gitignore` matches, then redeployed — confirmed via the SDK's own diagnostic endpoint flipping from `"mode":"dev"` (open, unauthenticated JSON) to properly requiring signed auth (real cloud behavior).
   - **Inngest Cloud didn't know the app's functions existed yet**, even after the mode fix — real events were being accepted (no more `ECONNREFUSED`) but never actually invoking `/api/inngest` to run `evaluateJobsAsync`, so every job search returned real results with every card permanently stuck on "Scoring…". The Vercel-Inngest Marketplace integration is supposed to auto-sync on every deploy; it either didn't fire or hadn't caught up after either of the two deploys this session. Fixed by manually triggering `PUT /api/inngest` (Inngest's own app-registration endpoint).
   - **A genuine pre-existing bug in `lib/inngest/functions.ts`, only exposed by Inngest Cloud's stricter engine, not the local Dev Server**: `evaluateJobsAsync`'s per-chunk loop called `step.run`/`step.sleep` with the same literal step ID (`"delay-between-ai-calls"`, and a `step.run` label keyed only on `chunk.length`, which collides whenever two chunks are the same size) on every loop iteration. Inngest Cloud logged `Duplicate step ID "..." detected across parallel chains (AUTOMATIC_PARALLEL_INDEXING)` and the function silently stalled after the first chunk — every job past the first 5 in any search stayed on "Scoring…" forever, no error shown anywhere. Fixed by keying both step IDs on the loop index (`evaluate-chunk-${chunkIndex}`, `delay-between-ai-calls-${chunkIndex}`). **This is a sharp, easy-to-reintroduce edge — any future `step.*` call added inside a loop in this codebase needs a per-iteration-unique ID or this exact bug returns.**
   - **All three confirmed fully fixed, live** — not just assumed from a clean deploy. After the step-ID fix + redeploy + re-sync, inserted 7 fresh test jobs (splits into 2 chunks at the existing chunk-size-5), manually sent a real `jobs/evaluate` event via Inngest's Event API (event key extracted through `vercel env pull` into a gitignored temp file, used once, deleted immediately — never printed to the transcript), and confirmed all 7 got real match scores, not just the first 5. Scores populate asynchronously via the existing 3-second client-side poll (`FindJobsForm.tsx`), gated by the same free-tier Gemini rate limit (~15 req/min) this project has hit before during the old backfill script — a large batch from one search will visibly finish "Scoring…" over several minutes, not instantly; that part is expected, pre-existing behavior, not a bug.
   - **Old test jobs from before the step-ID fix are permanently stuck** (their Inngest run checkpointed against the buggy code path) — harmless leftover data in the disposable test account only, safe to ignore or delete, not a real user's data.

10. **`SMTP_PASSWORD` registered as a real InsForge secret, closing out item 7's open gap** — user generated a fresh Brevo SMTP key (the original one's plaintext was unrecoverable, Brevo only shows it once), registered it via `insforge secrets add`, then `config apply` succeeded cleanly for the first time all session. **Live-verified, not assumed**: triggered a real password-reset email through the production site's own UI, confirmed via InsForge's own backend logs (`insforge.logs`) — `"info - Email sent via SMTP", template: "reset-password-code", to: "ridhamicproduction@gmail.com"`, `202` response. Real email delivery, not just a clean API response.

**Committed and pushed** — `f7b4ce2` (Equity/Tax/Leverage + deployment infra), `68d8ba4` (résumé ATS score), and a third commit for the cover-letter ATS score, all on `feature/my-experiment`. Run `git log -1`/`git status` to confirm before assuming otherwise.

**Next session: the "Engagement War Room" Interview section** (`build-plan.md` §N) — researched via 2 `agy` passes on 2026-08-12, not yet built, explicitly sequenced for after this session's Equity/Leverage/ATS work. Three phases: Phase 1 (Reconnaissance) is already done — Strategic Moat Briefing + Interview Panel Topology both ship data into this. Phase 2 (Arsenal) is the next real build: a cached AI Question Bank keyed on `(company, role-family, seniority)` — not per-job, genuinely reusable — explicitly labeled as AI-predicted, never implying real leaked/sourced questions (the honest answer after 2 research passes found no legal/free source for real company interview questions at any scale — Reddit/LeetCode/Blind/Glassdoor all ruled out for real ToS/CFAA reasons, not just inconvenience). Read §N in full before starting — it also scopes the STAR Story Matrix, Interrogation Plan, Trap Door Predictor, and a Phase 3 Live-Fire Simulator.

**Not yet done**: Wife's account still not created. Subscription/pricing model remains deferred to right before launch, unchanged.

Live-verified with real synthetic test data: the model correctly cited the exact fake evaluation grades it was given rather than inventing generic reasons.

Three careful browser-automation attempts (hover-confirmed coordinates, generous drop targets) never registered with `@dnd-kit`'s pointer sensor — a known-hard category for synthetic pointer-event automation, not traced to an app bug (the underlying `setApplicationStatus` call was proven correct via the click-based "Mark as applied" path instead). **Two real test job rows were deliberately left in the test account** (`ridhamicproduction@gmail.com` — credentials in `.env` as `TEST_ACCOUNT_EMAIL`/`TEST_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD`) specifically so a human can try the actual drag next session.

**The single biggest UI-facing stretch in this project's history, and the first phase where the user actually clicked through the work live, in real time, mid-session** — every round below includes at least one real bug or usability problem caught from an actual screenshot, not just `tsc`/`eslint`. Read this section fully before touching the résumé/cover-letter editors, `/resume`, or job listing/status code again. Full blow-by-blow (every round, in the order it actually happened) is in `progress-tracker.md`'s top ~15 entries; component-level detail is in `ui-registry.md`.

### What shipped, by theme (not chronological — see progress-tracker.md for the real order)

**1. Résumé Style/Editor tab visual redesign** — user asked to make it "better, professional, and interactive"; researched via `agy` against the actual component code (not generic advice), naming real competitors (Rezi/Novoresume/Kickresume/Enhancv/Teal/Zety/JobRight). `StyleTab.tsx`: controls grouped into 3 collapsible sections (a small hand-rolled accordion — deliberately not the installed `@base-ui/react`/shadcn set, which resolves through an off-brand oklch palette, confirmed by reading `app/globals.css`); font sizes collapsed into an S/M/L preset; accent color is 8 curated swatches + custom picker; spacing sliders show live pt/line-height readouts (`ResumePDF.tsx`'s exported `mapRange`/`SPACING_RANGES`). `EditorTab.tsx`: bullet textareas borderless-at-rest/bordered-on-hover; `Enter`/`Backspace` bullet shortcuts; placeholder guidance text; length hints. Biggest change: "Edit with AI"/"Regenerate" no longer overwrite a bullet directly — result is a `pending` Was/Now diff card (`BulletDiffCard`) with Accept/Try again/Discard.

**2. Expanded editing controls** (same-session follow-up, "give more editing features and control") — new 5th section type `custom` (preset catalog: Projects/Languages/Awards/Volunteer Experience + a blank escape hatch, one generic `CustomEntry` shape). Real gaps closed: Work Experience title/company/dates were a **read-only label** before this, now real fields; Education entries could not be **added or removed at all**, only edited in place. All entry types now have Add/Duplicate/Remove/drag-reorder (`SortableList` helper, isolated `DndContext` per section). **Certifications promoted to its own first-class 6th section type** (not a custom preset) after the user pushed back — `CertificationEntry {name, issuer, date}`, seeded from `profile.certifications`, mirrors `EducationEntryEditor` exactly.

**3. Three new résumé templates + three new themes** — `ResumeTemplate` is now `structured|centered|split|timeline|executive|block`; `ResumeTheme` is now `modern|classic|minimal|slate|editorial|sage`. `timeline` = dedicated date column per work-entry; `executive` = mirrors `split` but sidebar on the RIGHT with a full-width header above (not inside the sidebar); `block` = solid-color header banner + centered thick-ruled titles. All still strictly ATS-safe (single-column-per-region, no tables/images, standard-14 fonts only).

**4. Cover Letter Editor Workspace** — what started as "share the résumé's style" became a full second workspace after the user clarified they wanted a real editor, not passive style-sharing. Cover letter now shares the tailored résumé's exact `ResumeStyle` (one `applications.resume_style` row per job, not the old global `profiles.preferred_resume_theme`). `CoverLetterPDF.tsx` gained a 3-way structural branch + a `salutation` override (new `cover_letter_salutation` column — recipient line was hardcoded `"Hiring Team, {company}"` before). `CoverLetterWorkspace.tsx` mirrors `ResumeWorkspace`'s shell, sized to a letter's simpler content (Salutation + auto-expanding Body + chat/chips; Style tab reuses `StyleTab` with `documentType="cover_letter"`). New route `app/cover-letter/tailored/[jobId]/page.tsx`. **Deliberately no top-level nav item** (researched via `agy`: unlike `/resume`, a cover letter has no standalone library — real trackers keep this job-scoped) — instead a `DocumentSwitcher.tsx` pill cross-links the two per-job workspaces, and `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s job-details card got real Edit/View buttons for both documents (same shared component, so the "Refine with AI" chat box applies to both automatically too).

**5. `/resume` page redesigned as per-job "document tray" cards** — user asked to show both documents per job, then explicitly said not to skip the status badge. `TailoredResumeCard.tsx` deleted, replaced by `ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx` (researched via `agy`: job is the root entity, documents are attached assets — Teal/Huntr pattern). Header links to the job itself + real `CompanyLogo` + `application_status` badge; a "document tray" below shows résumé + cover-letter pills (solid if generated, dashed "+ Add" ghost slot if not — a missing document reads as an invite). The ghost slot deep-links into `DocumentGenerator.tsx`'s own `?generate=cover_letter` auto-trigger.

**6. Stale/unavailable job-posting detection** (new feature area, user-initiated: "how do we manage postings that are gone") — researched via `agy` (don't re-scrape/HEAD-check external listings, real trackers lean on user action + heuristics), then refined with a genuinely free signal found by reading this app's own code: `scraper.actions.ts` already stamps every search's `agent_runs.id` onto every job it returns, so a job from an exact repeat search missing from the newest run is a real, zero-cost signal. Three tiered signals in `lib/jobStatus.ts`'s `getListingSignal` (`marked_unavailable_at` user-confirmed > `dropped_from_search_at` auto-detected > `isStaleByAge` >45 days, lowest confidence), surfaced as badges in `JobResultCard.tsx`/`JobActionBar.tsx` and a dismissible warning in `DocumentGenerator.tsx` — but **only** while `application_status === "draft"`; once applied or further, nothing is ever disrupted, since the tailored documents may be the only surviving record of what was actually applied to.

### Real bugs caught live this phase (each fixed same-session, not left open)

- **`posted_at` frozen-text bug**: `job.posted_at` is Google Jobs' own relative string ("2 days ago") captured once at scrape time, never refreshed — a job found weeks ago would permanently show "Posted 2 days ago." This also silently broke `isStaleByAge` (`new Date("2 days ago")` = Invalid Date, no-op'd for any job with `posted_at` set). Fixed: `isStaleByAge` only reads `found_at` now; the badge shows a real computed `Found {formatTimeAgo(foundAt)}`.
- **Native `<select>` unreadable in dark mode**: Theme/Page size dropdowns' OS-chrome popup rendered every non-selected `<option>` in washed-out grey, unrelated to this app's CSS. Replaced with a custom `Dropdown` component.
- **That fix's own popup then invisible**: it lived inside `Group`'s `overflow-hidden` accordion wrapper, which silently clipped the absolutely-positioned popup. Fixed by portaling to `document.body` with `position:fixed` computed from the trigger's real `getBoundingClientRect()` — the exact pattern already proven in `ResumeManager.tsx`'s row-action menu.
- **That portal fix then "floated" disconnected on scroll**: `position:fixed` doesn't track the trigger when the side panel's own `overflow-y-auto` scrolls. Fixed by closing the panel on any scroll (`capture:true` on `window`, catches the nested scroll container too).
- **"Click the title to edit" was undiscoverable**: tried twice — first the whole title became a `Link` (only accent-colored on `:hover`, read as plain text at rest); reverted to a plain heading + explicit "Edit"/"View" text links, which then read as unfinished next to a solid button; researched via `agy` and landed on primary(solid)+secondary(outline) button hierarchy — `Edit`/`View` are now real outline buttons.
- **Browser-native `window.confirm`/`window.alert`**: user-flagged as unprofessional. New reusable `components/ui/ConfirmDialog.tsx` (same glass-modal chrome as `SectionModal.tsx`) replaces it everywhere it was used (`ApplicationDocumentsCard.tsx`, `ResumeManager.tsx`, `ResumeAnalysisView.tsx`).

### Verification status

`tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean across every round (checked incrementally). Every touched route confirmed live via the existing port-3001 dev server (clean `307 → /login`, not `500`s) — this is compile-level confirmation, not a full click-through. Given the density of real bugs caught THIS session purely from the user clicking around live, **do not assume anything not explicitly click-tested is correct** — treat every new control (all 6 templates, all 6 themes, drag-reorder, the AI diff card, the Salutation/auto-expanding textarea, the listing-status badges, the new dropdowns) as unverified until someone actually operates it.

### Migrations this phase

- `20260806230000_add-cover-letter-salutation.sql` — `applications.cover_letter_salutation`.
- `20260807010000_add-jobs-listing-status.sql` — `jobs.marked_unavailable_at`, `jobs.dropped_from_search_at`.

Both applied live via InsForge CLI, then written to match, per this project's established migration pattern.

## 30-second state (2026-08-05→06, Phase 7 — Résumé Editor Workspace, current state after 3 follow-up rounds)

**The full `context/build-plan.md` §C1 "Résumé Editor" feature shipped for real** — a live-preview editing workspace for AI-tailored per-job résumés (`app/resume/tailored/[jobId]`), replacing the old "click Generate → get a PDF blind" flow, then extended across three same-topic follow-up rounds driven by real usage/screenshots/a research pass. This is the largest body of work in the project so far — full plan at `~/.claude/plans/jaunty-jingling-bear.md`, full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s top entries (several, newest first), component notes in `ui-registry.md`. **Current shape, read this before touching this area again:**

**Data model**: `applications.resume_sections`/`resume_style`/`updated_at`/`quality_analysis`/`quality_analyzed_at` (two migrations, both applied live then written to match). A tailored résumé is a real independent 4-section snapshot (summary/skills/work_experience/education) from the moment it's generated — editing it never touches `profiles`.

**`ResumePDF.tsx`** takes `{profile, sections, style}` with **3 real, structurally distinct templates** (Structured/Centered/Split — Split is a genuine two-column sidebar, not just a style variant), lives in `components/documents/` along with `CoverLetterPDF.tsx`/`DocumentChatEditor.tsx`. All 3 document routes (`/api/resume/generate`, `/api/documents/{generate,chat}`) share this one shape.

**Workspace UI is 3 tabs — AI Rewrite (default) / Editor / Style**, restructured mid-session to match a real competitor's editor the user shared live screenshots of:
- **AI Rewrite tab**: score gauge (`ScoreGauge`, agent-teal — not a rainbow gradient, matches this app's own AI-score color rule) auto-checked once on workspace mount so it shows by default; `QualityGradeCard` (the 10-dimension whole-résumé grade, deliberately **not** live on every edit — capped 3/day vs. the fit-check's 15/day, only refreshes on demand or after a full Regenerate); `ActionPlan` (a ranked, capped-at-6 list merging urgent/critical bullet issues + missing keywords + weak dimensions, researched via `agy` — impact shown as qualitative badges, never a fabricated "+N pts", since the score is only ever the output of a real LLM call, not a point system); generic "Quick tweaks" chips; free-form chat. Clicking a bullet-issue Action Plan item jumps to the Editor tab and highlights that exact bullet.
- **Editor tab**: drag-to-reorder (`@dnd-kit`, first real drag-and-drop in this codebase), hide/show sections, and **per-bullet "Edit with AI"/"Regenerate"** (built here instead of as a hover-on-the-PDF overlay like the reference — `PDFViewer` renders a real PDF in an iframe, no DOM to hover/overlay).
- **Style tab**: template/theme/font/color/spacing, all live in the preview.

**Score-jump / quality-grade / gap analysis** (`lib/scoreJump.ts`, `lib/resumeQuality.ts`): real gap found mid-build — the fit score was always computed against the base profile, never the tailored résumé, so it could never move when you edited a tailored copy. Now rescores against what's actually in the workspace. The quality-grade AI call/parse/count logic is shared (`lib/resumeQuality.ts`'s `runResumeQualityAnalysis`) between the résumé-slot version (`actions/resumes.ts`) and this tailored version (`actions/documents.ts`) — one prompt, not a duplicate.

**`tsc`/`eslint` clean throughout** every round (checked incrementally, not just once). **Real bugs caught live, not just in code review** — see the gotchas section below for the full list (stale dev-server cache masquerading as a code bug; multiple `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` violations in focus/highlight effects; two separate `useCallback`-dependency staleness bugs where a manual re-check or post-regenerate quality-grade call would have silently scored/graded stale content; a missing try/catch in the per-bullet AI rewrite that left a spinner stuck forever on any thrown error).

**Not click-tested against a live authenticated session** — same standing no-OAuth-here limitation as everything else in this project. Drag-and-drop, live style updates, the Action Plan's jump-to-bullet highlight, and the full generate→edit→save→re-download loop all need a human pass.

## 30-second state (2026-08-05, Phase 7 — résumé sync fix)

**Follow-up to the same-day session below: résumé sync's "additive-only, never upgrades" gap (item 5's third bullet, and the old reorient step 1) is now closed — through two passes, the first one caught as insufficient by real live data within the same session.** `getResumeProfileDiff`/`syncResumeToProfile` (`actions/resumes.ts`) and `ResumeManager.tsx`'s `SyncModal` gained a per-entry "update this?" path for Education/Work Experience — a matched entry whose content has drifted shows as its own checkbox (struck-through old text / teal proposed text), default unchecked, opt-in per entry rather than a silent auto-replace.

**Pass 1** matched Work Experience entries by `company+title` (case/whitespace-normalized). **Immediately proven insufficient**: the user synced a second, differently-worded résumé over an already-synced LinkedIn export, and it duplicated two jobs instead of offering to replace them — "HCL Technologies"/"Full-stack Developer" vs "HCL America Inc"/"Developer" (same job, Gemini just phrased the company and title differently across the two source documents). **Pass 2** re-keyed the match on `start_date` instead (the one field that actually stayed consistent across both real extractions) and changed the replacement preview to show full `"{title} at {company}\n{responsibilities}"` on both sides, not just responsibilities, so the user can visually confirm a match spanning different company/title wording really is the same job. `tsc`/`eslint` clean both passes; **not click-tested against a live authenticated session** — same standing no-OAuth-here limitation, see gotchas below. **The duplicates already written to the live profile from Pass 1 are not auto-cleaned up** — needs manual deletion via Profile → Work Experience tab → trash icon on the duplicate entry. Full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s top entry.

## 30-second state (2026-08-05, earlier session)

**Huge session: profile rebuilt for real as a tabbed Command Center, a full multi-résumé slot system shipped, and a working AI résumé-quality grader built and debugged live against real data — plus a live Chrome-extension scan of JobRight (not just pasted screenshots) that grounded all of it.** Full detail in `progress-tracker.md`'s top entry and `context/jobright-resume-scan-2026-07-30.md`. Fast map:

1. **LinkedIn import — closed, no new code needed.** True "sync" isn't possible outside LinkedIn's enterprise Partner Program, but a real live test proved the *existing* résumé-upload pipeline already parses a LinkedIn "Save to PDF" export correctly end-to-end. Two real gaps that test surfaced got fixed: `education` is now an array (was single-object, silently dropped a second degree), and `certifications text[]` was added (didn't exist before).
2. **Profile page rebuilt as a tabbed "Command Center"** (`ProfileForm.tsx`, `Tabs.tsx`, new `SectionModal.tsx`) — chosen via a real 3-way Gemini design comparison, later confirmed to match JobRight's own live profile tab structure exactly. Real AI bullet tools added: rewrite, split-one-dense-sentence-into-several, generate-from-note. **Real bug found & fixed**: `Tabs.tsx`'s panel was silently shrink-wrapping to whichever tab's content was widest — a plain `w-full` class didn't reliably fix it (this build's known Lightning CSS/utility-layer history), inline `style={{width:"100%"}}` did, confirmed via live computed-width checks.
3. **Multi-résumé slot system shipped** (`actions/resumes.ts`, `ResumeManager.tsx`, new `resumes` table, up to 5 slots/user) — upload → extract → sync-to-profile, sync is additive-only (never overwrites), diff shown before writing.
4. **Résumé quality-analysis feature built**: grade (A-F), Urgent/Critical/Optional counts, a 10-dimension role-fit matrix (reused `JobEvaluationDimension`'s existing shape), a Strategic Narrative insight, an Interviewer Skepticism vulnerabilities list, and per-bullet drill-down (issue → AI rewrite → editable save). One Gemini call produces all of it. New `resume_quality_analysis` usage key (3/day, distinct from the older job-fit-gap `resume_analysis` key — don't confuse them).
5. **Three real bugs found via live debugging, all fixed except the last:**
   - Fixed-bullet-then-reanalyze appeared to show identical issues → `revalidatePath("/resume")` never touched the `/resume/[id]` détail route. Fixed (`revalidatePath` now hits both paths everywhere in `actions/resumes.ts`; both résumé pages are `force-dynamic`).
   - A duplicate work-experience entry appeared after a second sync → Gemini's extraction casing isn't fully consistent run-to-run (`"Wizcraft"` vs `"wizcraft"`), and the dedup key was case-sensitive. Fixed with a `dedupeKey()` helper.
   - **Not yet fixed, explicit next-session work**: sync is additive-only by design, so it never *upgrades* an existing profile entry even when a fresh extraction is much better — old pre-fix data can survive indefinitely. Worked around this session by directly correcting the live test profile's data via a one-off `createAdminClient` script, but the underlying merge logic still needs a real "replace if better" path.

**Also still true from before, unchanged:** the floating Navbar/JobActionBar/FloatingApplyButton layout and the icon-bullet job-details redesign are still type/lint-clean but not click-tested end-to-end (same standing OAuth-login limitation — see gotchas). Email/password auth (shipped 2026-07-29) is **still not live-tested end to end** — nobody has yet tried the real sign-up → receive-code → verify round trip with a real inbox.

## Previous session summary (2026-07-29) — Company logos fixed for real, Perplexity added, Phase 0, account deletion, settings modal, email/password auth

**Huge session, seven distinct threads, all shipped and live-verified except the last one (research only, no code yet).** Full blow-by-blow in `progress-tracker.md`'s top entries (several separate dated bullets, all this same 2026-07-29 session — the first one or two say "2026-07-28" by mistake, a dating error made early on and left as-is with a correction note rather than rewritten). This is the fast map:

1. **Company logos — the REAL fix, finally.** Last session's same-origin-proxy-for-Clearbit fix (see the old note preserved below) was solving the wrong problem. Confirmed live via `curl`/`nslookup`: `logo.clearbit.com` is fully DNS-dead, not ad-blocker-interfered. Swapped to `unavatar.io` everywhere (`app/api/logo/route.ts`, `CompanyLogo.tsx`, `lib/inngest/functions.ts`), backfilled the 17 DB rows still holding a dead Clearbit URL. **User-confirmed live: logos render again.** Lesson written into this file's own gotchas below.
2. **Perplexity Sonar** wired in as a company-research fallback (`agent/research.ts`), fires only when the existing free Jina+Gemini path fails — never a replacement, real cost (~$0.005/call) disclosed each time. Direct API key (user's own Perplexity account), not OpenRouter — both were confirmed live to cost the same either way.
3. **Phase 0 cleanup**, sourced from a Gemini-researched competitive strategy + UI/architecture review (new docs: `context/build-action-plan-2026-07-28.md`, `sortie-strategic-analysis-2026-07-28.md`, `sortie-design-architecture-analysis-2026-07-28.md` — all from `agy`, see gotcha below on which Gemini tool actually works): `/preview/*` gated from production via a new layout, match-score colors redesigned off a literal green/blue/amber traffic light (now respects the existing amber-for-user-actions/teal-for-AI-content rule), two component renames (`AutoResearchCompany` → `CompanyResearchAutoLoader`, `GlassCursorGlow` moved to `ui/`). ComingSoon nav/routes (`/agent`, `/interview`, `/notifications`) deliberately left untouched — the user still wants those as placeholders for now.
4. **Real account deletion shipped (GDPR/CCPA — closes a Phase 0 legal gap that was open for weeks).** InsForge has no documented delete-user API — found the real raw-SQL admin endpoint instead by reading `@insforge/cli`'s own published source (`lib/insforge-admin-sql.ts`, `actions/account.ts`). **Verified live against a real disposable test account**, not just read for correctness: confirmed the full deletion removes every row including cascades, AND confirmed a deliberately-broken statement mid-batch rolled back an already-executed delete too (genuinely atomic).
5. **Settings modal shipped**, replacing the settings page as the primary surface (the page itself stays as a fallback) — researched the UX pattern via `agy` first per the user's explicit ask. **Found and fixed a real, non-obvious SSR/hydration bug along the way**: the modal, mounted in the root layout, served genuinely unhydrated static HTML on a cold load with `?settings=1` already in the URL (confirmed via checking for React fiber attachment on the DOM node — there was none). Fixed via `next/dynamic({ ssr: false })`. Full writeup in `progress-tracker.md` and `components/settings/SettingsModalLoader.tsx`'s own comment — read that comment before ever moving this component or adding another `useSearchParams()`-driven component to the root layout.
6. **Email/password auth shipped** alongside the existing Google/GitHub OAuth — sign up, verify-by-code, sign in, forgot/reset password, all following this project's *existing* OAuth cookie-writing pattern rather than introducing a new one. Real SMTP configured via Resend (user gave a live API key directly in chat — saved straight to `.env`, never echoed back). **Real, disclosed limitation:** without a verified custom domain in Resend, verification emails only deliver to the email the user signed up to Resend with — not to arbitrary real users yet. **Not yet live-tested end to end** — the actual sign-up → receive-code → verify round trip needs the user to try it with their own email; deliberately not fired by the agent since it sends a real email to a real inbox.
7. **LinkedIn profile-import research done, zero code written — safe to resume from scratch.** User clarified they meant importing their *own* profile data (not job scraping, which already exists via SerpApi). Confirmed via `agy`: true "sync" isn't achievable outside LinkedIn's enterprise partner program (their consumer OAuth only ever returns name/email/photo). The actually-viable path is a **LinkedIn Data Archive ZIP importer** — users already have free, official access to export their own data (Settings → Data Privacy → "Get a copy of your data") as clean CSVs (`Positions.csv`, `Education.csv`, `Skills.csv`, etc.), fully legal, no ToS risk. Also confirmed: `components/profile/ConnectedAccounts.tsx` is dead code (not imported anywhere, calls `/api/linkedin/*` routes that don't exist) and its session-hijacking approach directly contradicts this project's own already-documented "no direct LinkedIn scraping" decision (`build-plan.md`, citing the hiQ Labs v. LinkedIn precedent) — recommended for deletion, not yet done. Was mid-way through verifying the exact CSV column format live (don't guess it — a real GitHub-hosted parser skill was found and partially fetched, `references/linkedin-export-format.md` 404'd, still need the exact headers) when the user asked to wind the session down instead.

**Also worth a real look, not assumed fine, carried over from the prior session:** the floating Navbar + non-sticky `JobActionBar` + `FloatingApplyButton` layout, the icon-bullet redesign on Qualification/Benefits/Responsibilities/HiringProcess, and the nav (Jobs dropdown, mobile drawer) — all built and type/lint-clean, still none click-tested end-to-end since this environment can't complete a real OAuth login (same standing limitation, see gotchas below) — though several of THIS session's features (account deletion, settings modal, email/password auth) got real live verification specifically because they didn't require an existing OAuth session to test.

## Previous session summary (2026-07-24 → 2026-07-27) — JD structure, Saved Jobs, original Liquid Glass build

**This session had two very different halves: (1) fixing a real, previously-latent data-pipeline bug plus a genuinely new feature (Saved Jobs), and (2) a large, iterative "Liquid Glass" visual redesign across the whole app that found several real build-tool/CSS bugs along the way.** Read `context/progress-tracker.md`'s top entries for full detail; this is the fast summary. Full detail on part 2 also lives in `context/ui-tokens.md`'s "Liquid Glass" section — **read that before touching `app/globals.css` again**, it documents three confirmed Lightning CSS bugs that will recur if not read first.

**Part 1 — JD structure + Saved Jobs (shipped, stable, not re-touched since):**

1. **A real bug found and fixed at the root**: `jobs.responsibilities`/`requirements`/`nice_to_have`/`benefits` had never been populated by any code path, ever — confirmed via full-codebase grep. `lib/evaluator.ts` now extracts these plus a cleaned `about_role` summary, `salary` (fallback-only, never overwrites a real scraped value), and a new `hiring_process` field, all riding along on the *same* Gemini call that already grades job fit — zero marginal AI cost per evaluation, not a second call.
2. New DB column `jobs.hiring_process text[]` (migration `20260724010000_add-jobs-hiring-process.sql`, applied live then written to match — this project's remote migration history is still untracked, don't run `migrations up --all` blind).
3. New JD layout: `Responsibilities.tsx`, `HiringProcess.tsx`, `Benefits.tsx` (all new, hide-if-empty) + reworked `Qualification.tsx` (merged single-row skill tags, matches a real JobRight screenshot) + simplified `JobDescription.tsx`.
4. **Backfill in progress, not finished**: `scripts/backfill-job-structure.mjs` re-extracts structure for jobs evaluated *before* this session's fix. As of this note: **449/633** evaluated jobs have structure. Free-tier `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` rate limits (confirmed live: 15 req/min) mean this takes real wall-clock time; the script is idempotent (targets `WHERE match_score IS NOT NULL AND about_role IS NULL`) so it's safe to just re-run `node --env-file=.env scripts/backfill-job-structure.mjs 700` if it's stopped.
5. **Saved Jobs page** (`app/saved-jobs/page.tsx`, new) — the "Saved only" toggle already in `FindJobsForm.tsx` only ever filtered the *current search's* results, not the user's full save history; this new page queries `is_saved = true` unscoped by `run_id`. Extracted `components/shared/JobResultCard.tsx` from `FindJobsForm.tsx`'s inline card JSX, now shared by both.

**Part 2 — Liquid Glass redesign (large, iterative, real bugs found — read `ui-tokens.md` before extending):**

User asked for an Apple Liquid Glass-inspired theme across the whole app. Built as a reusable CSS material system (`.glass-panel`/`.glass-panel-strong`/`.glass-panel-overlay`/`.glass-pill`/`.glass-panel-interactive`, all in `app/globals.css`), applied to every card across job-details/profile/dashboard, the navbar, and Find Jobs. Three real, confirmed build-tool bugs found in the process (full writeups in `ui-tokens.md`):

1. Lightning CSS (Tailwind v4's build) silently drops the standard `backdrop-filter` property if it's written *before* `-webkit-backdrop-filter` in the same declaration block — fixed by always writing the prefix first.
2. A `position: relative` declaration on `.glass-panel-strong` silently overrode `JobActionBar`'s `sticky` Tailwind utility (unlayered CSS beats layered utilities, by this file's own established rule) — combined with a `top-16` offset, this visually overlapped the sticky bar onto the page content below it. Confirmed via a real user screenshot showing the title text overlapped. Fixed by removing `position` from all three glass classes entirely — they must never declare position, since call sites need to set their own.
3. `animation`/`background-size` sharing a declaration block with a `color-mix()`-based `background-image` were silently dropped from the compiled CSS entirely (confirmed via `getComputedStyle(body).animationName === "none"` despite correct source) — fixed by isolating them into their own separate rule.

Also found via a real screenshot: the first ambient-backdrop design (4 radial gradient blobs, warm accent + cool agent-teal mixed in overlapping regions) read as a muddy brown/olive haze in dark mode, worsened by a drift animation sliding the blobs into each other's path over time. Redesigned Apple Music-inspired: 2 blobs in opposite corners, much lower alpha in dark mode, and the drift/breathe animations' travel range tightened so the blobs never cross paths.

Check with them first thing next session on whether it actually reads better, rather than assuming it's resolved.

**Original session summary below (2026-07-22 → 2026-07-23) — JobRight-parity job details page + LinkedIn/Apify integration.**

**Shipped, in rough order:**

1. **Résumé-fit analysis** (`agent/resumeGap.ts`, `/api/documents/analyze`) — finished a feature left uncommitted from a dropped prior session (missing rate-limit call added).
2. **Two real multi-tenancy/data bugs found and fixed via live testing:**
   - `profiles.email` never existed as a column despite being read everywhere — silently forced the admin account onto Gemini-only/full rate limits since Phase 0 shipped, with no error. Fixed with a migration + backfill + extending the auto-provision trigger.
   - `jobs.external_id` had a **global** unique constraint, but RLS enforces per-user rows — when two accounts' searches surfaced the same real job posting, the whole batch upsert silently failed. Fixed by scoping the constraint to `(user_id, external_id)`.
3. **Job details page restructured into JobRight's Overview/Company tabs** (`components/ui/Tabs.tsx`, new). Shipped every free-tier gap from the JobRight teardown: sticky action bar with Save/Hide (`jobs.is_saved`/`is_hidden`, new `actions/jobs.ts`), correctable skill tags + Required/Preferred split (`Qualification.tsx`, new `correctSkillTag` action), real posting freshness (`jobs.posted_at`, confirmed live that SerpApi actually provides this for free), and a from-scratch honest rewrite of `NetworkSignals.tsx` — dropped JobRight's fabricated "3 former colleagues" headcount claim entirely since there's no free data source for it; replaced with a real "you worked here before" fact plus a (now-fixed) single-company LinkedIn search link.
4. **`agent/research.ts`'s entire browsing layer replaced.** Browserbase/Stagehand was never actually configured (confirmed live) — swapped for Jina Reader (free, no browser to host) + Gemini extraction for both the main company-research dossier and a new Leadership Team lookup (Wikipedia first, site-guessing second, both free; paid Apify LinkedIn search only as a last resort). `@browserbasehq/*` packages removed, `lib/browserbase.ts` deleted.
5. **A systemic extraction bug found and fixed, bigger than it first looked:** the shared `extractStructured()` helper never told Gemini what JSON *shape* to return — only "return valid JSON." Gemini would return correct data in a differently-shaped object (e.g. a bare array instead of `{"leadershipTeam": [...]}`), schema validation silently failed, and it read as "nothing found" even when the model found real data. This hit **every** extraction call, including the main company-research homepage/subpage steps, not just the one place it was first noticed (Leadership) — likely explains any past "browser research came back empty" cases that weren't actually the fetch failing. Fixed everywhere by making each call site spell out its exact expected JSON shape in the prompt.
6. **Full "Insider Connections" + email-reveal feature**, matching a real JobRight screenshot you shared: three real-people buckets (Beyond Your Network / From Your Previous Company / From Your School) via a paid Apify integration (`harvestapi` actors — same vendor as Leadership's fallback), plus per-person email reveal. **Real cost, confirmed live, not estimated:** ~$0.31-0.32 per full connections lookup (3 search calls + company-URL resolves), ~$0.10 per email reveal (reworked mid-build after the originally-planned $0.01/profile tool turned out to be blocked on Apify's free plan for API use — confirmed live via a real 403). The standalone "paste any LinkedIn URL" email box from the JobRight screenshot was **dropped** — the fallback tool is filter-based (search by name+company), not URL-based, so it genuinely can't do that lookup; only reveals email for someone already found via the connections search. Both features are opt-in (button-triggered), gated by new `insider_connections` (3/day) and `email_lookup` (4/day) usage caps, and use a `*LookedUp` boolean pattern (same fix class as item 7 below) so repeat clicks return cached results instead of re-spending.
7. **A UX/cost-safety bug found and fixed on Leadership, then built correctly from the start on Insider Connections:** a successful search that found nothing looked identical to "never searched" (both showed empty state + the button), so users kept re-clicking — and *each* click re-ran the entire paid waterfall including the Apify fallback. Fixed with a `leadershipLookedUp`/`insiderConnectionsLookedUp` flag that distinguishes the two states and short-circuits repeat requests server-side.
8. **Leadership's own latency bug:** 7 guessed page paths were tried sequentially (up to 25s timeout each) — worst case minutes, read as "not working." Fixed to run concurrently (~4s in the real test).
9. **A real, general (not Leadership-specific) bug:** `cleanCompanyName()`'s legal-suffix-stripping regex was unanchored and matched substrings mid-word — "Scotiabank" (contains "co") got truncated to "S", resolving to `www.s.com`. Affects any company name containing "co"/"inc"/"ltd" as a substring (Coinbase, Discovery, Cisco, Costco...), not just Scotiabank. Fixed with a word-boundary-respecting regex.

**Real Apify spend this session:** roughly $0.55-0.65 total across verification calls (all disclosed inline as they happened, several deliberately reused already-known data instead of fresh speculative lookups to minimize cost per your explicit request).

**Not yet done:** the actual Phase 0 launch gate (Privacy Policy/Terms, account deletion, two-account isolation test in-browser, real Vercel deploy) — still 3 items, untouched this session, unchanged from before. A future paid-subscription tier is the stated plan for gating Insider Connections/email lookup once billing exists — not built yet.
