## Known environment gotchas

- **Recurred in Phase 9 (2026-08-12):** the stale/corrupted Turbopack module-graph issue first documented in Phase 7 (see the entry a few lines below) happened again, this time on `/missions` after the Pipeline→Missions rename — a live browser hit threw `ReferenceError: statusMenuRef is not defined`, a name that was renamed away earlier in the *same* session and doesn't exist anywhere in source (confirmed via full-codebase grep, `tsc`/`eslint` both clean). Given Phase 8's Next.js 16 change (below) means a second independent dev-server instance can no longer be spun up to cross-check, the only confirmed fix now is a real restart of the existing long-running process — not a `.next` clear alone, which the original Phase 7 entry already noted was "only partially" sufficient. **If a live check contradicts what the source code clearly says, and grep confirms the error references something that doesn't exist in the codebase, don't debug the code — ask for a dev-server restart first.**
- **New in Phase 8:** Next.js 16's dev server now refuses to start a second genuinely-independent instance while another one is already running against the same project directory — a fresh `next dev -p <other-port>` prints "✓ Ready" and then immediately "⨯ Another next dev server is already running" (naming the first instance's PID) and never actually binds/serves (confirmed via `curl` returning connection-refused on the new port). This is a change from earlier sessions' documented workaround ("spin up a second, genuinely separate dev server process to cross-check a stale-cache-suspected long-running one") — that workaround **no longer works** if another `next dev` is already up for this project. When you need to verify against a live server and a long-running one already exists (check first with `netstat`/`preview_list`), hit that existing process directly instead (`curl -i <url>` for status code + redirect target, and check its own `.next/dev/logs/next-development.log` for errors at current timestamps) rather than trying to start a second one.

- **New in Phase 7 (résumé editor build):** this project's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule fired **three separate times** across this session's `useEffect`s that auto-trigger something on mount or react to a prop change (score auto-check on workspace mount, the Editor tab's section auto-open on focus, the highlight-a-bullet scroll effect) — every time, the fix was the same: defer the actual `setState` call(s) inside `setTimeout(..., 0)` rather than calling them synchronously in the effect body. If you write a new `useEffect` in this codebase that calls `setState` directly (not inside a callback/promise/timeout), expect this lint rule to catch it — defer it up front rather than discovering it at lint time. When a deferred effect ALSO needs a separate longer-lived timer (e.g. a fade-out), track that second timer on its own `ref`, not the same one — a cleanup tied to the outer effect's own dependency churn will cancel a nested timer early otherwise (found live building the Action Plan's bullet-highlight fade).
- **New in Phase 7:** a `useCallback` whose body reads state (e.g. `sections`) but whose dependency array omits it will silently operate on stale data the moment it's invoked from inside another async flow (e.g. right after `setSections(...)` in the same tick — React state updates aren't synchronous, so the closure still has the old value). Found twice this session (`handleAnalyze`/`handleAnalyzeQuality` in `ResumeWorkspace.tsx`) — the fix both times was either including the real dependency in the array, or passing the fresh value in explicitly as a function argument rather than relying on the closure at all.
- Running `npm run dev` alone is not enough for Find Jobs search to actually score anything — needs a separate `npx inngest-cli@latest dev` process. Without it, jobs get scraped/saved but never evaluated, no error, just permanent `match_score: null`.
- **No way to complete a real Google OAuth login inside the automated browser tool** — every feature across sessions has been verified as deep as possible without one (direct DB round-trips, standalone scripts hitting real endpoints with real data, `/preview` mockup rendering checks) but the actual authenticated click-through is still something you need to do yourself, and is the main way real UI bugs (the JobActionBar overlap, the muddy dark-mode backdrop) actually got caught this session — via your screenshots, not automated checks.
- Two Apify actors have their own gates independent of your account balance — `dev_fusion/Linkedin-Profile-Scraper` refuses free-plan API calls entirely (Console-only), and some actors need a one-time manual permission approval in the Apify console before their first API call works (`https://console.apify.com/actors/<id>?approvePermissions=true` — the error response gives you the exact link when this is the blocker).
- **New this session:** the free-tier `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` key (used both by `gemini-mcp-tool` and this app's own `lib/evaluator.ts`) is rate-limited to 15 requests/minute — confirmed live from the actual 429 response. Any bulk/backfill script against this key needs real pacing (4.5s+ between calls) and generous backoff on 429/503, or it fails partway through.
- **New this session:** Lightning CSS (Tailwind v4's build tool) has silently dropped valid CSS properties three separate times when they shared a declaration block with a `color-mix()`-based value — see `ui-tokens.md`'s Liquid Glass section for the exact patterns and fixes. Don't assume a CSS change "didn't work" is your logic being wrong before checking the actual compiled output.
- **New this session:** `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`'s free tier has BOTH an RPM (15/min) and a separate **daily** RPD cap (500/day) — confirmed live against the real Google AI Studio usage dashboard. Retrying/backing off only helps with the RPM case; once RPD is exhausted there is no quota left until the next day no matter how long you wait or retry. `lib/models.ts` now has retry+backoff (`withRateLimitRetry`) AND a model fallback chain (`GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODELS`) AND a process-lifetime cooldown cache so a multi-call request (company research) doesn't re-pay the "primary is exhausted" discovery cost on every call — but if research/evaluation is ever slow or failing again, check that dashboard first before assuming it's a code regression.
- **New this session:** don't guess Gemini model ID strings from Google AI Studio's dashboard display names — several read as having real quota there but are actually `404 "no longer available to new users"` (deprecated for this account) when actually called. Verify live against the exact OpenAI-compat endpoint `lib/models.ts` calls (a real `chat.completions.create` test call), not just `models.list`, before adding a model anywhere.
- **New this session:** this sandboxed environment could not get outbound network access working reliably via either Bash (`curl` — no DNS resolution) or the browser tool (`navigate`/`fetch` to arbitrary external hosts kept failing) partway through the session — several live-verification attempts (Clearbit's actual behavior, Google favicon CORS headers) had to fall back to reasoning from user-provided screenshots instead of direct confirmation. If this recurs, don't assume an external service is broken just because it couldn't be reached from here — the CLI (`npx @insforge/cli`) and the running dev server's own Node process (`node --env-file=.env <script>`) still had working network access throughout, useful as an alternative verification path.
- **RESOLVED this session:** the Clearbit logo proxy above turned out to be fixing the wrong problem — `logo.clearbit.com` is fully DNS-dead (confirmed via `curl`/`nslookup`, not just theorized), not ad-blocker-interfered. Fixed for real via `unavatar.io`. Full write-up: item 1 in this session's 30-second state above.
- **New this session:** `gemini-mcp-tool` is a dead end — its underlying `gemini` CLI hit a `TerminalQuotaError` (20/day free-tier cap), and investigating further found Google discontinued personal-account OAuth login for the standalone `gemini` CLI entirely (`IneligibleTierError`, points to migrating to "Antigravity"). Use Antigravity's own CLI instead — already installed on this machine at `C:\Users\ridha\AppData\Local\agy\bin\agy.exe`, genuinely authenticated to the real Google account (confirmed via its own logs showing `authMethod=consumer`). Invoke as `"/c/Users/ridha/AppData/Local/agy/bin/agy.exe" -p "<prompt>" --print-timeout <seconds>`. Real constraints found the hard way: no stdin support (the prompt must be a single CLI argument, so very long prompts hit the shell's argument-length limit — condense rather than pipe), and it needs shell/file-read tool permission to explore a repo itself, which headless mode can't grant interactively (either paste context inline in the prompt, or figure out the right `permissions.allow` syntax in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — guessing the exact syntax didn't work this session, gave up and went with inline-context instead). The user explicitly wants this tool used for research/deep-research requests before spending the assistant's own reasoning tokens — see the `feedback_research_tool_priority` cross-session memory.
- **New this session:** this dev environment's Fast Refresh/HMR can leave a browser tab's console log and even live component behavior in a stale, misleading state after a file edit — happened at least twice (a `GlassCursorGlow` "module not found" error that was actually already fixed on disk, and a real settings-modal bug investigation that was initially confused by stale Fast Refresh state). **When debugging anything that seems inconsistent with the actual code on disk, verify in a genuinely fresh browser tab (`tabs_create` + navigate) before trusting what an already-open tab shows** — this resolved the confusion both times.
- **New this session (Phase 7, résumé editor build), a more severe version of the gotcha above:** a long-running dev server process (one you'd started yourself before this session, on port 3001, outside my own `preview_start`) started returning genuine server-side 500s citing a "name X defined multiple times" compile error at OLD, pre-edit line numbers — not just a stale client console, an actually-corrupted Turbopack module-graph cache on that process. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` were clean the whole time (they read fresh from disk every run), and it persisted across multiple full page reloads AND a fresh browser tab — a fresh *tab* wasn't enough this time, unlike the client-HMR case above. `rm -rf .next` only partially helped. What actually confirmed the code was fine: spinning up a **second, genuinely separate dev server process** via `preview_start` (own `.next`, own module graph) and hitting the exact same routes there — zero errors, correct behavior. **If a long-running dev server starts throwing compile errors that don't match the current file content, don't trust it — start a fresh process (or ask the user to restart theirs) before concluding the code is actually broken.** A `.next` cache clear alone is not guaranteed sufficient; the running process may need an actual restart.
- **New this session:** InsForge's documented REST API (OpenAPI spec, `npx @insforge/cli metadata`, the packaged skill docs) doesn't cover everything the platform can actually do — there is no documented endpoint to delete a user account, but the CLI's own npm package source (downloadable via `npm view @insforge/cli` → tarball URL, or `gh api search/code` against `InsForge/InsForge` on GitHub) reveals real, working admin endpoints like `POST /api/database/advance/rawsql` / `/rawsql/unrestricted` (raw SQL, same admin `INSFORGE_API_KEY` this project already uses) and `PUT /api/auth/smtp-config` (the real way to set SMTP credentials including password — the `config apply`/`insforge.toml` flow has no password field at all). When the packaged docs don't have something, check the CLI's own source before assuming it's not possible.
- **New this session:** the raw-SQL admin endpoint (`/api/database/advance/rawsql/unrestricted`) rejects explicit `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` statements ("Transaction control statements are not allowed") — but a plain multi-statement semicolon-separated string sent in one call IS atomic anyway, because Postgres's simple-query protocol wraps multiple statements with no explicit transaction control in an implicit transaction on its own. Confirmed live both ways: the happy path deletes everything, and a deliberately-broken statement appended after a valid one rolled the valid one back too.
- **New this session:** a `useSearchParams()`-driven Client Component mounted in the root layout (`app/layout.tsx`), even behind a `<Suspense>` boundary, can end up served as genuinely unhydrated static HTML on a cold load when the query param is already present in the URL — no console error, just a component that never runs its effects (checked by looking for React fiber keys directly on the DOM node: `Object.keys(el).some(k => k.startsWith('__react'))`). The root layout also wraps fully static pages (the marketing homepage), which is suspected to be why. Fix: `next/dynamic(..., { ssr: false })` via a small Client Component wrapper (`next/dynamic` with `ssr:false` can't be called directly inside a Server Component). See `components/settings/SettingsModalLoader.tsx`'s comment for the full story — read it before mounting anything else `useSearchParams()`-driven at the root-layout level.
- **New this session:** `revalidatePath(path)` only invalidates the *exact* path given — it does NOT invalidate a dynamic subroute. A server action called from `/resume/[id]` that only did `revalidatePath("/resume")` left that détail page serving a stale cached render indefinitely, even though the underlying data had genuinely changed (confirmed via direct `db query`, not assumption). Any action reachable from a dynamic route needs to revalidate that route's *actual* rendered path too (e.g. `` `/resume/${id}` ``), not just its parent list page. Also add `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` to any authenticated, frequently-mutated page — `app/profile/page.tsx` already had this, the newer résumé pages didn't and it was a contributing factor.
- **New this session:** Gemini's own JSON extraction isn't perfectly consistent on casing/formatting run-to-run — the exact same underlying PDF produced `company: "Wizcraft"` on one extraction and `company: "wizcraft"` on a later one. Any dedup/merge key built from AI-extracted string fields needs to be case- and whitespace-normalized (`.trim().toLowerCase()`) before comparing — a case-sensitive key silently treated these as two different companies and created a duplicate profile entry instead of recognizing an existing one.
- **New in Phase 7 (2026-08-05):** matching two AI-extracted entries as "the same real-world thing" by comparing free-text fields Gemini paraphrases (company display name, job title wording, institution name) is unreliable even after case/whitespace normalization — confirmed live with real data: the same job came back as `"HCL Technologies" / "Full-stack Developer"` from one résumé and `"HCL America Inc" / "Developer"` from another. Prefer a field extraction has actually been consistent on (here, `start_date`) as the match key, and only use free-text fields for *display*, not identity. Education's institution+degree matching hasn't shown this failure mode yet but is built the same brittle way — worth re-checking if a similar duplicate shows up there.
- **RESOLVED in Phase 7 (later 2026-08-05 session):** an *additive-only* merge (never overwrite existing data, only add what's missing) sounds safe but has a real failure mode — once an entry exists at all, later syncs with much better data silently skip it forever, and there's no visible sign anything is wrong. This was `syncResumeToProfile`'s behavior and was the reason old, low-quality profile data survived several résumé syncs undetected. Fixed via explicit per-entry "update this?" checkboxes for Education/Work Experience (default unchecked) rather than a silent auto-replace heuristic — see the Phase 7 entry in the 30-second state above and `progress-tracker.md`'s top entry for the full design.
- **New this session:** the InsForge CLI's raw-SQL endpoint (`db query`) rejected a legitimate multi-line JSONB `UPDATE` using Postgres `$$...$$` dollar-quoting as "could not be parsed and was rejected for security reasons" — not every valid SQL pattern gets through its security filter. When that happens, drop to the `@insforge/sdk`'s `createAdminClient({ baseUrl, apiKey })` (same `NEXT_PUBLIC_INSFORGE_URL`/`INSFORGE_API_KEY` env vars) and use its normal `.database.from(table).update(...)` REST path instead — it isn't subject to the same raw-SQL filter. Must be run as an ESM script (`.mjs`, `import` not `require`) from inside the project directory (`node --env-file=.env script.mjs`) — `@insforge/sdk` has package-exports that break plain CommonJS `require()` resolution, and `NODE_PATH` tricks for a script sitting outside the project don't help ESM's resolver the way they help CJS.
- **New this session, useful for testing:** `lib/usage.ts`'s `checkAndConsumeUsage` fully bypasses the daily cap (and never writes a `usage_daily` row at all) for any email on the `ADMIN_EMAILS` env var allowlist — confirmed live via `usage_daily` query returning zero rows despite real successful calls. If a usage-capped feature seems to "just keep working" during testing with the project's own dev account, that's why — it says nothing about whether the cap logic itself is correct for a real non-admin user.

## Where the real detail lives

- `context/build-plan.md` — full v1 (17 features) + v2 (career-ops port + custom, Phases 6-17) roadmap, feature-by-feature spec, and the Master Feature Inventory (§A-L) tracking every feature ever discussed against JobRight. Its DB schema/stack description is now confirmed stale (see gotcha above) — don't trust that part.
- `context/build-action-plan-2026-07-28.md`, `context/sortie-strategic-analysis-2026-07-28.md`, `context/sortie-design-architecture-analysis-2026-07-28.md` — **new this session**, Gemini-researched competitive strategy ("Sniper vs Machine Gunner" positioning) and UI/architecture review that Phase 0 and the settings-modal redesign came out of. Read these before proposing new features or big UI changes — the positioning/USP framing here should inform prioritization.
- `context/progress-tracker.md` — live status, decisions made during the build, notable gotchas per feature. **The top entry is this session's full detail** (résumé editor workspace — the big one).
- `~/.claude/plans/jaunty-jingling-bear.md` — **new this session**, the full approved plan for the Résumé Editor Workspace (data model decision, work-package breakdown, file list). Read this before extending the editor further — it explains *why* things are shaped the way they are (e.g. why `resume_sections` exists at all, why Split is a real two-column layout not a style variant).
- `context/jobright-resume-scan-2026-07-30.md` — a live Chrome-extension scan of JobRight (onboarding through résumé analysis/tailoring/cover-letter generation, not just pasted screenshots) — the grounding reference behind the résumé-analysis feature. Read this before extending that feature further.
- `context/ui-tokens.md` — the design system, including the Liquid Glass section (material recipe, three confirmed Lightning CSS bugs) and the Match Score Colors redesign (why AI-generated scores use `--color-agent`, not a generic traffic light).
- `context/ui-registry.md` — component-level class tables. New/updated entries this session: `ResumeWorkspace`, `EditorTab`/`StyleTab`, `ResumePDF` (generalized), `ResumeManager`, `ResumeAnalysisView`, `SectionModal`, `Tabs` (width fix).
- `context/library-docs.md` — has a full Perplexity Sonar section (pattern, cost, rules, the direct-key-vs-OpenRouter finding).
- Persistent cross-session memory (separate from this repo, in `~/.claude/projects/.../memory/`) — feedback memories on always verifying fixes live before claiming they work, paid-API cost discipline, using `agy`/Perplexity for research requests before the assistant's own tokens, and (**new this session**) driving a live Chrome-extension scan of a competitor rather than relying on pasted screenshots alone when asked to "scan" something.
